Original word and the claim\n\nFix an integer m>=4. Set\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m,\n    c_chr=sigma(1+1/m), a_chr=delta/c_chr.\n\nUse the actual chronological words\n\n    H_chr,0=(),\n    H_chr,j+1=(H_chr,j,1+a_chr*c_chr^j,H_chr,j), 0<=j<m.\n\nThus h=H_chr,m has exactly n steps and\n\n    h_i=1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n,\n\nwhere nu_2(v) is the exponent of2 dividing the positive integer v. Every\nsubword retains the displayed enclosing-m parameters. There is no reset,\npadding, or permutation of physical steps. In particular its last physical\nstep is h_(n-1)=1+a_chr in(1,2).\n\nSection2 specifies strictly positive probabilities w_s on s in{0,1}^m.\nWrite chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_l s_l i_l), using the ordinary binary digits i_l\nof the original sample index. Prescribe ONLY the queried gradients by\n\n    g_i=(Gamma_i*sqrt(w_s)*chi_s(i))_s, 0<=i<n,             (1)\n\nwhere every Gamma_i is arbitrary and positive. All Euclidean realizations\nof the same Gram matrix are included. The amplitudes may depend on the\nwhole word and the enclosing horizon. There is no adjacent saturation\nassumption and no common amplitude profile in repeated subwords.\n\nSuppose ONE finite-dimensional convex differentiable1-smooth function F\nwith a minimizer realizes these gradients along\n\n    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i*g_i, 0<=i<n.\n\nThe anchor, the values, the function, and the terminal gradient are otherwise\nunrestricted. Let X_star be a closest minimizer, and define\n\n    R=||X_0-X_star||, F_star=min F,\n    V_C89=2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2.\n\nThe claim is\n\n    R>0,\n    V_C89 <= beta_(N/4-1) <= 128/N^2,                    (2)\n\nwhere the accepted notation is\n\n    c_0=0, c_(k+1)=c_k+2+2sqrt(c_k+1),\n    beta_k=4/(1+sqrt(1+2c_k))^2.                         (3)\n\nConsequently, for the ORIGINAL exact benchmark B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1,\n\n    0 <= B_n V_C89\n       <= 128(1+4n^p)/(n+1)^2 ->0,\n    p=log_2(3)<2.                                      (4)\n\nThe bound B_n<=1+4n^p is from `b98bb807d514e80d`. This is an obstruction\nto the specified finite probability law, uniformly over all its legal\npositive amplitude histories. It is not an upper bound on unrestricted\nW_n(H_chr,m). Section6 proves the class nonempty by giving all original\njets of one explicit function. Section7 gives the non-dyadic and arbitrary\nschedule version of the obstruction, with its last-step factor explicit.\n\nFor L-smooth objectives, apply the proof to F=f/L and g_i=grad f(X_i)/L.\nThen (2) has exactly the denominator L*dist(X_0,argmin f)^2.\n\n## 2. Every probability atom, its correlations, and the removed hypotheses\n\nPut p_bias=1-1/N. For s in{0,1}^m let\n\n    B_sig=(-1)^s_0, C_sig=(-1)^s_1, D_sig=(-1)^s_2,\n    mu_+=1/2, mu_-=1/4.\n\nThe exact joint law is as follows. B_sig has mean p_bias; conditional on\nB_sig, C_sig is unbiased; conditional on B_sig=b and C_sig=c, the sign\nD_sig has mean mu_b*c. All bits s_3,...,s_(m-1) are independent unbiased\nbits, independent of the first three signs. Explicitly,\n\n    w_s=2^(-(m-3)) * (1+p_bias*B_sig)/2\n                         * (1/2) * (1+mu_(B_sig)*C_sig*D_sig)/2.       (5)\n\nAll factors are strictly positive. Summing in the order D_sig,C_sig,B_sig,\nthen in the higher bits, proves sum_s w_s=1. This is a real probability\nlaw; no assertion of real positivity is inferred from finite-field algebra.\n\nIts only nonzero nonconstant character moments are\n\n    E B_sig=p_bias,\n    E(C_sig D_sig)=q_mix=(3+p_bias)/8,\n    E(B_sig C_sig D_sig)=r_mix=(1+3p_bias)/8.               (6)\n\nIndeed conditional averaging first gives E(D_sig|B_sig,C_sig)=mu_(B_sig)\nC_sig and E(C_sig|B_sig)=0. Also mu_b=(3+b)/8. These formulas give (6),\nwhile E C_sig=E D_sig=E(B_sig C_sig)=E(B_sig D_sig)=0. A nonempty product\nof higher signs has expectation0, independently of the first three signs.\nEquivalently the density is exactly\n\n    w_s=N^(-1)[1+p_bias B_sig+q_mix C_sig D_sig\n                                      +r_mix B_sig C_sig D_sig].      (7)\n\nLet XOR mean bitwise addition modulo2 of label vectors, and let\n\n    H_corr={0,1,6,7}=span_(F_2){1,6}.                     (8)\n\nHere 1 is binary001, 6 is binary110, and any higher bits are zero.\nThe real normalized correlation kernel is\n\n    k(0)=1, k(1)=p_bias, k(6)=q_mix, k(7)=r_mix,\n    k(d)=0 for d outside H_corr,\n    <g_i,g_j>=Gamma_i Gamma_j k(i XOR j).                 (9)\n\nThis follows by multiplying the two real characters, and using (6).\nIn particular every queried Gram entry is nonnegative. The matrix is\npositive semidefinite because (1) is an actual real vector realization.\nIt is positive definite at distinct labels: for any nonzero coefficients\na_i, the squared norm of sum_i a_i g_i equals\nsum_s w_s(sum_i a_i Gamma_i chi_s(i))^2>0. For the strict inequality,\ncharacter orthogonality gives independence of the distinct character rows.\nThat orthogonality follows by pairing s with s+e_l at a bit where two\nlabels differ, so their signed sum is zero.\n\nThe law is nonproduct even after an invertible linear change of sign\ncoordinates. To see this, its nonzero Fourier support is the rank2 subgroup\nH_corr. A product law in some linear sign coordinates with this support\nwould have exactly two nonzero coordinate means, and one of the three\nnonconstant moments on H_corr would be the product of the other two.\nNone is:\n\n    r_mix-p_bias*q_mix=(1-p_bias^2)/8>0,\n    q_mix-p_bias*r_mix=3(1-p_bias^2)/8>0,\n    p_bias-q_mix*r_mix>0.                               (10)\n\nThe last inequality uses p_bias>=15/16 and q_mix,r_mix<1/2. A product law\nwith more nonzero means would have larger Fourier support, and a product\nlaw with fewer would have smaller support. Full support of the probability\nalso rules out a deterministic coordinate introduced by a change of basis.\n\nThe differences from the earlier laws are visible in the ORIGINAL pairs.\nEvery leaf has correlation p_bias approaching1, instead of the old fixed\nroot correlation -1/2. At the first level2 bridge, samples2,3,4 have\n\n    corr(g_2,g_3)=p_bias,\n    corr(g_2,g_4)=q_mix,\n    corr(g_3,g_4)=r_mix != p_bias*q_mix.                  (11)\n\nThus the product triangle identity with the third correlation equal to\nthe product of the first two cannot be imported. At a level-k bridge\nwith k>=3, the three samples 2^k-2,2^k-1,2^k instead have correlations\np_bias,0,0: the two label differences crossing the bridge contain a\nnonzero high bit. The old near-unit outer correlation is absent.\n\nThe common-prefix return mechanism is also absent. For m>=4,\ncorr(g_0,g_4)=k(4)=0, whereas the old common-prefix component repeats its\nfirst coarse direction after four samples and its full-law correlation\nthere is close to1. In (5), higher prefix characters are not forced to\nshare a common sign. These are changes of actual queried Gram entries,\nnot scalar amplitude reweighting of the old fixed law.\n\nRemoving these hypotheses does not establish interpolation. The exact\nobstruction below uses a different property: the many orthogonal selections\nleft by sparse, nonnegative correlations.\n\n## 3. Original queried selections with nonnegative past correlations\n\nHere is the general calculation used in this joint proof. Work on one\nactual trajectory of a convex differentiable1-smooth F, translated so that\na true closest minimizer is0 and F_star=0. A closest minimizer exists:\nargmin F is closed and nonempty, and a bounded sequence approaching its\ndistance from X_0 has a convergent subsequence in finite dimension.\n\nChoose original queried indices\n\n    j_0<...<j_(M-1),\n\nwhose gradients v_r=g_(j_r) are nonzero and pairwise orthogonal. Assume\nthat each selected gradient has nonnegative scalar product with every\nearlier queried gradient:\n\n    <v_r,g_t> >=0 whenever t<j_r.                       (12)\n\nLet f_r=F(X_(j_r)), q_r=||v_r||^2>0, and D_0=X_0-X_star. These f_r and\nq_r are local names for the selected values and energies in this section.\nNo physical query or step has been deleted or compressed.\n\nAccepted `1252b8823bb5a290` gives, on every actual ordered pair,\n\n    Q_ij=F(X_i)-F(X_j)+<g_i,X_j-X_i>\n                                      +||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.          (13)\n\nFor successive selected indices, the inequality with the LATER gradient\nand the earlier point gives\n\n    f_r >= f_(r+1) + (q_r+q_(r+1))/2\n                + sum_(t=j_r)^(j_(r+1)-1) h_t <v_(r+1),g_t>\n        >= f_(r+1)+(q_r+q_(r+1))/2.                    (14)\n\nAll terms in the displayed original displacement are present. Their\nnonnegativity, not a fictitious compressed GD identity, justifies the\nsecond line. Orthogonality is used only for the squared gradient difference.\n\nThe original optimum comparison similarly gives\n\n    <D_0,v_r> = <X_(j_r)-X_star,v_r>\n                       +sum_(t<j_r)h_t <g_t,v_r>\n              >= f_r+q_r/2.                            (15)\n\nAlso f_(M-1)>=q_(M-1)/2. Put b=f_(M-1)-q_(M-1)/2>=0 and\n\n    T_r=b+sum_(s=r)^(M-1)q_s.\n\nSumming (14) and using (15) gives <D_0,v_r> >= T_r>0 for every r.\nExpansion of the nonnegative squared norm\n||D_0-sum_r(<D_0,v_r>/q_r)v_r||^2 now gives\n\n    R^2=||D_0||^2 >= sum_(r=0)^(M-1) T_r^2/q_r.         (16)\n\nThis is the TRUE initial-distance norm of the given legal function.\nNo inverse Gram anchor or distance to an arbitrary minimizer replaces it.\n\nFor completeness the exact scalar elimination from the accepted\northogonal-moment proof is reproduced. Inductively,\n\n    sum_(r=0)^(k-1) T_r^2/q_r >= c_k T_k,\n    0<=k<=M-1.                                        (17)\n\nThe initial case is0>=0. Since T_k=q_k+T_(k+1), adding the next term to\nthe inductive bound gives\n\n    c_k T_k+T_k^2/q_k\n       =(c_k+1)q_k+(c_k+2)T_(k+1)+T_(k+1)^2/q_k\n       >=[c_k+2+2sqrt(c_k+1)]T_(k+1).\n\nThe last inequality is the nonnegativity of\n(sqrt(c_k+1)*sqrt(q_k)-T_(k+1)/sqrt(q_k))^2. This proves (17).\nLet c=c_(M-1), z=T_(M-1)/q_(M-1)>=1, and\nr_cap=(1+sqrt(1+2c))/2. Equations (16)-(17) imply\n\n    R^2 >= q_(M-1) z(z+c),\n    2f_(M-1)=q_(M-1)(2z-1).\n\nBecause c=2r_cap(r_cap-1),\n\n    z(z+c)-r_cap^2(2z-1)=(z-r_cap)^2>=0.\n\nTherefore\n\n    2F(X_(j_(M-1)))/R^2 <= beta_(M-1).                 (18)\n\nThe formula includes M=1, where beta_0=1. If a selected gradient were\nzero, its point would be a minimizer, every subsequent iterate would\nremain there, and the terminal objective would be0 instead.\n\nFor k>=1, induction in (3) gives c_k>=k^2. Since\nc_k=2r_cap(r_cap-1)<=2r_cap^2, this also proves\n\n    beta_k <= 2/k^2.                                   (19)\n\nThe new content relative to the accepted all-gradients-orthogonal fact is\nprecisely (14)-(15): nonorthogonal gradients at the skipped original samples\nare allowed, with their full chronological contributions retained.\n\n## 4. Apply the calculation to the law and the actual free terminal\n\nThere are N/4 cosets of H_corr among all N binary labels, each of size4.\nDeleting the one terminal label N-1 leaves every coset represented by\nqueried labels. Choose the largest queried index in each coset, and sort\nthese choices in increasing chronological order. Thus M=N/4 and the last\nselected index is n-1=N-2. More explicitly, in each complete group of eight\nlabels the representatives are8r+5 and8r+7; in the last group use N-3 and\nN-2, because N-1 is unqueried.\n\nDifferent selected labels lie in different cosets, so their differences\nare outside H_corr and their gradients are orthogonal by (9). Every\nscalar product with an earlier gradient is nonnegative by that same\nformula. Hence (18) gives\n\n    2(F(X_(n-1))-F_star)/R^2 <= beta_(N/4-1).             (20)\n\nFor a1-smooth function, integration along a segment gives\n\n    F(X-h grad F(X)) <= F(X)-h(1-h/2)||grad F(X)||^2.\n\nThe last ORIGINAL step is1+a_chr<2, so F(X_n)<=F(X_(n-1)). This statement\nuses the actual function; it makes no restriction on grad F(X_n).\nCombining it with (20) proves the first inequality in (2). For N>=16,\nN/4-1>=N/8, and (19) gives beta_(N/4-1)<=128/N^2. This proves (2)-(4).\nSince ||g_0||=Gamma_0>0, X_0 is not a minimizer and R>0.\n\nThe same estimate controls maximal queried-prefix interpolation, without\nadding an incoming terminal constraint. For any legal complete queried\nprefix and an optimum jet, `1252b8823bb5a290` constructs one maximal\n1-smooth interpolant and bounds every reduced-feasible terminal value by\nits actual value at the fixed X_n. The preceding argument applies to that\nfunction and its own closest-minimizer distance. If the original supplied\nanchor has norm at most1 relative to its optimum jet, that true distance\nis at most1, so twice the reduced-feasible terminal value is also at most\nbeta_(N/4-1). Free terminal selection cannot repair this law.\n\n## 5. The smallest cross-cycle and why PSD alone is not being used\n\nFor three consecutive original samples, temporarily call them0,1,2,\nwith steps1+t,1+u and normalized correlations P,Z,W in the order01,02,12.\nLet A=||g_0||, x=||g_1||/A, y=||g_2||/A. Expansion of the actual directed\ncycle in (13), with values and the anchor cancelling, gives\n\n    (Q_02+Q_10+Q_21)/A^2\n       =-t+x^2+y^2+(t-u-1)P*x+u*W*x*y-Z*y.             (21)\n\nIndeed before division the diagonal terms are -t K_00+K_11+K_22,\nand the cross terms are (t-u-1)K_01+u K_12-K_02.\n\nAt samples0,1,2 of the actual word, P=p_bias and Z=W=0. At the first\nlevel2 cross-cycle, samples2,3,4, formula (11) gives\n\n    t=a_chr, u=delta*c_chr,\n    P=p_bias, Z=q_mix, W=r_mix.                         (22)\n\nThus the mixed term in this actual cycle includes r_mix, not the product\np_bias*q_mix. The two moments E B_sig and E(C_sig D_sig) alone do not\ndetermine this third-order term in a necessary original cycle. We do not\nclaim that any local root rule is sufficient.\n\nSection6 now gives explicit amplitudes and values for which EVERY ordered\npair in both these triples is strictly negative, as are all other distinct\nfinite queried pairs. In particular (21) and its reverse are strictly\nnegative on that one specified complete construction. This resolves actual\ncross-cycles and full interpolation together; it does not infer either\nfrom the real PSD property alone.\n\n## 6. Nonvacuity: an explicit common anchor and one global function\n\nThis part only establishes nonemptiness; it is not a competitive payoff\nclaim. In fact it works for every finite schedule with h_i>1 and the same\nkind of nonnegative kernel. For the dyadic word above retain its exact\nweights and additionally prescribe a character gradient at the terminal.\nWrite\n\n    H_max=max_(i<n) h_i,\n    kappa=min(1,(min_(i<n)h_i-1)/4)>0,\n    eta=kappa/[8(H_max+kappa+1)],\n    Gamma_i=eta^i, f_i=(kappa+1/2)Gamma_i^2, 0<=i<=n,\n    K_ij=<g_i,g_j>.\n\nHere eta<1/8 and h_i>=1+4kappa. Define\n\n    b_i=(kappa+1)Gamma_i^2+sum_(t<i)h_t K_ti,\n    (X_0)_s=[1/(N sqrt(w_s))]\n                              sum_(i=0)^n (b_i/Gamma_i)chi_s(i),\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.                           (23)\n\nCharacter orthogonality proves <X_0,g_i>=b_i, hence\n\n    <X_i,g_i>=(kappa+1)Gamma_i^2.                       (24)\n\nFor i<j, direct expansion of the ORIGINAL residuals gives\n\n    Q_ij=(kappa+1-h_i)Gamma_i^2-kappa Gamma_j^2\n                          -sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_it-K_ij,\n\n    Q_ji=(kappa+1)Gamma_j^2-kappa Gamma_i^2\n                          +(h_i-1)K_ij+sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_jt.          (25)\n\nBy (9), 0<=K_it<=Gamma_i Gamma_t. The first line of (25) is at most\n-3kappa Gamma_i^2. The second, divided by Gamma_i^2, is at most\n\n    -kappa+(kappa+1)eta^2+H_max*eta/(1-eta)\n       <=-kappa+(2H_max+kappa+1)eta\n       <-3kappa/4.                                    (26)\n\nThe second line uses eta<1/2; the final inequality follows on inserting\nthe exact eta and using2H_max+kappa+1<2(H_max+kappa+1).\nThese inequalities include the first actual cross-cycle and both\norientations of every remote queried or terminal pair. With the optimum\njet (0,0,0), (24) gives exactly\n\n    Q_(i,star)=0, Q_(star,i)=-kappa Gamma_i^2<0.          (27)\n\nFor a concrete ONE static function, fixed before the run, let\n\n    C_grad=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),\n    F_C89(X)=max_(v in C_grad)(<X,v>-||v||^2/2).          (28)\n\nCompleting the square identifies its unique maximizing vector with the\nEuclidean projection P_C_grad(X). Projection onto this compact convex set\nis characterized by <X-P_C_grad(X),v-P_C_grad(X)><=0 for every v in C_grad.\nAdding those normal inequalities at two points proves\n||P_C_grad(X)-P_C_grad(Y)||^2<=<X-Y,P_C_grad(X)-P_C_grad(Y)>;\nhence projection is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing expressions\nin (28) bounds its first-order remainder by||X-Y||^2, proving that F_C89\nis differentiable with gradient P_C_grad. It is convex as a supremum of\naffine functions and is everywhere finite.\n\nThe values in (23)-(24) obey\n\n    <X_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>=Q_ji<=0,\n    <X_i-g_i,-g_i>=-kappa Gamma_i^2<0.\n\nThey check every vertex of C_grad, so P_C_grad(X_i)=g_i and\nF_C89(X_i)=f_i. Thus actual GD on the single function (28), with the\noriginal complete word, follows exactly all supplied samples. The terminal\ncharacter gradient in this example is one permitted completion; the\nobstruction in Sections3-4 allowed every terminal completion.\n\nThe function has min0 and minimizer set\n\n    C_grad^polar={Z:<Z,g_i><=0 for every i}.\n\nThis follows from the projection condition at0. The true initial distance\nis dist(X_0,C_grad^polar), is finite and positive, and is NOT identified\nwith||X_0||. Smoothness relative to a closest minimizer gives R>=||g_0||=1,\nwhile0 in C_grad^polar gives R<=||X_0||. The actual final numerator is the\nstrictly positive number(2kappa+1)eta^(2n). No unproved polar projection\nidentity or ignored normalization cost is needed for the uniform bound(2).\n\n## 7. Non-dyadic scope and the broader remaining interface\n\nThe construction of the probability law does not require a dyadic number\nof queries. For any integer n>=1, choose d>=3 with D=2^d>=n+1, set\np_bias=1-1/(n+1), and use the same conditional law (5) in d bits. Use only\nthe actual labels0,...,n-1 for the queried gradients. This enlarges the\ncoordinate space, not the physical schedule. There is no fictitious query\nor algorithm step.\n\nChoose one maximal queried label from each occupied H_corr-coset. The\nnumber M of occupied cosets satisfies M>=ceil(n/4), and this selection\ncontains n-1. For ANY actual nonnegative schedule h and every realizing\nfunction with positive queried amplitudes, the same proof gives\n\n    V_C89 <= A_last*beta_(M-1),\n    A_last=max{1,(h_(n-1)-1)^2}.                        (29)\n\nFor0<=h_(n-1)<=2 the descent estimate gives A_last=1. For h_(n-1)>2,\nsmoothness gives F(X_n)<=F(X_(n-1))+h_(n-1)(h_(n-1)-2)||g_(n-1)||^2/2.\nThe optimum inequality gives||g_(n-1)||^2<=2(F(X_(n-1))-F_star), yielding\nthe stated factor. Nothing is hidden in an asymptotic remainder.\nThe nonvacuity construction also works for non-dyadic n and all h_i>1:\nreplace N in (23) by the ambient D and sum only over i=0,...,n.\nDistinct-row character orthogonality still proves (24)-(28).\n\nMore generally, suppose a character correlation kernel has nonnegative\nvalues, is zero outside a subgroup of size K_corr, and the original\nqueried labels occupy M cosets, one containing the final query. No product\nassumption on its weights is needed. Representatives from those cosets\nsatisfy exactly (12), so (29) holds. On a complete dyadic set with one\nunqueried label, if all N/K_corr cosets remain represented and the last\nphysical step is at most2, then\n\n    V <= beta_(N/K_corr-1) <= 8 K_corr^2/N^2             (30)\n\nwhenever N/K_corr>=2. For any K_corr=o(N/sqrt(B_(N-1))), the original-B\nnormalized payoff of that class tends to0. In particular a fixed-size\nnonnegative correlation support cannot repair this route merely by making\nthe conditional probability law more intricate within its support.\n\nThis does NOT exclude arbitrary character measures. The proof can fail\nwhen there is no sufficiently large orthogonal selection containing the\nlast query, or when earlier gradients make negative chronological scalar\nproducts with a selected gradient. Growing correlation support and signed\nlong-range correlations are unexcluded freedoms, not automatic legal\nconstructions. They must still resolve all original cycles, one common\noptimum, the bridge timing, values, and true initial distance.\n\n## 8. Exact scope attack: orthogonal selected gradients alone are insufficient\n\nThe nonnegative-history hypothesis in (12) cannot be silently discarded.\nConsider the explicit convex1-smooth quadratic\n\n    F(x,y)=x^2/2+y^2/4, X_0=(1,1).\n\nIts unique minimizer is0 and R^2=2. Use the three actual GD steps\nh=(10,20/19,1/2), so that samples0 and2 are both queried. They give\n\n    g_0=(1,1/2), X_1=(-9,-4), g_1=(-9,-2),\n    X_2=(9/19,-36/19), g_2=(9/19,-18/19).\n    X_3=(9/38,-27/19).\n\nThe selected gradients g_0 and g_2 are orthogonal, but\n\n    <g_2,g_1>=-45/19<0,\n    2F(X_2)/R^2=729/722>1/4=beta_1.\n    2F(X_3)/R^2=1539/2888>1/4.\n\nThus the selected-gradient conclusion and its terminal consequence fail\nwithout the chronological sign condition, even with a final step below2\non an explicit static quadratic. Its full ordered\ninterpolation inequalities hold: writing A=diag(1,1/2), their residual is\n-<X_i-X_j,A(I-A)(X_i-X_j)>/2<=0. This is a symbolic hypothesis test, not\na numerical model evaluation or a proposed improving word.\n\n## 9. Provenance, checks, and status\n\nThe specified old laws were read from shared evidence only. No sibling's\nprivate memory was accessed. (1)\n\nHere Cbcd denotes C*b*c*d, and similarly for other concatenated factors.\nOn bc<1 these are exactly the complete lower-clipping inequalities,\nall four cuts, the stated first and middle triangles, mixed first cut,\nsuffix and unit-quadratic tests, last triangle, and lifted return.\nNo claim of sufficiency for the full primitive certificate is made.\n\nFor comparison with the endpoint notation, put\n\n    Ksum=C-5-a-b=c+d, y=Cb/P, m=max(1,aP),\n    Bprod=min(1/(m*y),Q/C), r_end=(C-1)/(C+1),\n    A=b*(R+b*(P+C))/J,\n    Lcut=(C*Ksum-2Q)/(C+Q),\n    Ltri=(A*Ksum-2)/(A+1).\n\nThe exact admissible endpoint interval has\n\n    d>=max(0,Ksum-1/b,Lcut,Ltri),\n    d<=min(Ksum,r_end,Ulift),\n    0<d<Ksum, d>Ksum-1/b, d(Ksum-d)<=Bprod. No endpoint belonging only\nto the closure of one of the three strict inequalities is admitted.\nWhen k=0, as will be proved on our survivor region, the actual stronger\nreturn endpoint is exactly\n\n    Ulift=(Ksum+2)/(1+y).                              (3)\n\nThus using (3) below is a proved inactive-lift branch, not deletion of\nan active term. The original lifted inequality in (1) is checked too.\n\n## 2. A four-dimensional region of strict core survivors\n\nLet B_surv be the following explicit region in the four shifts:\n\n    |a-2/5|<=1/1000, |b-4/5|<=1/1000,\n    |c-6/5|<=1/1000, |d-8/25|<=1/1000,\n    C=5+a+b+c+d>=193/25. This is not a\nfixed-capacity reduction; capacities strictly above the threshold and\na nonempty open four-dimensional set occur.\n\nThroughout (4),\n\n    399/1000<=a<=401/1000,\n    799/1000<=b<=801/1000,\n    1199/1000<=c<=1201/1000,\n    319/1000<=d<=321/1000,\n    193/25<=C<=1931/250<31/4<8. Also 1/2<77/97.\nFurther,\n\n    a^2+2a-1 <= (1401/1000)^2-2 <0,\n    aP <= (401/1000)(2401/1000)=962801/1000000<1.\n\nConsequently k=0, R=J=P^2, and m=1 throughout (4).\n\nEvery individual core inequality is checked next.\n\n### 2.1. The cuts and their independent first-cut conditions\n\nFor either u=a or u=d, u<1/2 and C>3 give\n\n    (C+1)u < (C+1)/2 < C-1.\n\nThis proves both endpoint cuts strictly. Since C<8, b<1, P>2 and H>4,\nthe second cut has Cb<8<PH. The third cut follows from\n\n    Cc < (31/4)(121/100)=3751/400,\n    QD > (419/100)(231/100)=96789/10000,\n    96789-25*3751=3014>0.\n\nSince k=0 and eta_mid=b/H, the mixed inequality C^2*eta_mid^2<J is\nthe square of the strict second cut. The middle triangle, including its lift\n\nWith k=0 the lifted middle triangle is exactly\n\n    bc[1+b(P+C)/P^2]<=D.\n\nThe bounds in (7) give\n\n    bc<=962001/1000000,\n    b(P+C)<=8110125/1000000,\n    P^2>=5755201/1000000,\n    D>=2319/1000.\n\nTherefore its left side is at most\n\n    (962001/1000000)*(13865326/5755201)<2319/1000.\n\nThe exact integer difference after clearing positive denominators is\n\n    2319000*5755201 - 962001*13865326\n        =7853641674>0.                                (8)\n\nThis proves strict survival uniformly, rather than relying on a sampled\nvalue of the middle inequality.\n\n### 2.3. Since sqrt(Delta)>4/5, d_plus>23/20.\nEvery admissible endpoint has d<=r_end<1, so the WHOLE upper product\ncomponent is empty for these projected values. Writing v=(Q+C)/Q^2<3/4,\nfor every 0<=d<=Ksum and c=Ksum-d one has\n\n    d*c*(1+v*c)<=Ksum^2/4 + (3/4)*(4*Ksum^3/27)\n               <(153/100)^2/4+(153/100)^3/9\n               =491589/500000<1.                    (11)\n\nThe product maxima used here follow from\ndc<=Ksum^2/4 and d*c^2<=4*Ksum^3/27; the latter follows by\ndifferentiating c^2(Ksum-c) on [0,Ksum]. Thus both (15) and (16) of\nthe preceding endpoint draft are actually decided on this region:\nthe upper product component is empty, the lower one has genuine strict\nsurvivors, and the last-triangle exclusion is inactive. The full certificate requires nonnegative energy on legal data\n\nThis section restates and directly derives the full interface needed\nfor the exclusion, so no draft compactness theorem is a premise.\n\nLet s=sum h_i=C-1, let e be the five-entry all-ones column, and let\n\n    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n    Lambda_ij>=0 for all i!=j in {0,...,4}, Lambda_ii=0,\n    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n    Dprim=diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)).\n\nThe original certificate in fact04219554e41d9750 requires\n\n    L^T e=omega, M=Dprim+(LT+T^T L^T)/2>=0 on R^5.     (12)\n\nFor any formal GD trajectory xi+1=xi-hi*gi, define\n\n    Qij=fi-fj+<gi,xj-xi>+||gi-gj||^2/2,\n    V=2 sum_(i<4)hi(fi-f4)\n       +sum_(i<4)hi(1-hi)||gi||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2.\n\nWriting yi=xi-gi and delta_i=fi-||gi||^2/2 gives\nQij=delta_i-delta_j+<gi,yj-yi>. Since yi=x0-(Tg)i,\nthe value-flow equality in (12) cancels all delta_i and yields\n\n    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Qij=sum_(i,j)Mij<gi,gj>.      (13)\n\nIf the data are legal, Qij<=0. Nonnegativity of every original Lambda\nand full PSD make the right side nonnegative. Therefore V>=0.\nThis uses no support deletion and no restriction on the rank of the\nformal five-gradient space.\n\nFor data with fi=<xi,gi>-||gi||^2/2 and x4=g4, direct telescoping gives\n\n    V=||x0||^2-C^2||g4||^2.                           (14)\n\nIndeed 2hi fi+hi(1-hi)||gi||^2=||xi||^2-||xi+1||^2,\nwhile f4=||g4||^2/2. These identities prove (14) after summation.\n\nWhenever all ordered Qij, including the zero optimum jet, are checked\nbelow, the interpolation construction of accepted1252b8823bb5a290\nproduces one everywhere-defined convex differentiable 1-smooth function\nrealizing the whole trajectory, with minimum zero at zero. One explicit\nform is F=Hstar, where Hstar denotes the convex conjugate of\n\n    H(v)=max_j {<xj,gj>-fj+<xj,v-gj>+||v-gj||^2/2}.\n\nAt gi, the j-th expression minus the i-th is Qij<=0; xi is a\nsubgradient of H at gi. The maximum is 1-strongly convex, so its\nconjugate has 1-Lipschitz gradient and interpolates all jets. The\nzero jet ensures F(0)=0 and gradient zero there. This also explains why\nchecking every original pair, rather than a selected subset, suffices.\n\n## 4. Direct necessary endpoint and clipping inequalities\n\nThese short derivations are included for the projected-prism proof.\nThey apply to a full certificate via (13), independently of any old\ndraft prerequisite package.\n\n### 4.1. The last endpoint and third cut\n\nFor the last cut use the scalar function with derivative\nclamp(x,[-d,1]), and start at x0=4+a+b+c=C-1-d.\nIts first four query gradients are 1 and its terminal position and\ngradient are -d. All samples have saturated values as in (14).\nThus V=(C-1-d)^2-C^2d^2>=0. Both quantities being nonnegative,\n\n    d<=r_end=(C-1)/(C+1)<1.                           (15)\n\nFor the third cut let eta=c/(2+d), use derivative\nclamp(x,[-eta,1]), and start at x0=Q. Its positions and gradients are\n\n    x=(Q,2+b,1,-c,-eta),\n    g=(1,1,1,-eta,-eta).\n\nThe updates are exactly the given h, and the lower plateau ends at\nx4=g4=-eta. Equation (14) yields\n\n    C*eta<=Q.                                        (16)\n\nThe derivative of either scalar function is continuous, nondecreasing,\nand 1-Lipschitz; integration with f(0)=0 proves convexity, smoothness\nand the stated minimum. Its saturated values are xg-g^2/2 on every\nplateau and at each adjoining quadratic endpoint.\n\n### 4.2. Both lower-clipping branches, with the terminal regime checked\n\nThe independent triangle proof in Appendix A gives cd<1 before any\nclipping result is used. This order matters: alpha=b and cd>1 would\nput the terminal point below the lower clip and invalidate (14).\n\nStart at x0=P and use f'(x)=clamp(x,[-alpha,1]). The first two\npositions are x1=1 and x2=-b, with g0=g1=1.\n\nIf bc<1, choose alpha=b. Then g2=-b, x3=bc is in (0,1), g3=bc,\nand x4=-bcd is in [-b,0] because cd<1. Thus g4=x4. The plateau\nand quadratic samples all have saturated values, so (14) gives\n\n    C*b*c*d<=P, if bc<1.                              (18)\n\nIf bc>=1, choose alpha=(1+b)/(1+c). The inequality bc>=1 gives\nalpha<=b, so g2=-alpha and x3=-b+(1+c)alpha=1. Hence g3=1 and\nx4=-d. Moreover cd<1 gives d<1/c, and\n\n    alpha-1/c=(bc-1)/(c*(1+c))>=0.\n\nThus -d belongs to [-alpha,0], g4=x4, and all samples again have\nsaturated values. Equation (14) yields\n\n    C*d<=P, if bc>=1.                                (19)\n\nThe equality bc=1 belongs to the second case. These are explicit\nlegal functions in both branches; no unproved terminal-clipping\ncondition is used. Their only extra prerequisite, cd<1, has its\nindependent proof in Appendix A.\n\n## 5. A complete legal repaired-cut witness\n\nLet a,b,c,d>0 and eta=c/(2+d). Assume b*eta<1. Put\n\n    epsilon=(1+eta)/(a^2*b*(1-b*eta))>0,\n    Jquad=[(1-b*eta)*Q^2+(1+eta)/(a^2*b)]/(1+b).\n\nDefine two scalar arrays, each following the original four steps:\n\n    xB=(Q,2+b,1,-c,-eta),\n    gB=(1,1,1,-eta,-eta),\n    xA=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,-a*b*eta),\n    gA=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*eta,-a*b*eta).\n\nIn each array define fi=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2. Now take the two-coordinate\nsum with positions X_i=(xB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*xA_i), gradients\nG_i=(gB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*gA_i), and values F_i=fB_i+epsilon*fA_i.\nInclude the optimum X_star=G_star=F_star=0.\n\n### 5.1. Every original pair and the energy\n\nFor saturated values Qij=<gi-gj,xj-gj>. The B array is the legal\nscalar cut from Section 4.1: at targets0,1 the normal is positive\nand the gradient1 is maximal; at target3 the normal is -(c-eta)<0\nand gradient-eta is minimal; targets2,4 and the optimum have zero\nnormal. All base pairs, including optimum pairs, are nonpositive.\n\nIn array A, every normal is zero except that at target3, which is\n-a*b*(c-eta)<0. The source gradients at0,1,2,4 and the optimum are\nrespectively 1,-a,ab,-ab*eta,0. Only source1 lies below -ab*eta,\nbecause b*eta<1. Thus the ONLY positive auxiliary residual is\n\n    Q_A(1,3)=a^2*b*(1-b*eta)*(c-eta)>0.\n\nIts base residual is\n\n    Q_B(1,3)=-(1+eta)*(c-eta)<0.\n\nThe chosen epsilon cancels this pair exactly. Every other original\nordered finite or optimum pair is the sum of nonpositive residuals.\nBoth arrays follow GD, so the two-coordinate array does too. The\ninterpolation construction after (14) realizes one actual function.\n\nBoth terminal positions equal their gradients. Therefore\n\n    V=Q^2-C^2*eta^2\n         +epsilon*(1-C^2*a^2*b^2*eta^2)\n      =(1+b)/(1-b*eta) * (Jquad-C^2*eta^2).            (20)\n\nEvery full certificate must consequently satisfy\n\n    C^2*eta^2<=Jquad. The repair is applicable throughout the projected prism\n\nUnder the projected-prism hypotheses in Section 6 and full\nfeasibility, the third cut (16) gives\n\n    eta<=Q/C<(17/4)/(193/25)<1.\n\nSince b<=81/100, it follows that b*eta<1. The entire projected prism is excluded from the full interface\n\nThe statement is: no full certificate (12) exists with\n\n    193/25<=C<87/10,\n    39/100<=a<=41/100, 79/100<=b<=81/100,\n    c>0,d>0, C=5+a+b+c+d. From (22),\n\n    Ksum=c+d>=3/2,\n    P<=241/100,\n    Q<=211/50<17/4,\n    B=P/(Cb)<2/5.                                    (23)\n\nThe last comparison follows from\n(2/5)*(193/25)*(79/100)>241/100.\n\nIf bc>=1, (19) gives d<=P/C<3/8. If bc<1, (18) gives cd<=B<2/5.\nSuppose in this second case that 3/8<=d<1, as allowed by (15).\nSince Ksum>=3/2,\n\n    cd=d(Ksum-d)>=d(3/2-d).\n\nThe last quadratic is concave on [3/8,1], and its endpoint values are\n27/64 and 1/2, both greater than 2/5. This contradicts cd<2/5.\nBoth branches, including the equality bc=1 in the first, thus give\n\n    d<3/8, c>9/8, eta=c/(2+d)>9/19. Using eta>9/19 and (22)--(23) gives\n\n    Jquad\n      < [(1-(79/100)*(9/19))*(17/4)^2\n          +(1+9/19)*(25/3)]/(179/100)\n      =2150863/163248 <53/4. Translation cancels from a\nwithin-right pair, so every such Q equals alpha^2 times its atomic Q.\nFor all atomic indices i,j, (3) and (10) give the two cross orientations\n\n    Q_(i,4+j)=-alpha^2 E_j+<Lg_i,t+alpha T y_j>,\n    Q_(4+i,j)=-<alpha T g_i,t>-E_j+<alpha T g_i,Ly_j>.   (12)\n\nEvery atom gradient and residual has nonnegative U,V coordinates. Thus\n<g_i,w>, <w,y_j>, <p,g_i>, and <p,y_j> are nonnegative. Equation (7)\nthen makes both mixed terms in (12) nonpositive. Also\n\n    <Lg_i,t>=-delta r_0 <g_i,w><=0,\n    <alpha T g_i,t>\n        =delta sigma r_0^2 N <p,g_i>/R^2>=0.\n\nConsequently both expressions in (12) are nonpositive for every i,j,\nincluding all endpoint directions.\n\nThe optimum rows follow from (3): every Delta_i is nonnegative, every\nleft aheight is zero, and every right aheight is nonpositive. This checks\nall 72 distinct ordered pairs on {star,0,...,7}: 24 within-child pairs,\n32 cross pairs, and 16 optimum incidences. No pair or original gradient\nhas been removed. The argument works for the whole interval 0<sigma<=1.\n\n## 5. One global function and its exact minimizer set\n\nDefine on the same R^3\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,max_(0<=i<=7)(<Y_i,v>-Delta_i)),\n    F(X)=sup_v(<X,v>-H(v)).                              (13)\n\nThe function H is finite, continuous, 1-strongly convex and has\nquadratic growth. The supremum has a unique maximizer. The explicit\ninterpolation construction in accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, applied\nto the full original pair check above, proves that F is finite,\neverywhere differentiable, convex and 1-smooth, and\n\n    F(X_i)=F_i, grad F(X_i)=G_i, F(0)=0=min F.\n\nThus all eight samples belong to ONE function fixed before GD starts.\nThe construction does not paste separate functions along the run.\n\nFor clarity its full minimizer set can be computed directly. All six\nnonendpoint Delta_i are strictly positive; the two endpoint quantities\nDelta_3 and Delta_7 are zero and their slopes in (13) are 0 and t.\nThus, along any fixed v and for all sufficiently small epsilon>0,\n\n    H(epsilon v)=epsilon^2||v||^2/2\n                          +epsilon max(0,<t,v>).        (14)\n\nFor any X in [0,t], <X,v><=max(0,<t,v>) for every v.\nEquation (13) then gives F(X)<=0; the choice v=0 gives F(X)>=0.\nConversely, if X lies outside that segment, take its closest point z on\nthe segment and v=X-z. Projection onto a segment gives\n<v,z'-z><=0 for every z' in the segment, whereas <v,X-z>=||v||^2>0.\nHence <X,v>>max(0,<t,v>). Equation (14), with epsilon sufficiently small,\ngives <X,epsilon v>-H(epsilon v)>0, so F(X)>0. We have proved\n\n    argmin F=[0,t].                                     (15)\n\nThis remains valid at sigma=1, when the segment is the singleton {0}.\nThe exact TRUE initial radius is R because\n\n    <Lp,t>=-delta r_0 D<=0,\n    ||Lp-q t||^2=R^2+q^2||t||^2-2q<Lp,t>>=R^2\n                                                    for 0<=q<=1.\n\nZero attains this minimum. No normalization has substituted distance to\na convenient, more distant minimizer.\n\n## 6. Exact state law, including the cost of translation\n\nThe physical capacity of (9) is\n\n    A_1=1+sum h_7=2A+r_0=lambda A.\n\nLet w_1=G_7 and N_1=||w_1||^2=alpha^2 N. The actual doubled terminal\ngap is J_1=2F(X_7)=N_1. Therefore\n\n    V_7=2(F(X_7)-min F)/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2\n       =alpha^2 N/R^2,\n    kappa_7=A_1^2 V_7=sigma^2 kappa_3^2.                (16)\n\nFor sigma>=9999/10000, (6) gives\n\n    sigma kappa_3 > (9999/10000)(2001/2000)\n                   =1+7999/20000000 > 1+1/3000,\n\nand hence (1) follows by squaring. This prices the contracted right\ncopy: its loss is precisely the factor sigma^2.\n\nThe endpoint shape has the exact two parameters\n\n    tau_1=<w_1,t>/N_1=chi D/N,\n    nu_1=||t||^2/N_1=chi^2 R^2/N.                       (17)\n\nAt the next bridge 1+r_1, the outgoing point is\n\n    d=t-r_1 w_1,\n    ||d||^2=N_1(r_1^2-2r_1 tau_1+nu_1).                (18)\n\nThe vector d is nonzero for sigma>0. Indeed the first isometry T sends\nw to a vector with a nonzero b_axis-perpendicular component, because p\nhas a nonzero U component and w has none. The vector t has zero in that\nlast direct-sum component. Thus r_1 w_1 cannot equal t.\n\nIf an origin-aligned scaled-copy next join were legal with its copied\nvalues, its forced scale would be beta=||d||/R, its capacity would be\nA_2=lambda A_1, and its formal endpoint law would be\n\n    kappa_15,formal\n       =(1-2tau_1/r_1+nu_1/r_1^2) kappa_7^2.           (19)\n\nThis is a conditional arithmetic state law, not a claim of legality.\nIt even has a surplus in the interval used here. The elementary bounds\nN>39/100,D<11/10 and chi<=1/9999 imply\ntau_1<3/9999<1/3000. Since r_1>5, the factor in (19) exceeds\n1-1/7500. Also kappa_7^2>1+1/750, so\n(1-1/7500)(1+1/750)>1. Thus the formal next state exceeds 1.\nThe following original rows explain why this positive number does not\nbelong to an actual matched fifteen-step trajectory.\n\n## 7. The exact two-row obstruction, with every transverse map free\n\nIn this section abbreviate the seven-step data by (X_i,G_i,F_i) as in\n(8), and let P=X_0=Lp. Preserve all these data in each child. Its physical bridge\nrequires\n\n    beta T_next P=E d, beta^2=||d||^2/R^2.              (20)\n\nAll orientations satisfying (20) are allowed. In particular we impose\nno sign, rank, symmetry or sparsity condition on their remaining cross\ninner products.\n\nThe retained child has G_3=Lw, G_6=alpha T V and G_7=rho G_6. Thus\n\n    t=-delta r_0 G_3,\n    d=-delta r_0 G_3-r_1 rho G_6,\n    G_6+c_sigma G_3=-d/(r_1 rho),\n    c_sigma=delta r_0/(r_1 rho)>=0.                     (21)\n\nFor its local sample 5 put\n\n    Delta_5=alpha^2 z>0,\n    Y_5=t+alpha T(zU+K V),\n    H_sigma=delta+sigma z Dlen/R^2.\n\nEquations (7) and (10) give, by direct multiplication,\n\n    <P,Y_5>=-r_0 D H_sigma,\n    aheight_3=0,\n    aheight_6=-<G_6,t>=-chi Delta_5.                    (22)\n\nApply the universally valid identity (3) to original parent rows (6,13)\nand (3,13). Their common receiver 13 is the right local sample 5. The\nincident is finding13d29bfe0c871924; no numerical restart is part of this paper.\nEverything below is symbolic proof text, not a numerical candidate evaluation.\n\nThe accepted input is the original full certificate interface in\nfact04219554e41d9750, Section10. Draft5bd01e2b19002a59 already contained the\nadjacent certificate and weighted-cut identity; those are explicitly recalled\nand rederived here, not claimed as new or imported as verified premises.\nDraft443f4b4325a9901d already contained the positive flow/kernel center. The\nnew results are the uniform threshold consequence in Section3 and the explicit\ndense strictly positive calibration family in Section5. No seed of sum168/25\nis constructed, and no unrestricted no-seed theorem is asserted.\n\n## 1. Original five-gradient space and its two exact balances\n\nLet h=(h0,h1,h2,h3) have nonnegative entries. Put\n\n    P_i=sum_(t<i) h_t, 0<=i<=4; s=P_4; p_i=1+P_i.\n\nLet e be the five-entry all-ones column, and let T have diagonal1,\nT_it=h_t for t<i, and entries0 above the diagonal. Every formal gradient\ng0,...,g4 is retained, even when a step vanishes. A full primitive certificate\nhas twenty nonnegative coefficients Lambda_ij, i!=j, zero diagonal, and\n\n    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n    D=diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),\n    L^T e=omega, M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0.                 (1)\n\nThis is the exact interface in accepted04219554e41d9750. Its identity is\n\n    V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g0,...,g4)>,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)\n          +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.\n\nThe matrix inequality in (1) is on ALL of R5. Since Te=p and\nsum_(i<4)h_i P_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2, direct expansion gives\n\n    e^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2,\n    omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2,\n    e^T M e=0.\n\nFor any real column v, nonnegativity of (e+t v)^T M(e+t v) for both signs\nof sufficiently small t forces v^T Me=0. Therefore Me=0. Substituting\nin (1) gives\n\n    LP=diag(p)omega.                                    (2)\n\nFor an explicit component check at i<4,\n(T^T omega)_i=-2h_i+2h_i(P_i+h_i), so\n(Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2. At i=4 it is\n-s(s+1)+(LP)_4/2. Thus every original row moment is retained.\n\nDefine mu_ij=Lambda_ij p_j. Equation(2), together with the value flow,\nimplies that mu is balanced at every vertex:\n\n    sum_j mu_ij=sum_j mu_ji.                             (3)\n\nIndeed (2) is equivalent to sum_j Lambda_ij p_j=p_i sum_j Lambda_ji.\nNo symmetry of Lambda or mu is assumed.\n\n## 2. Weighted cuts, with all original edges present\n\nFor j in{0,1,2,3}, let z^(j) be1 on indices0,...,j and0 thereafter.\nWrite A_j=P_(j+1), and define nonnegative weighted crossing sums\n\n    F_j=sum_(i<=j<k) Lambda_ik (p_k-p_(j+1)),\n    B_j=sum_(k<=j<i) Lambda_ik (p_j-p_k).                 (4)\n\nThese are sums over every original crossing pair. Nonnegative h makes\nevery weight nonnegative; zero displacement simply has weight0.\nThe adjacent forward and backward edges have weight0.\n\nLet O_j and I_j be the unweighted forward and backward crossing masses.\nSumming the original value flows gives\n\n    O_j-I_j=-2A_j.\n\nSumming(3) over the prefix gives\n\n    p_(j+1) O_j+F_j=p_j I_j-B_j,\n    h_j O_j=2p_j A_j-F_j-B_j.                            (5)\n\nWe also have the full formal-direction identity\n\n    (z^(j))^T M z^(j)=-A_j^2+O_j+F_j.                   (6)\n\nTo check it directly, Tz has entry1+P_i on i<=j and A_j otherwise.\nThe diagonal contribution is sum_(i<=j)h_i(2-h_i). The internal part\nof z^T LTz is minus the internal row moment; its forward-crossing\npart is sum_(i<=j<k)Lambda_ik(1+P_i-A_j). Sum the row moments(2)\nover i<=j to replace the internal part by\n\n    -2sum_(i<=j)h_i(1+P_i)\n                  +sum_(i<=j<k)Lambda_ik(P_k-P_i).\n\nThe combined forward coefficient is1+P_k-A_j. The remaining scalar is\n\n    sum_(i<=j)h_i(2-h_i)-2sum_(i<=j)h_i(1+P_i)=-A_j^2.\n\nThis proves(6) without deleting a gradient coordinate or an interpolation\npair. Define the step deficit polynomial\n\n    Dstep_j=h_j(P_j+h_j)-2(P_j+1).                       (7)\n\nCombining(5) and(6), for h_j>0, yields\n\n    h_j (z^(j))^T M z^(j)\n       =-A_j Dstep_j+(h_j-1)F_j-B_j.                    (8)\n\nConsequently full PSD requires\n\n    (h_j-1)F_j-B_j >= A_j Dstep_j.                      (9)\n\nFormula(8) is the weighted-cut identity already present in\ndraft5bd01e2b19002a59, rederived here. The new point below is that a\nUNIFORM strictly positive burden in(9) is forced at the target sum.\n\n## 3. A uniform remote-edge burden at sum168/25\n\nClaim. If a full certificate(1) has sum h>=168/25, then some\nj in{0,1,2} has\n\n    Dstep_j>1/16, h_j>1,\n    (h_j-1)F_j-B_j > P_(j+1)/16,\n    F_j>P_(j+1)/(16(h_j-1))>1/16.                       (10)\n\nThis holds for all nonnegative steps, with no assumption of positivity,\norder, symmetry, rank, saturation, or support.\n\nFirst prove a purely scalar assertion. Suppose, to the contrary, that\nDstep_j<=1/16 at all four indices. Let r(P) be the positive root of\n\n    r^2+Pr=2(P+1)+1/16, P>=0.\n\nThe polynomial is strictly increasing in its nonnegative argument.\nAt argument2 its left side minus right side is2-1/16>0, so r(P)<2.\nImplicit differentiation gives\n\n    r'(P)=(2-r(P))/(2r(P)+P)>0. Rational scalar estimates used in the full pair check\n\nThe following deliberately loose rational bounds follow from\n14142/10000<sqrt(2)<14143/10000, proved by squaring, and the defining\nformulas:\n\n    341/100<m<171/50, 241/100<K<121/50,\n    1<z<201/200, 29/5<Dlen<59/10,\n    29/5<r_1<6, 12/5<r_0<49/20,\n    14<R^2<71/5, 2/5<N<21/50, 0<D<11/10,\n    e>1/2, rho>99/100, 0<=chi<=1/9999. (12)\n\nIndeed Hout>(29/5)^2(2/5)-2(1/9999)6(11/10)>67/5.\nUsing sigma^2>999/1000, the lower estimate for nscale is greater than\n\n    (999/1000)(12/5)^2(2/5)(67/5)/(71/5)^2 >1/8.\n\nFor the upper estimate discard the negative term in Hout and use\nchi^2 R^2<1/1000000. The upper bound becomes\n\n    (49/20)^2(21/50)[36(21/50)+1/1000000]/14^2 <1/5.\n\nDefine the auxiliary quantities\n\n    Caff=K Dlen/(r_0 r_1 rho^2),\n    Tfirst=Mout Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout),\n    Lcoef=omega eta sigma r_0 D/R^2,\n    Tsecond=Lcoef Dlen/(R^2 nscale).\n\nThe crucial strict endpoint bound is\n\n    rho Caff<1.                                    (13)\n\nIt has the exact algebraic certificate\n\n    r_0 r_1 rho-K Dlen\n       =(-381891+284836 sqrt(2))/9000000>0,\n\nwhere already sqrt(2)>7/5 proves positivity. In particular\nCaff<600/599 and Caff-1<1/599.\n\nWe also have\n\n    Tfirst<=Caff,\n    Tsecond-Tfirst < (11/5)chi   if chi>0,\n    Lcoef/z<1/2,\n    Tsecond+chi(Lcoef-z)/(nscale z)<=Caff.           (14)\n\nAt chi=0 the second difference is zero and the other non-strict\nrelations continue to hold. Here are derivations. The inequality\nTfirst<=Caff is equivalent to\n\n    (1+chi)(r_1 D-chi R^2)r_1 N <= D Hout.\n\nAfter cancelling the constant terms, the difference of the left side\nand right side is\n\n    chi r_1 [r_1 DN-R^2 N+2D^2]\n                 -chi^2 R^2(r_1 N+D) <=0.\n\nThe bracket is negative because R^2 N>28/5, whereas\nr_1 DN+2D^2<6(11/10)(21/50)+2(11/10)^2<28/5.\nNext z omega=Mout+chi m^2, so\n\n    Tsecond-Tfirst=chi m^2 Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout).\n\nThe coefficient is less than 11/5 by (11)-(12): its numerator is\nless than (171/50)^2(59/10), and its denominator exceeds\n(999/1000)(12/5)(99/100)(67/5); comparison of these two rational\nproducts gives the asserted bound. Also\n\n    Lcoef/z <= r_0 r_1 N/R^2\n             < (49/20)6(21/50)/14 <1/2.\n\nThus chi(1-Lcoef/z)/nscale >=(5/2)chi, with strict inequality when\nchi>0. Subtracting this from the second bound gives the last part of\n(14). These estimates keep the internal residual cost; it is precisely\nthe term chi(Lcoef-z) that must not be dropped at the critical row.\n\nFor later use, Tfirst,Tsecond<6/5. This follows from Caff<600/599,\nthe second difference bound, and chi<=1/9999. Moreover\n\n    nscale z(Caff-1)<d_atom/2.                      (15)\n\nIf Caff<=1 it is immediate. Otherwise the left side is less than\n(1/5)(201/200)/599=201/599000, while d_atom/2>1/2400;\nthe comparison is 482400<599000.\n\nPut b_j=<p,y_j> and l_j=<w,y_j> for the atom. Their table is\n\n| j | E_j | b_j | l_j |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| 0 | m-1 | R^2-m | D |\n| 1 | z | z Dlen | D |\n| 2 | e | (K-1)z | rho e |\n| 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n\nFor j=0,1,2 one has b_j<R^2 and l_j/E_j<=rho. Also\nb_1/E_1=Dlen, while b_j/E_j<(4/5)Dlen for j=0,2.\nFor j=0, the numerator is less than 54/5 and\n(4/5)Dlen(m-1)>(4/5)(29/5)(12/5)>54/5.\nFor j=2, b_2/E_2=K<(4/5)Dlen follows from (11).\n\nFinally the two bounds needed in the reverse orientation are\n\n    sigma r_0 r_1 D m rho/R^4 <1/3,\n    (sigma r_0 D/R^4) Mout <1/10<nscale.             (16)\n\nFor the first, bound its numerator by (49/20)6(11/10)(171/50)\nand its denominator below by 196. For the second, use\nMout<=r_1D and bound the numerator by (49/20)6(11/10)^2.\nBoth resulting rational inequalities are strict.\n\n## 7. Every forward cross row\n\nAbbreviate the following four exact child correlations:\n\n    u_i=<G_i,d>, v_i=<P,G_i>,\n    p_i=<P,Y_i>, q_i=<d,Y_i>.\n\nFrom (6)-(9) their entries are\n\n    u_0=u_1=0,\n    u_2=sigma r_0 rho eta Mout/R^2, u_3=rho u_2,\n    u_4=u_5=k chi m, u_6=-k omega eta, u_7=rho u_6;\n\n    v_0=v_1=m, v_2=z, v_3=D,\n    v_(4+j)=-(sigma r_0 D/R^2)<p,g_j>;\n\n    p_j=b_j,\n    p_(4+j)=-sigma r_0 D(chi+b_j/R^2);\n\n    q_j=sigma r_0 Mout l_j/R^2,\n    q_(4+j)=k[-chi Mout+chi b_j-r_1 l_j],           0<=j<=3.\n                                                               (17)\n\nIn particular p_0,p_1,p_2>0, p_3=0, p_4,p_5,p_6<0,\nand p_7<=0. All q_0,q_1,q_2>0, q_3=0, q_4,q_5,q_6<0,\nand q_7=-k chi Mout<=0. To see the strict late signs, use\nchi b_j<=chi R^2<1/600, while r_1 l_j>1 for j=0,1,2.\n\nEquation (4) and the cross identity (10) give every forward cross row:\n\n    Q_(i,8+j)=aheight_i-c_i-nscale Delta_j+u_i p_j/R^2.\n                                                               (18)\n\nAll aheight_i-c_i are nonpositive. Therefore only positive products\nu_i p_j need further consideration; zero products cause no problem.\n\nFirst let the receiver be j=0,1,2. Positive products can have source\ni=2,3,4,5. For i=4,5, aheight_i=-chi k m and u_i=chi k m,\nso their sum with the mixed term is chi k m(b_j/R^2-1)<=0.\nFor i=2 the ratio of u_2 b_j/R^2 to nscale E_j is\n\n    Tfirst * b_j/(Dlen E_j).\n\nAt j=0,2 it is less than (6/5)(4/5)<1. At j=1, (14)-(15)\ngive Q_(2,9)<=nscale z(Caff-1)-d_atom<-d_atom/2.\nFor i=3 the same ratio is multiplied by rho, and (13)-(14)\nmake it strictly smaller than one, including at j=1.\n\nNow let the receiver be 4+j, for atomic j=0,1,2,3. A positive product\ncan only have source i=6 or 7. Formula (18) becomes exactly\n\n    Q_(6,12+j)/k\n       =chi(Lcoef-z)+Lcoef b_j/R^2-nscale E_j-d_atom,\n    Q_(7,12+j)/k\n       =rho[chi(Lcoef-z)+Lcoef b_j/R^2]-nscale E_j.\n                                                               (19)\n\nFor j=3, E_j=b_j=0 and Lcoef<z, so both are nonpositive.\nFor j=0,2 drop the nonpositive chi term and use\nTsecond b_j/(Dlen E_j)<(6/5)(4/5)<1.\nFor j=1 use the last inequality of (14). It gives\n\n    Q_(6,13)/k <=nscale z(Caff-1)-d_atom<-d_atom/2,\n    Q_(7,13)/k <=nscale z(rho Caff-1)<0.\n\nReceivers with p_j=0 and products of opposite signs were already\ncovered by (18). This exhausts all 64 forward cross rows, including\nevery row into the actual final sample 15.\n\n## 8. Every reverse cross row and the total original system\n\nThe other orientation is\n\n    Q_(8+i,j)=nscale aheight_i-Delta_j+c_j+v_i q_j/R^2.\n                                                               (20)\n\nFor early receivers j=0,1,2, a positive product requires i=0,1,2,3.\nTheir v_i are at most m. The positive term divided by E_j is at most\n\n    m sigma r_0 Mout rho/R^4\n       <=m sigma r_0 r_1 D rho/R^4<1/3.\n\nBut Delta_j-c_j>=rho E_j, so (20) is nonpositive. Other source signs\nonly help. Receiver j=3 has q_j=Delta_j=c_j=0, leaving\nnscale aheight_i<=0.\n\nFor a late receiver 4+j, a positive product requires a late source\n4+i. Set A_i=<p,g_i>, which is one of m,m,z,D and is positive.\nUsing (7) and (17), combine the source height and mixed term exactly:\n\n    nscale aheight_(4+i)+v_(4+i)q_(4+j)/R^2\n      = k A_i {chi[-nscale+(sigma r_0 D/R^4)Mout]\n               +(sigma r_0 D/R^4)[-chi b_j+r_1 l_j]}.\n                                                               (21)\n\nFor j=3 the last bracket is zero; (16) makes (21) nonpositive.\nFor j=0,1,2 discard its nonpositive chi terms. The remaining term,\ndivided by k E_j, is at most\n\n    m sigma r_0 r_1 D rho/R^4<1/3.\n\nThe receiver has Delta_(4+j)-c_(4+j)>=k rho E_j. Thus (20) is\nnonpositive. Early sources have v_i>0 and q_(4+j)<=0, so they are\nalready safe. This checks all 64 reverse cross rows, including rows\nwhose source is the final gradient.\n\nSections 3-4 check all 56 ordered within-left rows; section 3 and\nscaling check all 56 within-right rows. Sections 7-8 check all 128\ncross rows. The 32 optimum incidences hold because every aheight is\nnonpositive and every Delta is nonnegative, including after (1).\nThus all 272 distinct ordered rows on {star,0,...,15} are proved.\nNo sampled gradient, remote pair, or original position has been removed.\n\n## 9. One global function and the TRUE radius\n\nLet yhat_i=Z_i-J_i and Deltahat_i=fhat_i-||J_i||^2/2. On the same R^5\ndefine\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,max_(0<=i<=15)(<yhat_i,v>-Deltahat_i)),\n    F_parent(Z)=sup_v(<Z,v>-H(v)).                  (22)\n\nAll rows just proved, together with accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`,\nshow that this is one finite, everywhere differentiable, convex,\n1-smooth function satisfying F_parent(Z_i)=fhat_i, grad F_parent(Z_i)=J_i,\nand F_parent(0)=0=min F_parent. Its trajectory from Z_0 is exactly (2).\n\nOnly samples 3,7,11,15 have Deltahat_i=0; their residuals are\n0,E_top t,0,B_top t. All other Deltahat_i are strictly positive.\nIt follows directly that\n\n    argmin F_parent=conv{0,E_top t,B_top t}.         (23)\n\nFor completeness, at sufficiently small epsilon>0 in any fixed\ndirection v,\n\n    H(epsilon v)=epsilon^2||v||^2/2\n       +epsilon max(0,<E_top t,v>,<B_top t,v>).\n\nEvery point of the displayed convex hull has conjugate value zero.\nIf Z is outside it, let z be its closest point and v=Z-z. This proves both inclusions in (23).\n\nThe exact nearest-minimizer distance is R. Indeed\n\n    <P,t>=-delta r_0 D<=0,\n    <P,d>=sigma r_0 D Mout/R^2>0,\n    <E_top P,B_top t>=<P,d><P,t>/R^2<=0.\n\nHence <Z_0,z><=0 for every minimizer z in (23), and\n||Z_0-z||^2=R^2+||z||^2-2<Z_0,z>>=R^2.\nZero attains equality. At sigma<1 the internal residuals are nonzero;\nthey have not been identified with zero or hidden in a surrogate radius.\n\nThe endpoint value is fhat_15=nscale k N/2. Thus (3)'s exact payoff is\n2F_parent(Z_15)/dist(Z_0,argmin F_parent)^2=nscale k N/R^2.\nRescaling F_parent(RZ)/R^2 makes the true initial distance one without\nchanging this normalized payoff or any step. Multiplying the function\nby a desired L_smooth>0 gives the same normalized payoff with steps\nh_i/L_smooth. This is the original doubled-gap W=2R convention.\n\n## 10. Paid state, retained value costs, and the next question\n\nThe atom's state is kappa_3=A^2 N/R^2. Its exact defining expressions are\n\n    m=(574+319s)/300, K=(567+622s)/600,\n    z=(145+4s)/150,\n    R^2=(620189+458670s)/90000,\n    N=358801(145+4s)/[90000(567+622s)].\n\nMultiplication using s^2=2 gives\n\n    kappa_3-1=(-8324118905+8781249146s)\n                     /(2305581607500+1614558620000s)>1/2000.\n\nThe denominator is positive. Two thousand times its numerator minus\nits denominator equals 15947939672000s-18953819417500; using s>7/5\ngives the positive lower bound 3373296123300.\n\nThe actual capacities are A_1=lambda A and A_2=lambda^2 A. The seven-\nstep endpoint and the new fifteen-step endpoint satisfy\n\n    N_1=kN, kappa_7=A_1^2 N_1/R^2=sigma^2 kappa_3^2,\n    N_2=nscale N_1, kappa_15=A_2^2 N_2/R^2\n                   =[Hout/(r_1^2 N)] kappa_7^2.     (24)\n\nSince sigma kappa_3>(9999/10000)(2001/2000)>1+1/3000,\nkappa_7>1+1/1500. Put tau=<w_1,t>/N_1=chi D/N and\nnu_res=||t||^2/N_1=chi^2 R^2/N. Bounds (11) give\ntau<3/9999<1/3000, so\n\n    Hout/(r_1^2 N)=1-2tau/r_1+nu_res/r_1^2>1-1/7500.\n\nTherefore kappa_15>(1-1/7500)(1+1/1500)^2>1+1/1000.\nFor the last comparison it suffices to use\n1/750-1/7500-1/(750*7500)>1/1000.\n\nThe actual next boundary includes MORE than these three scalars. Its\ncomplete residual/value data are\n\n    yhat_i=E_top Y_i,\n    Deltahat_i=Delta_i-c_i,\n    aheight_hat_i=aheight_i-c_i,                    0<=i<=7,\n    yhat_(8+j)=B_top Y_j,\n    Deltahat_(8+j)=nscale Delta_j,\n    aheight_hat_(8+j)=nscale aheight_j,              0<=j<=7.\n                                                               (25)\n\nIn particular the two costs in (1) are real intrinsic height deficits\nat queried points, not available a second time for free. At samples 2\nand 6 their incoming predecessor rows from 3 and 7 have been saturated.\nEvery residual and value in (25) must be kept in the next model.\n\nWriting w_2=J_15=B_top w_1 and t_2=yhat_15=B_top t gives\n\n    <w_2,t_2>/N_2=tau, ||t_2||^2/N_2=nu_res.\n\nAt the next physical bridge r_2=lambda A, the outgoing vector is\nd_2=t_2-r_2 w_2, with\n\n    ||d_2||^2=N_2(r_2^2-2r_2 tau+nu_res).            (26)\n\nIf another origin-aligned source-matching join, with endpoint values\nretained, were actually legal, its formal state would be\n\n    kappa_31,formal\n       =(1-2tau/r_2+nu_res/r_2^2) kappa_15^2>1.\n\nThis last formula is CONDITIONAL. Proof extracts a new exact full-cone region from charged case31; no threshold seed or new fact.", "evidence": "# A new full-cone endpoint prism excluded by the original repaired cut\n\nStatus: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED, max4 round27. The witness\nmechanism is the previously published C83 repaired cut, not a new witness\nformula. It is rederived completely below. The new result is the following\ncapacity-uniform exclusion prism, prompted by charged discovery case31.\nNo numerical output is a premise of the proof, and no fact_submit is authorized.\n\nWrite h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), C=1+sum h=5+a+b+c+d. There is NO full primitive\ncertificate from accepted fact04219554e41d9750 when\n\n    C>=193/25,\n    27/50<=a<=14/25,\n    69/100<=b<=71/100,\n    c>0, d>0.                                             (1)\n\nThere is no upper bound on C, no relation or ordering between c and d,\nand no restriction on any of the twenty nonnegative multipliers or on the\nrank of the full original five-gradient matrix. This is a restricted\nstep prism, not global four-step seed nonexistence.\n\n## 1. Full interface, endpoint bound, and quadratic test\n\nLet P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P4, p_i=1+P_i, and let e be the five-entry\nall-ones column. With the original twenty nonnegative Lambda_ij,\n\n    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i,\n    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n    D=diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),\n    L^T e=omega, M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0.                  (2)\n\nAll matrices act on the five independent formal gradients. This is the\naccepted interface, which makes the original primitive energy\n\n    V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                               -s(s+1)||g4||^2\n\nnonnegative on every actual convex differentiable1-smooth GD trajectory.\n\nThe identities Te=p and sum_i h_i P_i=(s^2-sum_i h_i^2)/2 give e^TMe=0.\nFull PSD then forces Me=0 by expanding(e+t v)^T M(e+t v) for both signs\nof t. In particular, its terminal row moment is\n\n    sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j(s-P_j)=2sC.                       (3)\n\nThe terminal diagonal in(2) is sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j-s(s+2)>=0.\nSince s-P_j>=h3=1+d and s>0, (3) implies\n\n    1+d<=2C/(C+1), hence d<1.                           (4)\n\nUse the actual scalar function f(x)=x^2/2, started at x0=1. Its gradients\nare pquad_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t), and Tpquad=e. Therefore\n\n    pquad^T M pquad=pquad^T D pquad\n         =1-C^2 pquad_4^2.\n\nThe last equality telescopes\nh_i(2-h_i)pquad_i^2=pquad_i^2-pquad_(i+1)^2.\nAll four excesses in(1) are positive, so full PSD requires\n\n    C a b c d<=1.                                       (5)\n\nThese arguments use no compact-domain draft or prefix primitivity.\n\n## 2. A scalar cut and the repaired two-dimensional cut\n\nPut Q=3+a+b and eta=c/(2+d)>0. The scalar function with derivative\nclamp(x,[-eta,1]), normalized to minimum0, has the actual trajectory\n\n    xB=(Q,2+b,1,-c,-eta),\n    gB=(1,1,1,-eta,-eta).\n\nAt every sample its value is fB_i=xB_i*gB_i-gB_i^2/2.\nThe derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, so the\nfunction is convex, differentiable and1-smooth. The four original GD\nupdates follow from c=(2+d)eta. Telescoping the primitive energy gives\n\n    V_B=Q^2-C^2 eta^2.\n\nConsequently every full certificate also obeys\n\n    C eta<=Q.                                          (6)\n\nOn(1), b<1,Q<5,C>7, so(6) implies b eta<1.\nWe now rederive the actual all-pair repaired cut on this matched domain.\nSet\n\n    epsilon=(1+eta)/(a^2 b(1-b eta))>0,\n    xA=(1,-a,ab,-abc,-ab eta),\n    gA=(1,-a,ab,-ab eta,-ab eta),\n    fA_i=xA_i*gA_i-gA_i^2/2.\n\nBoth scalar arrays follow the SAME actual four steps. Define actual\ntwo-dimensional data\n\n    X_i=(xB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*xA_i),\n    G_i=(gB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*gA_i),\n    F_i=fB_i+epsilon*fA_i,\n\nand include the optimum X_star=G_star=0,F_star=0.\n\nHere is a complete check of all original interpolation pairs, including\nall optimum and terminal pairs. For saturated values f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2,\nthe original residual is Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j).\nIn B, targets0 and1 have nonnegative normal and maximal gradient1;\ntarget3 has negative normal-(c-eta) and minimal gradient-eta;\ntargets2,4 and star have zero normal. Every B residual is nonpositive.\nIn A, only target3 has a nonzero normal, namely-ab(c-eta)<0.\nAmong all A gradients, only gA_1=-a lies below-ab eta, since b eta<1;\nthe others are1,ab,-ab eta,-ab eta,0. Thus the only positive A residual is\n\n    Q_A(1,3)=a^2 b(1-b eta)(c-eta).\n\nThe same B pair has residual-(1+eta)(c-eta). The stated epsilon cancels\nthis pair exactly, and every other total residual is nonpositive.\n\nFor a direct realization, let K be the convex hull of the six vectors\nG_star,G0,...,G4. Define\n\n    F(X)=max_(G in K)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).\n\nThe pair inequalities just proved say that X_j-G_j is an outward normal\nto K at G_j, for every j. Completing the square shows that the maximizer\nis the Euclidean projection of X onto K; it is unique. Hence F(X_j)=F_j\nand grad F(X_j)=G_j. Projection is1-Lipschitz: adding the two projection\nnormal inequalities gives ||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>. Thus this F is a convex\ndifferentiable1-smooth function. Since0 belongs to K, F>=0 and F(0)=0.\nThis constructs one actual function and all the GD updates, rather than\ncombining transcripts from different functions.\n\nBoth terminal positions equal their gradients. Telescoping the saturated\nvalues in the original primitive energy therefore gives\n\n    V=Q^2-C^2 eta^2+epsilon(1-C^2 a^2 b^2 eta^2)\n      =(1+b)/(1-b eta) * (J-C^2 eta^2),\n    J=[(1-b eta)Q^2+(1+eta)/(a^2 b)]/(1+b).              (7)\n\nThe coefficient identity uses (1-b eta)+b(1+eta)=1+b.\nFull-certificate feasibility consequently requires C^2 eta^2<=J.\nThis is the old repaired-cut mechanism with all hypotheses and all pairs\nreproduced; the new prism exclusion follows next.\n\n## 3. Hence\n\n    d<31/100, c>57/50,\n    eta=c/(2+d)>38/77.                                 (9)\n\nSince C>=193/25>77/10, (9) yields\n\n    C^2 eta^2>(19/5)^2=361/25. Therefore the coefficient of eta\nin J from(7), namely[1/(a^2 b)-bQ^2]/(1+b), is strictly negative.\nSince eta>0,\n\n    J<J(0)=(Q^2+1/(a^2 b))/(1+b)\n       <24/(169/100)=2400/169<361/25.                   (11)\n\nThe last comparison is60000<61009. Equations(10)--(11) make the\nactual legal energy(7) strictly negative. This contradicts the full\ncertificate, and proves the whole prism exclusion(1).\n\nThe proof retains ALL twenty multiplier signs and the full original\nfive-gradient PSD condition. (38)\n\nIn particular barDelta_(4b+j)>=rho s_b E_j for every b,j.\nThe increments (28) are c_lift chi s_b D for b=1,3 and zero\notherwise. Thus every barH_i+L_i is nonpositive, and every\nbarDelta_i+L_i is nonnegative.\n\nWrite\n\n    V_i=<P_2,J_i>, Pval_i=<P_2,barY_i>,\n    U_i=<J_i,d_2>, Qval_i=<d_2,barY_i>.\n\nThe following is a complete four-block table, derived from (6), (10),\nand d_2=B_top alpha T(chi p-r_2 w):\n\n| b | V_(4b+j) | Pval_(4b+j) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0 | Patom_j | b_j |\n| 1 | -q_axis Patom_j | -q_axis(chi R^2+b_j) |\n| 2 | Cpair_1 Patom_j | Cpair_1 b_j |\n| 3 | -Cpair_1 q_axis Patom_j | -Cpair_1 q_axis(chi R^2+b_j) |\n\n| b | U_(4b+j) | Qval_(4b+j) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0 | Cpair_2 sigma r_0 Mout_1 Zeta_j/R^2 | Cpair_2 sigma r_0 Mout_1 l_j/R^2 |\n| 1 | Cpair_2 k(chi Patom_j-r_1 Zeta_j) | Cpair_2 k(-chi Mout_1+chi b_j-r_1 l_j) |\n| 2 | n sigma r_0 Mout_2 Zeta_j/R^2 | n sigma r_0 Mout_2 l_j/R^2 |\n| 3 | nk(chi Patom_j-r_2 Zeta_j) | nk(-chi Mout_2+chi b_j-r_2 l_j) |\n                                                               (39)\n\nFor example a vector in the left seven-step part pairs with\nB_top alpha T(chi p-r_2w) by multiplying its pairing with the old\noutgoing vector d by <P,alpha T(chi p-r_2w)>/R^2=Cpair_2.\nA vector in the right seven-step part instead gets the factor n.\nThe first table follows by taking the same old cross identity with\nE_top P. These arguments derive every table entry, without a new\nPSD relaxation or a sign assumption on an unlisted coordinate.\n\nThe signs are as follows. In blocks 0,2, Pval is positive for j<3\nand zero for j=3; U is positive only for j=2,3. In blocks 1,3,\nPval is nonpositive; U is nonnegative for j=0,1 and strictly negative\nfor j=2,3. Qval is positive in blocks 0,2 for j<3 and zero for j=3;\nit is negative in blocks 1,3 for j<3, and nonpositive for j=3.\nFor the latter sign, chi b_j<=chi R^2<1/600 and r_1 l_j>1 for j<3;\nMout_j>0. At chi=0 the endpoint entries vanish, consistently with\nthe same inequalities.\n\n## 16. Every forward cross row of the thirty-one-step parent\n\nFor any 0<=i,j<=15, (29) and the universal original-row identity (4)\ngive exactly\n\n    Q_(i,16+j)=barH_i-t_scale(barDelta_j+L_j)\n                                      +U_i Pval_j/R^2.          (40)\n\nIf the last product is nonpositive there is nothing to cancel:\nboth preceding terms are nonpositive. We check every possible\npositive product, retaining the zero-energy receivers separately.\n\nFirst suppose the receiver lies in block 0 or 2 and its atomic\nindex is j<3. Its energy is at least rho s_b E_j. The ratios of its\npositive Pval to its block scale are b_j and (Cpair_1/n)b_j, respectively.\nBoth are at most b_j. For sources in blocks 0,2 at atomic indices\n2,3, the mixed term divided by t_scale s_b E_j is at most,\nrespectively,\n\n    (Cpair_2/v_scale)Tfirst_1 < (1/3)(101/100)<1/2,\n    Tfirst_2 < 1/2.                                 (41)\n\nWe used b_j/E_j<=Dlen, and the endpoint source j=3 multiplies\nthe corresponding atomic-index-2 expression by rho. Since rho>99/100,\nthese products fit within the receiver energy. The other positive\nsources can only be atomic U samples in blocks 1,3. For block 1,\nthe sum of its height and the product is at most\nchi k m[-1+Cpair_2 Pval_j/R^2]<=0. For block 3 it is at most\nchi nk m[-1+Pval_j/R^2]<=0. Here Pval_j<=R^2. Receivers at atomic\nindex 3 in blocks 0,2 have Pval=0 and are already covered.\n\nNow the receiver is in block 1 or 3. A positive product requires\na source at atomic index 2 or 3 in block 1 or 3. For an atomic-index-2\nsource, put s_source=k and theta_source=Cpair_2 if its block is 1, and put\ns_source=nk and theta_source=1 if its block is 3. Let r be r_1 or r_2\naccording to that source block. Let theta_receiver=1 in receiver\nblock 1 and Cpair_1 in receiver block 3. The source height plus product,\nbefore its nonpositive paid deficit is subtracted, is exactly\n\n    s_source { chi(theta_source theta_receiver Lcoef_r-z)\n             +theta_source theta_receiver Lcoef_r b_j/R^2 }.   (42)\n\nHere Lcoef_r means Lcoef_1 or Lcoef_2. For an endpoint source this\nwhole expression is multiplied by rho. For atomic receiver j<3,\nthe non-chi part of (42), divided by t_scale s_b E_j, is at most\n\n    (Cpair_2/v_scale)Tsecond_1 < 2/5       (source block 1),\n    Tsecond_2 < 1/2                   (source block 3).          (43)\n\nFor receiver block 3 these ratios are further multiplied by\nCpair_1/n<4/5. Thus the energy rho s_b E_j pays for the non-chi part.\n\nThe chi part is nonpositive except possibly for source block 3\nand receiver block 1. In that remaining case it is at most\n\n    nk chi z/1000,\n\nby (34). The added receiver energy in (40) is\n\n    t_scale eps_lift = nk chi z (v_scale c_lift rho).\n\nIt is at least the required amount since\nv_scale c_lift rho>(99/100)(1/100)(99/100)>1/1000.\nThe same bound covers the endpoint source, whose coefficient is smaller.\nAt atomic receiver j=3, b_j=E_j=0, so this chi calculation alone\nproves the row. If chi=0 both expressions vanish. This exhausts\nall 256 forward cross rows, including every incoming terminal row.\n\n## 17. Every reverse cross row and the full original system\n\nThe other orientation is exactly\n\n    Q_(16+i,j)=t_scale(barH_i+L_i)-barDelta_j\n                                      +V_i Qval_j/R^2.         (44)\n\nThe height term is nonpositive. Products of opposite signs are\ntherefore already covered. Consider first a receiver in block 0 or 2\nwith atomic index j<3. A positive product requires a source in block\n0 or 2, whose V_i is at most m. Dividing the mixed term by the\nreceiver's s_b E_j, and using l_j/E_j<=rho, gives at most\n\n    Cpair_2 m sigma r_0 Mout_1 rho/R^4\n        < Cpair_2/3 < 3/4,                    receiver block 0,\n    m sigma r_0 Mout_2 rho/R^4\n        <= m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4<3/4,   receiver block 2.\n\nThese are smaller than rho, so barDelta_j>=rho s_b E_j suffices.\nTheir atomic endpoint receivers have Qval=barDelta=0 and leave\nonly the nonpositive height term.\n\nFor receivers in blocks 1,3, a positive product requires a source\nin blocks 1,3. Let s_source=k,nk and a_source=1,Cpair_1 in these two\nsource blocks. Its height satisfies\n\n    barH_i+L_i<=-(1-c_lift)chi s_source Patom_i.\n\nLet s_receiver=k,nk and theta_receiver=Cpair_2,1 in receiver blocks\n1,3, with r=r_1,r_2 and M_r=Mout_1,Mout_2 respectively. The height term\nplus mixed term in (44) is at most\n\n    chi Patom_i[-t_scale(1-c_lift)s_source\n           +a_source q_axis s_receiver theta_receiver M_r/R^2]\n      +a_source q_axis Patom_i s_receiver theta_receiver\n                                      (-chi b_j+r l_j)/R^2.    (45)\n\nThe coefficient in the first line is nonpositive. Indeed\na_source/s_source<=1/k, since Cpair_1/n<1. After division by s_source,\nthe positive term is at most Cpair_1 Cpair_2 for receiver block 1, and\nn Cpair_2 for receiver block 3. Both are at most n Cpair_2 because Cpair_1<n.\nOn the other hand,\n\n    t_scale(1-c_lift)=n v_scale(1-c_lift)>n Cpair_2\n\nby (32). The -chi b_j term in the second line is also nonpositive.\nFor atomic receiver j<3, its remaining term divided by s_receiver E_j\nis at most\n\n    q_axis m r_2 rho/R^2\n       =m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4<3/4<rho.\n\nThe receiving energy pays for it. At atomic index j=3 both b_j and\nl_j vanish, so the first-line estimate proves the row directly.\nThis exhausts all 256 reverse cross rows, including every outgoing\nterminal row.\n\nThe left child contributes 240 ordered within-child rows by sections\n2-8. The lifted right child contributes 240 by section 14 and scaling.\nThe two cross orientations contribute 512 by sections 16-17. The 64\noptimum incidences hold by (38) and (36): all physical heights are\nnonpositive and all energies are nonnegative. These are precisely\n1056 distinct ordered rows on {star,0,...,31}. No original gradient,\nqueried pair, final sample, or range condition has been removed.\n\n## 18. One global function, its minimizers, and exact normalization\n\nDefine ynew_i=Xnew_i-Gnew_i and\nDeltanew_i=Fnew_i-||Gnew_i||^2/2. On the same R^9 put\n\n    Hnew(v)=||v||^2/2\n               +max(0,max_(0<=i<=31)(<ynew_i,v>-Deltanew_i)),\n    F_31(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-Hnew(v)).                    (46)\n\nAll original rows have just been proved. Applying the constructive\ninterpolation proof of accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290` proves that\nF_31 is finite, convex, everywhere differentiable, and 1-smooth,\nwith F_31(Xnew_i)=Fnew_i, gradient Gnew_i, and minimum value zero.\nThis is one static function chosen before the run, not a sequence\nof changed functions.\n\nIf chi>0, the zero energies occur at left indices 3,7,11,15 and\nright indices 19,27. Their residuals are, respectively,\n\n    0, E_2 E_top t, 0, E_2 B_top t, 0, 0.\n\nEvery other energy is positive: in particular the newly lifted right\nendpoint energies at 23 and 31 are t_scale eps_lift and\nt_scale n eps_lift. (47)\n\nTo check this directly, for any fixed v and sufficiently small\nepsilon>0, the maximum of affine terms in Hnew(epsilon v) retains\nonly the zero-energy slopes. Its linear term is the support function\nof the hull in (47). Points in that hull have conjugate value zero.\nFor a point outside, its difference from the closest point of the\nhull is a strict separating direction; sufficiently small epsilon\nin that direction gives a positive conjugate value. This proves\nboth inclusions. At chi=0 all translations and increments vanish,\nand the same formula gives the singleton {0}.\n\nSection 9 proved that <Z_0,z><=0 for every\nz in conv{0,E_top t,B_top t}. The TRUE initial distance is exactly R. Neither\ninternal residual nor the new endpoint energy has been hidden in a\nsurrogate distance to a more remote minimizer.\n\nLet N_3=||Gnew_31||^2=t_scale N_2. Capacity squared\nhas not replaced B_31. Why copied values fail and what the new boundary actually costs\n\nThe extra values are essential for this concrete join when chi>0.\nIf L_i in (28) were zero, the actual queried row with source 15 and\nreceiver 23 would be\n\n    Q_(15,23)=chi n k rho(Lcoef_2-z)>0. This is not a separator of the released affine family and\nnot a claim that it was necessary to change the queried geometry.\n\nThe full new boundary, not just its scalar payoff, is\n\n    ynew_i=E_2 barY_i, Deltanew_i=barDelta_i,\n    Hnewheight_i=barH_i,                             0<=i<=15,\n    ynew_(16+j)=B_2 barY_j,\n    Deltanew_(16+j)=t_scale(barDelta_j+L_j),\n    Hnewheight_(16+j)=t_scale(barH_j+L_j),            0<=j<=15.\n                                                               (52)\n\nHere Hnewheight denotes Fnew-<Gnew,Xnew>+||Gnew||^2/2 and must not\nbe confused with the conjugate function Hnew in (46). In particular\nthe endpoint height is -(1-c_lift)tau N_3, its energy is\nc_lift tau N_3, and the earlier two paid deficits remain in the left\nchild and in the appropriately scaled right copy. Formula (53) is not\na feasibility assertion, and no actual63 construction or separator\nis claimed here.
