{"id": "2948d4cd3660413f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T09:57:19.019392+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4 round28 COMPLETE JOINT DRAFT: the full original first row forces a strictly positive, explicit lifted-cut gap when h0>sqrt2. Exact PSD coupling of the lifted-cut energy Erow and unit-quadratic energy Qenergy yields (A)--(C); on ab<=1,cd<=1 it gives the rational strict bound b<(a+3)(C-a-2)/((a+1)(C+a+2)). All20 signs retained, no rank/saturation restriction. No seed and no global no-seed theorem.", "evidence": "# A quantitative first-row gap for the full primitive certificate\n\nStatus: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4 round28,\nthe final round allocated by TASK.md and guidance8211d6128deabdf3,\nwith the corrected accounting instructions c9ebc26382c63e1d.\nNo fact_submit is authorized. The accepted mathematical input is\n04219554e41d9750, namely its original full five-gradient primitive\ncertificate identity. All other assertions used here are proved below.\n\nThe new obstruction couples a lifted middle-cut energy to the actual\nfirst row of the full PSD matrix and its unit-quadratic energy. It keeps\nall twenty nonnegative multipliers, all five formal gradients, and every\nflow equation. No rank, support, order, symmetry, or quadratic equality\nis assumed. It does not construct a threshold seed or prove that every\nthreshold seed is impossible.\n\n## 1. Statement and notation\n\nWrite a positive-excess four-step word as\n\n    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d),    a,b,c,d>0,\n    C=1+sum h=5+a+b+c+d.\n\nSuppose it admits the full primitive certificate of accepted fact\n04219554e41d9750. Suppose additionally that a>sqrt(2)-1. Define\n\n    u=1+a,             delta=u^2-2,\n    H=3+c+d,           eta=b/H,\n    R=a+3+1/a,         k=u*delta/a,\n    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,\n    Erow=R-k*eta-C^2*eta^2,\n    Qenergy=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*d^2,\n    Wrow=max(1+b,1+b*c*d),\n    Krow=max(u,a*Wrow).\n\nFull feasibility forces a<1, a*eta<1, nu>0, and\n0<=Qenergy<1. The following quantitative necessary condition holds:\n\n    u*delta <= Krow*Erow/nu + 2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).          (A)\n\nIn particular,\n\n    Erow >= nu * [u*delta /\n          (sqrt(Qenergy+Krow*u*delta)+sqrt(Qenergy))]^2 > 0.  (B)\n\nThus equality in the previously used lifted middle-cut condition Erow>=0\nis impossible throughout this initial-step regime. A version avoiding\nsquare roots is\n\n    Erow>=0,\n    [max(u*delta*nu-Krow*Erow,0)]^2 <= 4*Qenergy*Erow*nu.     (C)\n\nIf a*b<=1 and c*d<=1, the simpler strict necessary inequality is\n\n    Erow > nu*delta^2/u^2,                                 (D)\n\nequivalently\n\n    b < (a+3)*(C-a-2) / ((a+1)*(C+a+2)).                (E)\n\nThe last two product assumptions are explicit hypotheses of this\nsimplification. They are not silently declared lossless for the\noriginal problem. Conditions(A)--(C) do not require them. The separate\nassumption a,b,c,d>0 is also retained explicitly; no assertion about a\nzero step or a step at most one is being imported from an unverified\ncompactness draft.\n\n## 2. Original formal certificate and its constant kernel\n\nPut P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P_4=C-1, and let e be the five-entry all-ones\ncolumn. Write e_i for the i-th original standard coordinate of R^5,\nwith indices0 through4. Let T have diagonal1, entry h_t at(i,t) for t<i, and zero\nentries above the diagonal. The original twenty multipliers satisfy\n\n    Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, Lambda_ii=0,\n    L=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),\n    L^T e=omega,\n    M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0 on ALL of R^5.               (1)\n\nThe identity in accepted04219554e41d9750 is\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n                      =sum_(i,j) M_ij <g_i,g_j>,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)\n          +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2,\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i.                                (2)\n\nSince Te=e+P and sum_(i<4)h_i P_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2,\n\n    e^TDe=-s^2-sum h_i^2,\n    omega^TTe=s^2+sum h_i^2,\n    e^TMe=0.\n\nFor every real v, the nonnegative quadratic(e+t*v)^T M(e+t*v)\nhas zero constant coefficient. Considering both signs of t forces\nv^TMe=0, hence Me=0. The initial row of this identity is\n\n    sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j=2h0.                        (3)\n\nTo check(3) without any suppressed flow condition, direct expansion\ngives(Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2 for i<4. At i=0,\nP0=0 and(LP)_0=-sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j.\n\nLet Aout=sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j and m=M00. Its original diagonal is\n\n    m=1-a^2-a*Aout.                                    (4)\n\nIndeed the first column of T is(1,u,u,u,u)^T, so(LT)_00\n=(1-u)Aout=-a*Aout. Since m,Aout>=0 and a>0, (4) gives\na<=1. Equality would give m=Aout=0, contradicting(3). Thus a<1.\n\nFor any positive semidefinite real matrix, the two-vector inequality\n\n    |v^T M w|^2 <= (v^T M v)*(w^T M w)                 (5)\n\nfollows by applying nonnegativity to(v+t*w)^T M(v+t*w).\nIf w^T M w=0, the linear coefficient must vanish; otherwise minimizing\nin t proves(5). This argument does not assume positive definiteness.\n\n## 3. Two exact scalar energies\n\nHere clamp(x,[-eta,1]) means min(1,max(-eta,x)).\nThe scalar function with this derivative and minimum0\nhas the actual trajectory\n\n    x=(2+a,1,-b,-(2+d)*eta,-eta),\n    g=(1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta).\n\nHere b=(3+c+d)*eta. Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and\n1-Lipschitz. At every displayed sample its value is\nf_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2. The first step gives x1=1; the other three GD\nupdates follow directly from the displayed relation for b.\n\nFor any GD data with saturated values\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, telescoping the squared distances gives\n\n    V_h=||x0||^2-||x4+s*g4||^2.                        (6)\n\nIn detail, the first two sums in(2) reduce to\n2sum h_i<x_i,g_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2-2s*f4;\nthe first two terms telescope to||x0||^2-||x4||^2.\nSubstitution of f4 then gives(6). The scalar trajectory has x4=g4,\nso its energy is(2+a)^2-C^2*eta^2. By(1)--(2) it is nonnegative.\nConsequently\n\n    C*eta<=2+a,\n    a*eta<=a*(2+a)/C<3/5<1,                           (7)\n\nbecause 0<a<1 and C>5. This proves nu>0.\n\nNext define the actual unit-quadratic gradient column\n\n    p=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*d)^T.\n\nIt satisfies Tp=e. Since Le=0,\n\n    p^T M p=p^T D p=1-C^2*p4^2=Qenergy.               (8)\n\nFor the telescoping equality, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i gives\nh_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2; the terminal diagonal\nthen supplies-s(s+2)p4^2. Full PSD gives Qenergy>=0.\nAll a,b,c,d are positive, so Qenergy<1.\n\n## 4. Complete lifted-cut data and its exact multiplier cost\n\nLet U=(1,0), V=(0,1) be orthonormal physical vectors. Set\n\n    z=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V.\n\nThen\n\n    ||z||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a,\n    <U,z>+a||z||^2=1-a*eta.\n\nUse the five two-dimensional data\n\n    X=(U+u*z, U, -b*U, -(2+d)*eta*U, -eta*U),\n    G=(z, U, -eta*U, -eta*U, -eta*U),\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (9)\n\nEvery GD update has the original step length. For these saturated\nvalues the original ordered interpolation residual is exactly\n\n    Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>.\n\nHere is a check of all twenty ordered finite pairs, grouped by their\ntarget j. Targets1 and4 have zero normal. At target0 the normal is\nU+a*z. The incoming pair(1,0) has residual0, whereas each incoming\npair(i,0), i=2,3,4, has residual-a*nu. At target2 the normal is\n-(b-eta)U. The only nonzero finite residual there is\nQ_12=-(1+eta)(b-eta). At target3 the normal is\n-(1+d)*eta*U. The only nonzero finite residual there is\nQ_13=-(1+eta)(1+d)*eta. Thus all twenty residuals are nonpositive.\nThe zero optimum jet can also be added: Q_(i,star)=0 by the chosen\nvalues; the opposite inequalities follow from the same normals and\na*eta<1. These optimum pairs are not additional multipliers in(1).\n\nFor completeness these are actual jets of one global smooth convex\nfunction. Let Kphys=conv{0,z,U,-eta*U} and define\n\n    F(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).\n\nThe comparisons just made say that X_i-G_i is an outward normal\nto Kphys at G_i. Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer\nas the Euclidean projection of X onto Kphys. If projections at X,Y\nare G,H, adding their normal inequalities gives\n||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>, and hence ||G-H||<=||X-Y||.\nTo see differentiability directly, optimality at X and X+v bounds\nF(X+v)-F(X)-<G,v> between0 and<G(X+v)-G,v>, which is at most||v||^2.\nThus the gradient is the projection and is1-Lipschitz. The function\nis convex as a maximum of affine functions of X; it has minimum0 at0\nbecause0 belongs to Kphys. It realizes all of(9).\n\nBy(6), its primitive energy is\n\n    ||U+u*z||^2-C^2*eta^2\n      =a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2=Erow.            (10)\n\nLet\n\n    Bin=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40.\n\nIn the U coordinate the gradient column is-eta*e+(1+eta)*e1;\nin the V coordinate it is sqrt(nu)*e0. The constant kernel proved\nin Section2 therefore gives the full matrix remainder\n(1+eta)^2*M11+nu*m. Substituting every nonzero residual above into\nthe original identity(2) yields the exact accounting identity\n\n    Erow=nu*(m+a*Bin)+(1+eta)^2*M11\n       +(1+eta)*eta*((2+c+d)*Lambda12+(1+d)*Lambda13). (11)\n\nEvery term on the right is nonnegative. In particular\n\n    0<=m<=m+a*Bin<=Erow/nu.                              (12)\n\nNo incoming coefficient has been set to zero. Identity(11) prices the\noriginal incoming coefficients explicitly.\n\n## 5. The original first row couples the two energies\n\nDefine the weighted incoming combination\n\n    Bweight=(1+b)*Lambda20+(1-b*c)*Lambda30\n                                    +(1+b*c*d)*Lambda40.\n\nThe exact first-row identity is\n\n    2*e0^T M p = u*(m-delta)+a^2*Bweight.             (13)\n\nHere e0 denotes the original first coordinate column of R5. A direct\nderivation avoids any coordinate reduction. Since Tp=e and Le=0,\nMp=Dp+T^T L^T p/2. Also Te0=u*e-a*e0, giving\n\n    2*e0^T M p=2(1-a^2)-a*p^T L e0.\n\nThe flow at node0 gives Lambda10=Aout+2u-Bin. Hence\n\n    p^T L e0\n      =Aout+a*Lambda10-a*b*Lambda20\n                        +a*b*c*Lambda30-a*b*c*d*Lambda40\n      =u*Aout+2a*u-a*Bweight.\n\nFinally substitute a*Aout=1-a^2-m from(4). The constant term becomes\n\n    (2-u)(1-a^2)-2a^2*u\n      =u*((1-a)^2-2a^2)=-u*delta,\n\nwhich proves(13), with all row and flow contributions retained.\n\nAll three incoming multipliers are nonnegative. Since b,c,d>0,\neach coefficient in Bweight is at most Wrow. This remains true even\nif 1-b*c is negative. Thus Bweight<=Wrow*Bin. Applying(5) with e0,p,\nand using(8), gives from(13)\n\n    u*delta <= u*m+a^2*Wrow*Bin+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)\n             <= Krow*(m+a*Bin)+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)\n             <= Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).        (14)\n\nThis is(A). Put t=sqrt(Erow/nu). Because Krow,u,delta are positive,\n(14) says\n\n    Krow*t^2+2*sqrt(Qenergy)*t-u*delta>=0.\n\nSolving this increasing quadratic for t>=0 and rationalizing its\npositive root gives(B). Its numerator is strictly positive, so Erow>0.\nMultiplying(A) by nu and squaring only when its left side after\nsubtraction is positive gives exactly(C). There is no sign-losing\nsquaring in this formulation.\n\n## 6. A rational strengthened middle-cut boundary\n\nIf c*d<=1, then Wrow=1+b. If also a*b<=1, then\na*Wrow=a+a*b<=1+a=u, so Krow=u. Since Qenergy<1 and Erow>0,\n(14) implies the strict inequality\n\n    u*delta < u*t^2+2t,       t=sqrt(Erow/nu)>0.\n\nThe right side is strictly increasing for t>=0. Its value at\nt=delta/u is\n\n    delta^2/u+2delta/u=delta*(delta+2)/u=u*delta,\n\nbecause delta+2=u^2. It follows that t>delta/u, which is(D).\nThe exact algebraic identity\n\n    Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2\n      =[(a+3-delta*eta)^2-u^2*C^2*eta^2]/u^2          (15)\n\ncan be checked by expanding the constant, linear, and quadratic\ncoefficients in eta. Those coefficients in the numerator are\n(a+3)^2, -2(a+3)*delta, and delta^2-u^2*C^2.\n\nThe first factor a+3-delta*eta is positive: by a*eta<1,\n\n    a+3-delta*eta > a+3-delta/a = 1+1/a >0.\n\nThus(D) and(15) give\n\n    a+3 > (u*C+delta)*eta.\n\nSubstitute eta=b/(C-2-a-b), whose denominator is H>0. Since\ndelta+a+3=(a+1)(a+2), the result is exactly(E). Every denominator\nin(E) is positive.\n\nIn particular this is a necessary bound on an entire step region,\nnot a condition on a sparse coefficient family. The exact stronger\ncondition(A) still retains the actual Qenergy rather than discarding\nit in favor of the upper bound1.\n\n## 7. Adversarial scope checks\n\nThe initial-step hypothesis is substantive. At a=sqrt(2)-1 the\nquantity delta is zero, and(A) supplies no positive gap. This agrees\nwith the possibility of saturated basic certificates at that endpoint.\nWe do not extend the strict conclusion by continuity across delta=0.\n\nIf b*c>1, the coefficient of Lambda30 in(13) is negative. The proof\ndoes not replace this coefficient by its absolute value or infer a\nfalse lower bound. It uses only the valid upper bound Bweight<=Wrow*Bin.\nThus(A)--(C) still apply in that regime. The simplified statement(E)\nrequires only its explicitly stated a*b and c*d hypotheses.\n\nIf Qenergy=0, formula(B) reduces to Erow>=nu*u*delta/Krow;\nno division by Qenergy occurs. If a multiplier vanishes or M has\nadditional kernels, the semidefinite form of(5) still applies. No\nstrict complementarity, full rank, or strictly positive support was used.\n\nHere is a purely symbolic test family showing why the separate tests\nErow>=0 and Qenergy>=0 cannot justify the omitted coupling. It is an\nanalytical counterexample to that shortcut, not a numerical seed proposal\nor a claim to survive the other existing necessary inequalities. Take\n\n    a=1/2, eta=7/24, C=39*sqrt(2)/7,\n    H=24*(C-5/2)/31, b=7*(C-5/2)/31, Ktail=H-3,\n    0<d<min(Ktail/2,1/Ktail,1/(C*a*b*Ktail)), c=Ktail-d.\n\nThe elementary bounds 7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7 give\n193/25<39/5<C<8. Thus Ktail>0, all four excesses are positive,\na*b<1, c*d<1, and 0<C*a*b*c*d<1. Also C=5+a+b+c+d\nand eta=b/(3+c+d). Direct exact substitution gives\n\n    R-k*eta=169/32=C^2*eta^2,\n    Erow=0, Qenergy>0,\n    nu=1271/576, delta=1/4, u=3/2.\n\nYet(D) requires Erow>1271/20736. Hence none of these words has the\nfull certificate. This illustrates the active original-row obstruction;\nit is not asserted to be a newly uncovered region beyond every prior\nobstruction. No finite numerical values were evaluated for this family.\n\n## 8. Discovery provenance and remaining problem\n\nThe first half of the allocated two-round batch was already recorded\nin339cfa9a90f403ec and the corrected accounting handoff b94ad81b2b227c34.\nAt the start of this round the owner ledger had37 charged cases,\n32 discovery cases, and30.953246246000163 accounted seconds.\n\nThis round made24 additional pre-registered discovery requests in\npublic batch3 and batch4. Every case was evaluated inside the main-owned\nMeter and launched through run_seed_with_remaining_budget.py with its\ncumulative hard timeout. Batch3 added the original C83 quadratic bound\nto endpoint selection. For fixed C,a,b, put\n\n    Qprefix=3+a+b,\n    j0=(Qprefix^2+1/(a^2*b))/(1+b),\n    j1=(1/(a^2*b)-b*Qprefix^2)/(1+b),\n    t83=2*j0/(sqrt(j1^2+4*C^2*j0)-j1).\n\nOn b*Qprefix<C the scalar cut bounds c/(2+d)<1/b, so the previously\nderived repaired-cut condition C^2*t^2<=j0+j1*t becomes t<=t83.\nWith Ktail=c+d this is the endpoint lower bound\nd>=(Ktail-2*t83)/(1+t83). Batch4 also selected b as a registered\nfraction of the explicit mixed-cut/first-triangle upper bounds. This\nwas one candidate construction per charged callback, not an inner\nsweep. The source and request hashes, original statuses, and endpoint\ndiagnostics are retained publicly. These recalled screening formulas\nare not mathematical premises of Sections1--7.\n\nAll24 requests were prefiltered:21 had empty endpoint components and\n3 failed the scalar conditions independent of the last endpoint. There\nwas no new conic solve. The final allocation readback was\n\n    charged_cases_used=61 of64,\n    discovery_cases_used=56 of56,\n    measured_seconds_used=31.476150974999655,\n    external_timeout_seconds_charged=11.999997916000211,\n    accounted_seconds_used=43.476148890999866 of60.\n\nThe whole two-round allocation therefore retains20 returned conic solves\n(18 optimal,2 optimal_inaccurate),40 prefiltered requests, and the one\nconservatively charged startup timeout. Raw inaccurate statuses have not\nbeen relabeled. Three reserved non-discovery cases remain unused; the\ndiscovery quota is exhausted and does not renew. {"id": "01b16d23d5b66ba2", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T10:02:07.647377+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "NEW full-cone joint DRAFT: at EVERY capacity C>=193/25, both orientations of the boundary block h0 in[139/100,141/100],h1 in[179/100,181/100] are impossible, with the other two steps ARBITRARY NONNEGATIVE. This is two actual exclusion regions, NOT general reversal or a seed impossibility theorem.", "evidence": "# Two orientations of a boundary block excluded from the full primitive cone\n\nStatus: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No numerical evaluation, solver, word scan, machine\nalgebra, replay, verifier, installation, or subordinate agent is used.\n\nAccepted inputs: fact04219554e41d9750, Section10, for the full primitive\ncertificate identity; fact1252b8823bb5a290 for the interpolation convention\nand its explicit realizing function. Every other mathematical ingredient\nused below is proved in this document. Earlier draft d3abee1b17da52da\nmotivates the prefix construction but is not used as a fact.\n\n## 1. Statement and exact scope\n\nFor a nonnegative four-step word h=(h0,h1,h2,h3), write\n\n s=h0+h1+h2+h3, C=1+s.\n\nRetain all twenty ordered multipliers Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j in {0,...,4},\nLambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-one column, T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i,\nT_it=0 for t>i, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, and\n\n omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n Dprim=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)).\n\nThe full ordinary primitive cone requires\n\n L^T e=omega, M=Dprim+sym(LT)>=0 on ALL of R^5.          (1)\n\nHere sym(A)=(A+A^T)/2. No formal gradient is identified with another,\neven if a physical step is zero.\n\n**Joint draft result.** No certificate (1) exists in either of these\ntwo sets:\n\n (P) C>=193/25, h0 in[139/100,141/100],\n     h1 in[179/100,181/100], h2,h3 arbitrary nonnegative;\n\n (R) C>=193/25, h2 in[179/100,181/100],\n     h3 in[139/100,141/100], h0,h1 arbitrary nonnegative.\n\nFor each word the proof supplies an explicit legal negative-energy\ntrajectory, selected by stated inequalities. Thus both orientations of\nevery word in (P) are excluded from the original full certificate cone.\nThere is no upper cap on C and no assumption that the two free steps\nexceed one. This strictly expands the hypotheses covered in the old\nprefix-prism draft, which required C<87/10 and both free steps>1.\n\nThis is a direct proof for two reversed sets. It is NOT a certificate\nmap, an existential reversal theorem for the entire four-step cone, a\ncounterexample to such a theorem, or a resolution of the original\nunrestricted asymptotic target. In particular it does not permit imposing\nan ordering or symmetry on schedules outside these sets.\n\n## 2. Legal data and full-cone soundness\n\nUse normalized smoothness1. On formal GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i define\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2 sum_(i<4) h_i(f_i-f_4)\n       +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_4||^2.\n\nFact04219554e41d9750 gives the full identity\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n                         =sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>.       (2)\n\nIf all sampled data come from one convex1-smooth function, every Q_ij\nis nonpositive by fact1252b8823bb5a290. Hence (1) implies V_h>=0 on\nevery such trajectory. A single legal trajectory with V_h<0 excludes\nall possible arrays in (1), regardless of their support or rank.\n\nAll constructions below use saturated values\n\n f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.                             (3)\n\nTelescoping the GD updates gives, for these values,\n\n V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_4+s g_4||^2.                        (4)\n\nIndeed 2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n=||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2; the remaining terminal terms complete the\nsquare in (4). In particular, if x_4=g_4 then\n\n V_h=||x_0||^2-C^2||g_4||^2.                           (5)\n\nFor values (3), all interpolation residuals simplify to\n\n Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>.                               (6)\n\nInclude the optimum jet x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Whenever (6) is\nnonpositive for every ordered finite and optimum pair, an explicit\nrealizing function is F=H^*, the convex conjugate of\n\n H(v)=max_j{<x_j,g_j>-f_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2},   (7)\n\nwith j running over all samples and the optimum. At g_i the j-th\nquadratic minus the i-th is Q_ij<=0, and x_i is a subgradient of H\nthere. The function H is1-strongly convex; its conjugate is everywhere\nfinite, convex, differentiable and1-smooth, and interpolates the jets.\nThe optimum jet gives min F=F(0)=0. This is the construction in accepted\nfact1252b8823bb5a290; checking (6) below checks every original pair.\n\nWrite\n\n h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), C=5+a+b+c+d,                     (8)\n\nwhere a,b,c,d>=-1. Positive shifts will only be assumed where explicitly\nproved or specified.\n\n## 3. Endpoint and quadratic witnesses\n\nIf a>0, let Htail=1+h1+h2+h3=C-1-a and eta=a/Htail.\nUse the scalar function obtained by integrating the derivative\nclip(x,[-eta,1]) from0, and start at x0=1. The first step reaches-a.\nAll subsequent gradients equal-eta and the terminal point equals-eta:\nat every suffix query its position is-eta times one plus the sum of\nthe remaining suffix steps. Thus all these positions are on the lower\nplateau, including any repeated positions from zero steps. Its energy is\n\n V_h=1-C^2 a^2/Htail^2.\n\nConsequently full feasibility implies\n\n a<=(C-1)/(C+1)<1 when a>0.                            (9)\n\nFor a<=0 the bound a<1 holds directly. The upper endpoint is obtained\nindependently: if d>0, start at C-1-d with derivative clip(x,[-d,1]).\nAll four queried gradients before the last update equal1 and the final\npoint and gradient equal-d. Formula (5) gives\n\n d<=(C-1)/(C+1)<1 when d>0; thus always d<1.            (10)\n\nIf an endpoint inequality fails, the displayed scalar function itself\nhas strictly negative V_h.\n\nThe actual unit quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, started at1, has terminal gradient\nabcd and saturated values. Hence\n\n V_h=1-C^2 a^2 b^2 c^2 d^2,\n                    C|abcd|<=1                       (11)\n\nis necessary for the full cone. No sign assumption on the shifts is\nneeded for (11).\n\n## 4. The optimum comparisons follow because0\nis in the triangle. No numerical evaluation,\nsolver, scan, machine algebra, replay, verifier, installation or subagent.\n\nAccepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11 (the full\nprimitive matrix and primitive join identities), and fact1252b8823bb5a290\n(the interpolation convention). All additional calculations and scalar\nwitnesses are proved below. Earlier drafts4a064f93111ac5a2,\ne59fe188876aa98d and2ff1d137e4ae5a15 are awareness, not proof premises.\n\n## 1. Exact scope\n\nFor three strictly positive steps h=(h0,h1,h2), set s=sum h_i and C=1+s.\nLet lambda be a full signed primitive certificate: every value coefficient\nin V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij vanishes and its remainder is PSD on all four\nindependent gradients. All nonterminal-targeting entries are nonnegative.\nFor a specified 0<=theta<1, allow only\n\n                   lambda_(i,3)>=-theta h_i, i<3.      (1)\n\nOrdinary certificates are the special case theta=0. Definitions of Q,V\nand every matrix appear below.\n\nThe first result is an explicit full reversal map with only SIX sign\ntests on the supplied array. Six other off-diagonal signs are automatic.\nThe tests are necessary and sufficient for THIS canonical output to be\nordinary. They do not characterize existence of a different output\ncertificate or prove that the input can always be re-selected to pass.\n\nThe second result applies this map to a full four-step certificate with\na saturated first or last cut and a strictly positive three-step child.\nIf the extracted child's six tests hold, the parent can be reversed with\nall twenty signs, exact flow, and full five-gradient PSD. Furthermore\nthat conditional class has parent capacity strictly below193/25.\nThus any threshold certificate with such a saturated endpoint cut must\nfail at least one of the six tests for EVERY supplied full parent array.\n\nThis leaves endpoint cuts whose extracted arrays fail a test, zero-step\nboundary cases outside the positive-child hypothesis, and all unsaturated\nfour-step words unresolved. The original exact-profile target remains open.\n\n## 2. Grounding and the unsigned full-space identity\n\nFor arbitrary formal data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i use\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)\n       +sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_3||^2.\n\nLet e=(1,1,1,1)^T, e3=(0,0,0,1)^T, hbar=(h0,h1,h2,0)^T,\nw=(h0,h1,h2,1)^T, and T have diagonal1 and T_it=h_t for t<i,\nwith zero entries above its diagonal. Put p=T e. The row Laplacian L\nhas L_ij=-lambda_ij for i!=j and diagonal equal to the outgoing sum.\nValue cancellation and direct expansion give\n\n L^T e=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,2s)^T=omega,\n M=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),-s(s+2))+sym(LT).\n\nThe full remainder M is PSD by hypothesis. Constant-gradient affine\ndata have V_h=Q_ij=0, whence e^T M e=0 and PSD gives Me=0. This\nkernel assertion can be checked by inserting e+t z for arbitrary z\nand both signs of t; it is not a quotient imposed on the certificate.\n\nDefine\n\n K=L+diag(hbar)-hbar e3^T+diag(w), G=K^(-1).\n\nIts exact equations are\n\n M=sym(KT)-C^2 e3 e3^T,\n K e=w, K^T e=C e3, Kp=-Cw+2C^2 e3.                  (2)\n\nFor the first equation, sym((K-L)T)\n=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),1). The last equation follows from\nMe=0 and T^T e3=w. These also check every affine and value condition.\n\nAll off-diagonal K entries are nonpositive. At the terminal column\nthey equal-lambda_(i,3)-h_i<=-(1-theta)h_i<0, and its row surpluses\nare w_i>0. Strict row diagonal dominance proves invertibility: a\ncoordinate of maximum absolute value in a putative nonzero kernel\nvector gives a contradiction. Equations (2) imply\n\n G w=e, G e3=(p+Ce)/(2C^2), e3^T G=e^T/C.            (3)\n\nLet Delta_ij=1_(j=3-i)-1_(j=2-i), with out-of-range indicators zero.\nIt is symmetric and invertible. Its rows are\n(e3-e2)^T,(e2-e1)^T,(e1-e0)^T,e0^T. For hrev=(h2,h1,h0), define\nTrev,wrev in the same way as T,w. Direct row subtraction gives\n\n Delta e=e3, Delta p=wrev, Delta Trev=T^T Delta.\n\nSet\n\n F=Delta G Delta,\n Krev=4C^2 F-(wrev+2C e3)e3^T,\n lambda_rev,ij=-4C^2 F_ij for i!=j.                    (4)\n\nEquations (3) imply Krev e=wrev and Krev^T e=C e3. The last-column\ncorrection in (4) is exactly the grounding term-hrev_i; all other\noff-diagonals are-lambda_rev,ij. Thus (4) has every required reversed\nvalue flow, even if some of its coefficients are signed.\n\nIts entire quadratic remainder satisfies\n\n                  Mrev=4C^2 Delta G M G^T Delta.       (5)\n\nTo verify (5), insert (2) on the right. Its non-boundary part is\n4C^2 sym(Delta G T^T Delta). Its boundary term is\n-(wrev+C e3)(wrev+C e3)^T, by (3). Inserting Krev into\nsym(Krev Trev)-C^2 e3e3^T gives the same two terms, since\nTrev^T e3=wrev. This proves (5) on the full original reversed space.\nOnly the signs in (4) remain to be checked.\n\n## 3. The leading matrix and all nine nonterminal-targeting entries\n\nFor i,j<3,i!=j write x_ij=lambda_ij. Put\n\n r_i=2h_i+lambda_(i,3)>0,\n t_i=lambda_(3,i)>=0,\n A=K restricted to rows and columns0,1,2,\n k_i=A_ii, d_A=det A.\n\nThe row and column sums of A give\n\n k0=x01+x02+r0=x10+x20+t0,\n k1=x10+x12+r1=x01+x21+t1,\n k2=x20+x21+r2=x02+x12+t2.                            (6)\n\nThe off-diagonal entries are-x_ij<=0 and the row surpluses r_i are\nstrictly positive. Thus A is invertible by diagonal dominance. Also\nd_A>0: multiply every off-diagonal by a parameter in[0,1]; every such\nmatrix is strictly row diagonally dominant, so its determinant never\nvanishes and has the positive sign of the diagonal matrix at parameter0.\n\nEvery column difference G(e_i-e_j) has last coordinate0 by (3).\nIts first three coordinates are obtained by solving with A. Expanding\nthe nine cofactors of A gives the following COMPLETE table:\n\n d_A F01=-k0 t1-x01 t0,\n d_A F02= x20 t1-x21 t0,\n d_A F10=-k0 r1-x10 r0,\n d_A F12=-t0 r2-x20(t0+t1+t2),\n d_A F20= x02 r1-x12 r0,\n d_A F21=-r0 t2-x02(r0+r1+r2),\n d_A F30=-x01 x12-k1 x02,\n d_A F31= x02 t1-x01 t2,\n d_A F32=-k2 t1-x21 t2.                              (7)\n\nFor a full check, the three rows of d_A A^(-1) are\n\n (k1 k2-x12 x21, x01 k2+x02 x21, x01 x12+k1 x02),\n (x10 k2+x12 x20, k0 k2-x02 x20, k0 x12+x02 x10),\n (x10 x21+k1 x20, k0 x21+x01 x20, k0 k1-x01 x10).\n\nFor example, F02=(A^(-1))20-(A^(-1))21; substituting\nk0-x10=x20+t0 and k1-x01=x21+t1 yields its entry in (7).\nThe other entries follow by the row differences in Delta, using the\nlisted cofactors and (6); the terminal row is the first row of G.\nThis specifies the entire calculation, including every ordered sign.\n\nSix entries in (7) are automatically nonpositive. The other three\nare nonpositive exactly when\n\n x20 t1<=x21 t0,\n x02 r1<=x12 r0,\n x02 t1<=x01 t2.                                     (8)\n\nThese are products of actual directed coefficients and the actual\nrow surpluses. Full PSD has not been substituted for any of (8).\n\n## 4. The three terminal signs\n\nLet hcol=(h0,h1,h2)^T and e0small=(1,0,0)^T, and define\n\n                  z=A^(-1)(e0small-hcol/C).           (9)\n\nColumn0 of G has last entry1/C. Its top block solves\nA G_top,0=e0small+(lambda_(i,3)+h_i)_i/C.\nSince the vector in the latter parentheses is r-hcol and A^(-1)r\nis the all-one three-vector, the top block is1/C+z. Therefore\n\n F03=-z2, F13=z2-z1, F23=z1-z0.                       (10)\n\nThese are nonpositive exactly when\n\n                       z0>=z1>=z2>=0.                (11)\n\nEquations (8) and (11) are the promised SIX tests. Together they check\nall twelve entries in (4); flow and full PSD follow from (2)--(5).\nThus they give an explicit correctly delimited reversal map for full\nordinary or signed-terminal three-step certificates satisfying (1).\nIf a test fails, only this supplied canonical output is rejected.\n\n## 5. A scalar capacity bound when both orientations pass the core-ended tests\n\nThis section requires only nonnegative intrinsic energy on specific\nlegal trajectories whose incoming-terminal residuals Q_(i,3) are all0.\nIt therefore applies to a signed certificate satisfying (1): every\nnegative coefficient multiplies0, while all other Q have nonnegative\nmultipliers in the certificate. It applies in particular to an ordinary\ncertificate. No general signed-terminal repair is assumed.\n\nWrite the three physical steps as (u,v,w), total capacity T=1+u+v+w,\nt=sqrt(2), ell=1+t, and T0=ell^2=3+2t. Suppose both (u,v,w) and\n(w,v,u) are nonnegative on the core-ended trajectories below.\nThen\n\n                         T<=T0.                      (12)\n\nHere is a complete proof. At a chosen step b>1 with sums P before\nand R after it, use the scalar function with derivative\nclip(x,[-(b-1)/(R+1),1]), starting at P+1. It has constant gradient1\nbefore the chosen step and constant gradient-(b-1)/(R+1) after it,\nending at a point equal to that gradient. All values are xg-g^2/2,\nand every incoming-terminal Q is0. Its energy is\n(P+1)^2-T^2(b-1)^2/(R+1)^2. Thus\n\n T(b-1)_+<=(P+1)(R+1),\n u,w<=m(T):=2T/(T+1)<2.                              (13)\n\nAssume T>=T0. First v>1: otherwise T<=2+2m(T), so\nT^2-5T-2<=0. At T0 this polynomial equals2t>0 and its derivative\nis positive thereafter, a contradiction.\n\nPut A0=u+1, D0=w+1, r=v-1>0. The middle cut gives\n\n Tr<=A0 D0, T=A0+D0+r.\n\nSince A0 D0<=(A0+D0)^2/4, solving the positive quadratic gives\n\n T<=ell(A0+D0)/2<=ell(m(T)+1).\n\nConsequently T^2+(1-3ell)T-ell<=0. This polynomial is increasing\nfor T>=T0, and at7 it is56-22ell>0 because ell<5/2. Thus T<7,\nand u,w<7/4. Both endpoints exceed1. For if u<=1, then A0<=2,\nD0<11/4, r>T-19/4, and\n\n Tr>T(T-19/4)>=T0(T0-19/4)=11/4+5t/2>11/2>A0D0,\n\ncontradicting the middle cut. The same argument applies to w.\nAlso\n\n u+w>=2T/ell-2>=2t. Exact scope\n\nFor three strictly positive steps h=(h0,h1,h2), set s=sum h_i and C=1+s.\nLet lambda be a full signed primitive certificate: every value coefficient\nin V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij vanishes and its remainder is PSD on all four\nindependent gradients. All nonterminal-targeting entries are nonnegative.\nFor a specified 0<=theta<1, allow only\n\n                   lambda_(i,3)>=-theta h_i, i<3. Definitions of Q,V\nand every matrix appear below.\n\nThe first result is an explicit full reversal map with only SIX sign\ntests on the supplied array. Six other off-diagonal signs are automatic.\nThe tests are necessary and sufficient for THIS canonical output to be\nordinary. They do not characterize existence of a different output\ncertificate or prove that the input can always be re-selected to pass.\n\nThe second result applies this map to a full four-step certificate with\na saturated first or last cut and a strictly positive three-step child.\nIf the extracted child's six tests hold, the parent can be reversed with\nall twenty signs, exact flow, and full five-gradient PSD. Furthermore\nthat conditional class has parent capacity strictly below193/25.\nThus any threshold certificate with such a saturated endpoint cut must\nfail at least one of the six tests for EVERY supplied full parent array.\n\nThis leaves endpoint cuts whose extracted arrays fail a test and all\nunsaturated four-step words unresolved. Section8 separately excludes\nevery zero-step parent at the threshold, so the positive-child hypothesis\nomits no threshold endpoint boundary. The general map is still stated\nonly for a positive child. The original exact-profile target remains open.\n\n## 2. Grounding and the unsigned full-space identity\n\nFor arbitrary formal data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i use\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)\n       +sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_3||^2.\n\nLet e=(1,1,1,1)^T, e3=(0,0,0,1)^T, hbar=(h0,h1,h2,0)^T,\nw=(h0,h1,h2,1)^T, and T have diagonal1 and T_it=h_t for t<i,\nwith zero entries above its diagonal. Put p=T e. The row Laplacian L\nhas L_ij=-lambda_ij for i!=j and diagonal equal to the outgoing sum.\nValue cancellation and direct expansion give\n\n L^T e=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,2s)^T=omega,\n M=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),-s(s+2))+sym(LT).\n\nThe full remainder M is PSD by hypothesis. This proves (5) on the full original reversed space.\nOnly the signs in (4) remain to be checked.\n\n## 3. At T=T0,\nm(T0)=1+t/2, the corner values are\n\n q_T0(t,t)=1,\n q_T0(t,m(T0))=q_T0(m(T0),t)=ell/2>1,\n q_T0(m(T0),m(T0))=ell/2>1.\n\nFor T>=T0 every corner value strictly increases: each positive factor\nincreases, and the derivatives of T-t-m(T)-2 and T-2m(T)-2 are\nrespectively1-2/(T+1)^2 and1-4/(T+1)^2, both positive.\nThus (16) fails for every T>T0. Put Cpar=1+D+r, kappa=(Cpar/D)^2=1/r^2.\n\nThe four-step word is hpar=(1+r,b0,b1,b2). Suppose it has a full\nordinary twenty-multiplier certificate Lambda. Direct\nsubstitution gives Q_(0,j)=-(1+1/Cpar)R_j/Cpar<0. Complementarity\nwith Lambda>=0 and the zero energy therefore forces\nLambda_(0,j)=0 for j=1,2,3. Using rCpar=D gives\n\n Lambda_(0,4)=r(kappa-1),\n Lambda_(j,0)=(kappa-1)b_(j-1), j=1,2,3,\n Lambda_(4,0)=kappa-1.                               (17)\n\nEvery cross coefficient is now fixed. The full primitive join identity\nin accepted fact04219554e41d9750, applied as an algebraic identity,\nshows that the extracted three-step array is\n\n lambda_ij=Lambda_(i+1,j+1)/kappa\n                 -theta b_i 1_(i<3,j=3), i!=j,       (18)\n\nand the parent remainder equals kappa times its full four-gradient\nremainder, with a zero first row and column. Therefore (18) cancels\nall its values, has full PSD, and satisfies the signed bound (1).\nNo claim that every entry of (18) is nonnegative is made.\n\nIf (18) passes tests (8),(11), Sections2--4 produce an ordinary full\ncertificate for rev(b). Join rev(b) to the empty right child with the\nsame bridge1+r. The accepted join identity has only nonnegative added\nmultipliers and preserves the full child remainder, giving the actual\nreversed parent (rev(b),1+r) on all five gradients.\n\nMoreover the original signed b certificate obeys every core-ended\ntest in Section5, and rev(b) is ordinary. Hence D<=3+2sqrt(2).\nAs Cpar=Omega(1,D), this bounds the parent strictly below193/25.\nFor a direct rational check of the threshold separation, if instead\nCpar>=193/25 then\n\n D=Cpar(Cpar-1)/(Cpar+1)>=16212/2725>41/7>3+2sqrt(2).\n\nThe first function is strictly increasing for Cpar>=1; the middle\ncomparison has positive numerator1759 on multiplying by7*2725.\nThe last uses sqrt(2)<10/7. This contradicts the child bound.\n\nThus at a saturated first cut, EVERY threshold full parent array must\nhave an extracted signed child violating at least one of the six tests.\nThis is a necessary coefficient obstruction, not proof that no such\narray exists.\n\n## 7. Saturated LAST cuts and exact limitations\n\nFor hpar=(b0,b1,b2,1+r) with the same positive b and rCpar=D,\nthe saturated clipped trajectory and its constant-gradient counterpart\nlikewise put the two block indicators in the full kernel. Complementarity\nforces tail-to-prefix edges only into the bridge query and prefix-to-tail\nedges only from it. The same row calculation as in Section6, with the\nempty right child of capacity1, gives the cross coefficients of the\naccepted primitive join. Subtracting them leaves the ordinary full\ncertificate of b on its original four gradients; there is no terminal\ncredit because b is the left child.\n\nFor explicit cross entries, put kappa_last=Cpar^2, let index3 be the\nbridge query and index4 the empty tail query. The only cross terms are\n\n Lambda_(3,4)=r(kappa_last-1),\n Lambda_(4,3)=kappa_last-1;\n\nall Lambda_(i,4),Lambda_(4,i) with i<3 vanish. Indeed complementarity\nfirst gives Lambda_(4,i)=0 for i<3; the row i<3 against the final-block\nindicator is-Lambda_(i,4)/2, forcing the other direction to vanish.\nNode4 flow gives Lambda_(4,3)-Lambda_(3,4)=2(Cpar-1), and the\nkernel equation gives Lambda_(3,4)=r Lambda_(4,3). The displayed\nsolution follows from (1-r)(Cpar+1)=2, which is equivalent to\nrCpar=D and Cpar=1+D+r. The retained prefix array is ordinary by\nnonnegativity, and its full matrix is the corresponding PSD block.\n\nIf that child passes (8),(11) with theta=0, its reverse is ordinary,\nand the accepted join with the empty left child reverses the parent.\nSection5 again gives D<=3+2sqrt(2), so this class also has capacity\nbelow193/25. Therefore every threshold full parent on this face must\nfail a test in EVERY supplied extracted array.\n\nThe six tests do not have a proved universal selection theorem. In\nparticular, (8) asks for genuine comparisons of directed coefficient\nproducts; they do not follow merely from nonnegative entries, value\nflow, or the displayed congruence. The terminal conditions (11) remain\nseparate. Sections6--7 state positive-child hypotheses; the following\nsection handles all zero-step threshold parents by actual functions.\n\nThe independent joint draft full_primitive_reversal_boundary_blocks.md\nexcludes actual threshold words in both orientations of a different\nboundary block, with no saturation or sign-test restriction. Those\nexclusions were proved from legal functions and do not use the map here.\nNeither result decides general four-step reversal, constructs a feasible\nthreshold seed, excludes the whole seed domain, or solves the original\nasymptotic exact-profile problem. No new fact is asserted.\n\n## 8. No zero-step threshold parent is omitted\n\nEvery nonnegative three-step word with capacity T>=7 fails at least\none of its three scalar cut tests from Section5. Here is a proof that\ndoes not assume either orientation is primitive. If all tests held,\nthe endpoint bounds would give u,w<=m(T)=2T/(T+1)<2. If v<=1,\nthen T=1+u+v+w<6, a contradiction. Thus v>1; the middle cut and\nthe capacity identity give, just as above,\n\n T<=ell(m(T)+1), hence T^2+(1-3ell)T-ell<=0.\n\nAt T=7 this polynomial equals56-22ell>0, since ell=1+sqrt(2)<5/2.\nIts derivative2T+1-3ell is positive for T>=7. This contradiction\nproves that some cut fails. Delete all zero updates and, if needed,\npad the resulting word with zero updates to length3. Removing padding\nand reinserting the original zero steps repeats the same query point,\ngradient and value at each zero update. It preserves the entire GD run\nand the primitive energy, since zero steps contribute nothing and the\nterminal point is unchanged. This lifts the negative-energy function\nto the original four-step word.\n\nBy the full identity in fact04219554e41d9750, such a word cannot have\ntwenty nonnegative multipliers, exact flow, and full ambient R^5 PSD.\nThis is an actual-function exclusion, not an identification of formal\ngradients inside a proposed full certificate. {"id": "cbf9d8608baa8867", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T10:44:52.994043+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max2 round23 COMPLETE JOINT DRAFT: exact terminal-source value/flow pricing is attained by one static smooth convex function with real minimizers; the ENTIRE unit-curvature repaired-cut family has identically ZERO price for every legal amplitude, arbitrary nonnegative words and zero steps. C82 has an explicit nonzero price and closed optimized formula on0<a<1,0<b<=1; it adds NO condition when a^2+a<=1, including the prior projected prism. Its complementary family improvement is not yet a new surviving full-cone exclusion.", "evidence": "# Exact value-flow prices and two complete redundancy results\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof draft. This is max2's first analytical round\nafter completed baseline22, under TASK.md and guidance f7608aa358751108.\nNo numerical evaluation, solver, parameter scan, replay, machine algebra,\nformal verification, installation, or subordinate agent was used.\n\nAccepted inputs: 04219554e41d9750, Section10 (the ORIGINAL primitive\ncertificate), and 1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraphs1--2 (all-pair smooth\nconvex interpolation and its actual conjugate construction). Everything\nelse needed below is proved in this document. Earlier C82/C83 and coupling\ndrafts are comparison targets, never accepted premises.\n\nThe required concrete outcome is exact redundancy of value pricing on\nthe ENTIRE unit-curvature repaired-cut family, including all its legal\nrepair amplitudes, arbitrary nonnegative words, zero steps, and all\noriginal coordinates. The lifted-cut geometry in2948d4cd3660413f also has\nidentically zero price. A separate C82 calculation gives an exact price\nand a fully minimized formula in a specified parameter domain. It proves\nredundancy there when a^2+a<=1; its complementary regime is not asserted\nto resolve a surviving full-cone region.\n\nThe original asymptotic target, existence/nonexistence of a threshold\nseed, and general four-step cone reversal remain OPEN. No new fact is\nasserted and no theorem below is promoted past the verifier gate.\n\n## 1. Original orientation, objective, and formal coordinates\n\nLet n>=0, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a finite nonnegative word, s=sum h_i,\nC=1+s, and I={0,...,n}. Keep every original gradient coordinate, including\nthe terminal coordinate and every coordinate at a zero step. Fixed\nphysical vectors x_i,g_i in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space obey\n\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.\n\nFor sampled values f_i put\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V(f)=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n          +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_n||^2.       (1)\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 proves Q_ij<=0 for every ordered pair of actual\nsmooth convex samples, and constructs a single interpolant from these\ninequalities. Assume initially that all these finite inequalities hold.\nAn optimum sample will be retained explicitly when it is part of the\nfixed geometry; Section3 also handles the case where its location is free.\n\nFor the original nonnegative multipliers Lambda_ij, put Lambda_ii=0 and\n\n    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,\n    L^T e=omega.                                         (2)\n\nHere e is the (n+1)-entry all-ones column. Thus omega_i is outgoing minus\nincoming flow. The TERMINAL is the source, of mass2s, and index i<n is\na sink of mass2h_i. Reversing these arrows gives the wrong value problem.\n\nWith T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i, let\n\n    D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<n, -s(s+2)),\n    M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 gives, on every physical realization,\n\n    V(f)+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.   (3)\n\nThe full certificate requires M PSD on ALL of R^(n+1). Nothing below\ndeletes a coordinate, restricts Lambda support in that feasibility\nquestion, or identifies formal gradients at zero steps. Sparse flows\nconstructed below solve an auxiliary transport minimum; they are NOT\nclaimed to have PSD matrices.\n\n## 2. An exact finite price, with elementary proof and attained flow\n\nFix legal base values f_i and put w_ij=-Q_ij>=0 for distinct i,j. Regard\nw_ij as the cost of the directed arc i->j. Let d_i be the least cost of\na path from n to i, with d_n=0. The complete finite graph makes every\ndistance finite. A minimizing path exists: a repeated-vertex segment\nhas nonnegative cost and can be deleted, so it is enough to consider\nfinitely many simple paths.\n\nFor replacement values f_i+z_i, the complete constraints are exactly\n\n    z_i-z_j<=w_ij.                                      (4)\n\nSumming along an n->i path gives z_n-z_i<=its cost. Therefore\n\n    z_i-z_n>=-d_i,\n    V(f+z)>=V(f)-Pflow,\n    Pflow=2sum_(i<n)h_i d_i.                             (5)\n\nAll h_i are nonnegative, including possibly zero. The simultaneous\nchoice z_i=-d_i attains every lower bound in(5): the distance inequality\nd_j<=d_i+w_ij is precisely its feasibility in(4). Thus this is an\nattained minimum of the value problem, not only a lower bound from a\ndual program.\n\nThe same number is the exact minimum slack cost of the ORIGINAL flow:\n\n    min_{Lambda>=0, L^T e=omega} sum Lambda_ij w_ij\n       =Pflow.                                         (6)\n\nTo prove the lower bound, multiply w_ij>=d_j-d_i by Lambda_ij and sum.\nThe result is\n\n    sum Lambda_ij w_ij >= -sum_i omega_i d_i\n                         =2sum_(i<n)h_i d_i.\n\nFor attainment, choose a shortest n->i path for every i<n with h_i>0\nand send mass2h_i along that path. Sum these path flows. Intermediate\nvertices have equal incoming and outgoing mass; the endpoints give\nexactly(2). Each path contributes2h_i d_i. Zero-weight sinks need no\npath, but their vertices and all their available arcs remain present.\n\nCombining(3) with(6) gives a necessary condition for any full certificate:\n\n    V(f)>=Pflow.                                        (7)\n\nThe selected optimal values give the same condition as V(f-d)>=0. Indeed\ntheir new slacks are w'_ij=w_ij+d_i-d_j, and every prescribed-flow array\nsatisfies\n\n    sum Lambda_ij w'_ij=sum Lambda_ij w_ij-Pflow.\n\nThe Gram matrix and the formal certificate matrix do not change with\nthe values. The full PSD restriction can impose more than the minimum\nover flows in(6); it has not been dropped from the original problem.\n\n### Zero costs, coincident gradients, and fixed optimum constraints\n\nNo strict positivity of costs is needed. A zero-cost directed cycle\nhas every arc of cost zero, and(4) then forces equal shifts around that\ncycle. Distances and the path-flow construction remain valid without\nchoosing a unique shortest path.\n\nIf two base gradients coincide, Q_ij+Q_ji=0, so legality forces BOTH\nresiduals to vanish. Hence replacement shifts at these two samples are\nequal, even when their positions differ. If two base positions coincide,\nthe sum of the two residuals is ||g_i-g_j||^2; legality first forces\ncoincident gradients and then coincident values. These are properties of\nactual jets, not extra formal equations in the PSD certificate.\n\nOne may also include a fixed optimum jet x_star=g_star=f_star=0 as an\nextra graph vertex. Let d_i^* be distances in this larger graph. Then\nz_i=d_star^*-d_i^* is feasible, has z_star=0, and minimizes the same\nvalue objective subject to ALL finite and optimum pairs. Its improvement\nis2sum h_i d_i^*. In general d_i^*<=d_i, so this price need not equal\nthe original finite-flow price. They WILL agree in every concrete family\nbelow. The distinction is important: optimum edges are not original\nprimitive multipliers and cannot silently be used as such.\n\n## 3. The optimized finite values give one static function and real minima\n\nThis section also explains why(5), without a fixed optimum vertex, is\nnot merely a formal jet result. For any legal replacement values f'_i\non the finite samples, set\n\n    y_i=x_i-g_i, beta_i=||g_i||^2/2-f'_i,\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in I){<y_i,v>+beta_i},\n    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.                              (8)\n\nThis is the construction in accepted1252b8823bb5a290, written out here\nto identify its minimizers. H is finite, continuous, 1-strongly convex,\nand has quadratic growth. The supremum has one maximizer v(x). Strong\nsubgradient inequalities give\n\n    <x-x',v(x)-v(x')>>=||v(x)-v(x')||^2,\n\nand the maximizing inequalities give\n\n    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<v(x),x'-x>\n      <=<v(x')-v(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.\n\nThus F is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with\ngradient v(x). The all-pair inequalities say that at v=g_i the i-th\nquadratic in H is active and has gradient x_i. Consequently v(x_i)=g_i\nand F(x_i)=f'_i. This is ONE fixed F used for all steps.\n\nLet beta_max=max_i beta_i, and A={i:beta_i=beta_max}. The exact minimizer\nset of F is\n\n    argmin F=conv{y_i:i in A},   min F=-beta_max.          (9)\n\nIndeed gradient F(x)=0 is equivalent to x being a subgradient of H at0.\nThe subgradients there are exactly the convex hull of the active affine\nslopes y_i. To check the reverse inclusion without an unproved formula,\nif x is outside that convex hull, a separating vector u has\n<x,u> greater than every <y_i,u> for i in A. For sufficiently small\npositive t, inactive affine pieces remain inactive at tu, and\nH(tu)-H(0)=t max_(i in A)<y_i,u>+t^2||u||^2/2,\ncontradicting the subgradient inequality for x. The forward inclusion\nfollows by convexly combining the active supporting inequalities.\n\nIn particular a real minimizer always exists, even if none was fixed in\nthe graph. The true initial distance is exactly\n\n    dist(x_0,conv{y_i:i in A}).                           (10)\n\nNo arbitrary radius or changed function is inserted into the GD run.\nThe specific families below either preserve the optimum0 or identify\nthe closest minimizer explicitly.\n\n## 4. Entire unit-curvature repaired-cut family: price exactly zero\n\nThis is the main complete redundancy result. It works for any n>=1,\nany nonnegative word, and any cut j with0<=j<n and h_j>1. Put\n\n    P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t,\n    eta=(h_j-1)/(1+T_j)>0,\n    p_0=1, p_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) for0<=i<=j.\n\nChoose an amplitude epsilon>=0. Define two scalar coordinate arrays:\n\n    xB_i=1+sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t, gB_i=1        (i<=j),\n    xA_i=gA_i=p_i                            (i<=j),\n\n    R_k=sum_(t=k)^(n-1)h_t                    (k>j),\n    xB_k=-eta*(1+R_k), gB_k=-eta              (k>j),\n    xA_k=p_j*xB_k, gA_k=-eta*p_j              (k>j).\n\nEmpty sums have value zero. At the cut,\nxB_(j+1)=1-h_j=-eta*(1+T_j), and the same equality multiplied by p_j\nholds for coordinate A. Before the cut the A coordinate follows unit\nquadratic GD. After it both coordinates have constant gradients. Hence\nthe vectors\n\n    X_i=(xB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*xA_i),\n    G_i=(gB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*gA_i),\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2                            (11)\n\nobey every original GD equation. Include X_star=G_star=F_star=0.\n\nHere are ALL interpolation pairs. For saturated values(11),\n\n    Q_ik=<G_i-G_k,X_k-G_k>.\n\nFor a prefix target k<=j, its normal is\n\n    X_k-G_k=(sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t,0).\n\nEvery prefix source has residual zero. Every tail source has residual\n-(1+eta)sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t<=0. The optimum source has residual\n-sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t<=0.\n\nFor a tail target k>j, its normal is\n\n    X_k-G_k=-eta R_k*(1,sqrt(epsilon)*p_j).\n\nEvery tail source has residual zero. A prefix source i<=j has residual\n\n    -eta R_k*[1+eta+epsilon*p_j*(p_i+eta*p_j)].           (12)\n\nThe optimum source has residual\n-eta^2 R_k*(1+epsilon*p_j^2)<=0. Every pair into the optimum is zero.\nThese cases exhaust all directed finite and optimum pairs.\n\nIf T_j>0, legality is EXACTLY the finite set of inequalities\n\n    1+eta+epsilon*p_j*(p_i+eta*p_j)>=0   for i<=j.        (13)\n\nThe necessity follows by using target j+1, where R_(j+1)=T_j>0.\nIf T_j=0, every tail normal vanishes and no condition(13) is necessary.\nZero p_j, zero epsilon, zero intermediate steps and repeated gradients\ncause no exception in these formulas. This specifies the full legal\nrepair-amplitude family, rather than only its most-used endpoint.\n\nFor EVERY legal epsilon, the finite zero-cost arcs include\n\n    n->j,\n    j->i for every i<j,\n    n->k for every j<k<n,\n    i->n for every i<n.                                 (14)\n\nThe first and last groups hold because X_j=G_j and X_n=G_n. The second\ngroup holds because both sources and targets are prefix samples. The\nthird holds because all tail gradients coincide. Thus every original\nsample is in the zero-cost component of n, so\n\n    d_i=d_i^*=0 for EVERY i,\n    Pflow=0.                                           (15)\n\nEquivalently, all feasible value replacements have z_i=z_n. With the\nfixed zero optimum, Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=0 also forces z_n=0. Thus ALL\nsampled values in(11) are forced, not merely optimal for this objective.\n\nAn explicit attaining ORIGINAL flow sends2sum_(i<=j)h_i from n to j,\nsends2h_i from j to i for i<j, and sends2h_k directly from n to k for\nj<k<n. Its other entries vanish. The balance is(2), and every used arc\nis in(14). It has zero cost. This does not assert its matrix is PSD.\n\nThere is one actual static smooth convex witness for every legal\namplitude: use(8) with the zero optimum included. For its prefix samples,\nbeta_i=-sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t; for its tail samples,\nbeta_k=-eta^2 R_k*(1+epsilon*p_j^2). These constants are nonpositive,\nand equality in either formula implies that the corresponding normal\nis zero. The optimum sample also has constant and normal zero. Thus(9)\ngives the UNIQUE minimizer0 for this single conjugate interpolant. Its\nTRUE squared initial distance is(1+P_j)^2+epsilon, in every case above.\n\nThe projection construction used in the earlier drafts is also valid:\nlet K=conv({0} union {G_i}) and define\n\n    F(X)=max_(G in K){<X,G>-||G||^2/2}.                  (16)\n\nThe complete normal list proves that the optimizer at X_i is G_i.\nProjection onto K is1-Lipschitz: add the two projection normal\ninequalities to obtain ||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>. The same maximizing\ninequalities used in Section3 prove differentiability. Since0 is in K,\nF>=0=F(0). This is a real minimizer, and(11) are its actual values.\n\nThe saturated-value telescoping identity is\n\n    V=||X_0||^2-||X_n+sG_n||^2.\n\nTo check it, use2h_i F_i+h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2\n=||X_i||^2-||X_(i+1)||^2, sum, and substitute F_n.\nHere X_n=G_n, so the family gives exactly\n\n    V=(1+P_j)^2+epsilon\n         -C^2 eta^2*(1+epsilon*p_j^2).                  (17)\n\nReoptimizing values adds NO inequality to nonnegativity of(17), for\nany legal epsilon, word, cut or zero-step pattern. Consequently it adds\nnone to an existing collection of necessary conditions that already\nincludes this ENTIRE family. This is a family-level exact redundancy\nclaim, not global redundancy for arbitrary queried geometries.\n\n### The actual four-step C83 member and true radius\n\nTake h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), a,b,c,d>0, j=2, and\n\n    C=5+a+b+c+d, Q=3+a+b, eta=c/(2+d), p_2=ab.\n\nThe only possibly negative coefficient in(13) is i=1, where p_1=-a.\nIf b eta<1, the full amplitude interval is\n\n    0<=epsilon<=(1+eta)/(a^2 b(1-b eta)).                (18)\n\nThe other two coefficients have positive p_i. The endpoint in(18)\nis exactly the repaired-cut value used in d3abee1b17da52da; there is\nno new function-value freedom at ANY amplitude in this interval.\n\nFor0<ab<1 and epsilon>0, the two prefix gradients\n(1,sqrt(epsilon)) and(1,-a sqrt(epsilon)) have G_2 in the relative\ninterior of their segment. Since G_3=-eta G_2, zero is in the interior\nof their nondegenerate triangle with G_3. Thus the minimizer of(16) is\nuniquely0: a nonzero normal at0 would have positive pairing with a\npoint of an open ball in K. The TRUE squared distance is Q^2+epsilon.\nAt epsilon=0 the instance is scalar and0 is again the closest minimizer\nto the displayed initial point. No benchmark ratio is inferred from V.\n\n## 5. The lifted-cut coupling geometry also has zero value price\n\nThis section reconstructs the actual geometry used in draft\n2948d4cd3660413f, independently of that draft's quantitative matrix gap.\nLet a,b,c,d>0, put u=1+a, C=5+a+b+c+d, H=3+c+d, eta=b/H,\nand assume a eta<1. Define\n\n    nu=(1+eta)(1-a eta)/a>0,\n    U=(1,0), Z=-eta U+sqrt(nu)*(0,1),\n    X=(U+uZ, U, -bU, -(2+d)eta U, -eta U),\n    G=(Z, U, -eta U, -eta U, -eta U),\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (19)\n\nThe original four updates follow from b=H eta. Include the zero optimum.\nThe normals at targets1 and4 vanish. At target0 the normal is U+aZ;\nthe source1 residual is zero, and each source2,3,4 residual is -a nu.\nThe optimum-source residual there is -(1-a eta)<0. At target2 the\nnormal is -(b-eta)U: source0 has residual zero, source1 has residual\n-(1+eta)(b-eta), and tail sources have residual zero. At target3 the\nnormal is -(1+d)eta U: source0 has residual zero, source1 has residual\n-(1+eta)(1+d)eta, and tail sources have residual zero. Optimum-source\nresiduals at2,3 are respectively -eta(b-eta) and-(1+d)eta^2.\nEvery residual into the optimum is zero. This checks every pair.\n\nIn particular the zero-cost finite paths are\n\n    4->1->0, 4->2, 4->3, and i->4 for every i<4.        (20)\n\nAll value differences are therefore forced, and the optimum pairs fix\ntheir common shift. Both finite-flow and fixed-optimum prices vanish.\nThe function(16) with K=conv{0,Z,U,-eta U} realizes these samples.\nIts minimizer set is{(0,t):t<=0}; the initial second coordinate is\nu sqrt(nu)>0, so0 is the closest minimizer. Its true squared initial\ndistance is ||U+uZ||^2, not an independent calibration variable.\n\nThe energy is exactly\n\n    Erow=||U+uZ||^2-C^2 eta^2\n        =a+3+1/a-[(1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a]eta-C^2 eta^2.     (21)\n\nValue-flow pricing returns only Erow>=0. It cannot recover a strictly\npositive lower bound on Erow on this SAME geometry: the auxiliary flow\nminimum is attained at zero and the values have no remaining freedom.\nThe further full-matrix restrictions in2948d4cd3660413f use PSD coupling\nwith a different quadratic direction and the first row. This result\nneither assumes that unverified coupling theorem nor refutes it; it\nidentifies exactly what the present price can and cannot add to it.\n\n## 6. C82 triangle: exact nonzero price and a real interpolant\n\nHere keep canonical h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) with a,b,c,d>0, and let\n\n    C=5+a+b+c+d, D=2+d, eta=bc/D,\n    ell=max{0,c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2},\n    t=a v-1, Htri(v)=(v+1)(a v-1).\n\nUse parameters v>=b and z>=0 with ell<=z^2<=Htri(v). For nondegenerate\ntriangles take z>0; the boundary z=0 is legal by the same normal checks.\nThese inequalities imply t>=0. Define\n\n    X_0=((1+a)v-1,z), G_0=(v,0),\n    X_1=G_1=(-1,z),\n    X_2=(b,-bz),     G_2=(b,0),\n    X_3=(-bc,-bz),   G_3=(-eta,0),\n    X_4=(-eta,-bz),  G_4=(-eta,0),\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (22)\n\nEvery GD equation has the original four step lengths. For completeness,\nK=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3} contains0. At target0 the residual normal is(t,z).\nIts comparisons with G_1,G_2,G_3 and0 are, respectively,\n\n    z^2-(v+1)t, (b-v)t, -(eta+v)t, -v t,\n\nall nonpositive. At target1 the normal vanishes. At target2 the normal\nis(0,-bz), so all horizontal-gradient sources give zero and source1\ngives -bz^2. At target3 the normal is(-(1+d)eta,-bz). Its source1\nresidual is b*[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0; its horizontal-gradient\nsources and optimum source are nonpositive. At target4 the normal is\n(0,-bz), again giving zero for horizontal-gradient sources and -bz^2\nfor source1. Every residual into the optimum is zero. Thus all finite\nand optimum pairs hold, and(16) gives one actual base function.\n\nThe finite zero-cost arcs4->1,4->2,4->3 show d_1=d_2=d_3=d_4=0.\nThe least cost into0 is\n\n    Dprice=min{(v-b)t, (v+1)t-z^2}.                      (23)\n\nThe other incoming costs, (v+eta)t and v t from the optimum, are no\nsmaller than(v-b)t. Every path into0 has a final incoming arc, while\neach minimizing source1 or2 is reached at cost zero. Therefore\n\n    d_0=d_0^*=Dprice, all other d_i=d_i^*=0,\n    Pflow=2(1+a)Dprice.                                 (24)\n\nThe complete fixed-geometry value optimum is attained by\n\n    F'_0=F_0-Dprice, F'_i=F_i for1<=i<=4, F'_star=0.      (25)\n\nAll incoming residuals at0 increase by Dprice and stay nonpositive\nby(23); all outgoing residuals from0 decrease. All other pairs are\nunchanged. This is a direct check of all thirty ordered pairs including\nthe optimum, independently confirming the path calculation.\n\nFor the actual function use(8) with these values and the optimum.\nIts affine constants beta_i are\n\n    beta_0=-v t+Dprice<=-b t,\n    beta_1=beta_2=beta_4=beta_star=0,\n    beta_3=-(1+d)eta^2<0.\n\nWhen t>0, the active normals in(9) are0 and(0,-bz). Therefore its\nminimizer set is the segment joining these two points, and0 is the\nclosest minimizer to X_0, whose second coordinate z is nonnegative.\nIf t=0, z=0 and the initial normal also vanishes, with the same\nclosest minimizer0. Thus the true squared initial distance is\n\n    ((1+a)v-1)^2+z^2.\n\nThe base telescoping energy and the exact optimized energy are\n\n    Ebase(v,z)=((1+a)v-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2 eta^2,\n    Eprice(v,z)=Ebase(v,z)-2(1+a)Dprice.                 (26)\n\nIn particular a generic claim that saturated projection values are\nalways rigid is FALSE. Formula(23) gives a strictly positive price\nwhenever v>b, t>0, and z^2<(v+1)t. This is a statement about a fixed\nactual geometry, not yet about improvement over an optimized family.\n\n## 7. Full C82 value-and-parameter minimum for0<a<1,0<b<=1\n\nKeep c,d>0 arbitrary and ell as above. Put\n\n    R=[1-a+sqrt((1+a)^2+4a ell)]/(2a),\n    v_cross=[1+ell/(b+1)]/a,\n    v_stationary=a/(1-a),\n    v_best=min{v_cross,max{R,v_stationary}},\n    K=C^2 eta^2.                                       (27)\n\nThen R>=1/a>b and R<=v_cross. The first assertion follows because\nHtri has its nonnegative zero at1/a and is increasing thereafter. For\nthe second, Htri(R)=ell implies(b+1)(aR-1)<=ell.\n\nThe exact infimum of(26) over EVERY legal triangle parameter is\n\n    Enew=(1-a^2)v_best^2-2(1+a)a v_best+1+2(1+a)\n                +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.                 (28)\n\nIf ell>0 the minimizing triangle has z=sqrt(ell)>0 and v=v_best.\nIf ell=0 the same formula is attained by the displayed degenerate\nlegal data; alternatively it is the limit of nondegenerate triangles.\n\nHere is the complete minimization. For fixed v>=R, the price switches\nat z^2=(b+1)t. Below this value its coefficient of z^2 is1-b^2>=0;\nabove it the coefficient is1-b^2+2(1+a)>0. Therefore z^2=ell is always\na minimizer. For R<=v<=v_cross the second term in(23) is the minimum,\nand(26) becomes\n\n    J_left(v)=(1-a^2)v^2-2(1+a)a v+1+2(1+a)\n                         +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.         (29)\n\nFor v>=v_cross it becomes\n\n    J_right(v)=(1-a^2)v^2+2(1+a)ab v+1-2(1+a)b\n                              +(1-b^2)ell-K.           (30)\n\nThe two expressions agree at v_cross. J_right has strictly positive\nderivative on that half-line. J_left is a strictly convex quadratic\nwith stationary point a/(1-a). Clipping that point to[R,v_cross]\nproves(27)--(28), including both endpoint cases.\n\nThe original unpriced C82 minimum in this domain is\n\n    Eold=((1+a)R-1)^2+(1-b^2)ell-K,                    (31)\n\nsince its energy increases in v and is minimized at z^2=ell. At v=R,\nHtri(R)=ell makes Dprice=0, and J_left(R)=Eold.\n\nConsequently value pricing adds EXACTLY NO information to this fully\noptimized C82 condition whenever\n\n    R>=a/(1-a).                                        (32)\n\nIn particular(32) holds throughout a^2+a<=1, because R>=1/a.\nThe positive root of a^2+a=1 is(sqrt(5)-1)/2. This includes the\nfirst-step projection range39/100<=a<=41/100 from d3abee1b17da52da,\nwith its b range79/100<=b<=81/100. The C83 price was already zero there\nby Section4, independently of this comparison.\n\nWhen ell>0 and R<a/(1-a), one has v_cross>R and v_best>R, so the\noptimized energy is strictly smaller. The precise positive reduction is\n\n    Eold-Enew=(1-a^2)*[(R-v_stationary)^2\n                                 -(v_best-v_stationary)^2]>0. (33)\n\nEquivalently this strict-improvement regime is\n\n    a^2+a>1,\n    0<ell<(a^2+a-1)/(1-a)^2.                            (34)\n\nThis proves a strict improvement of the C82 FAMILY VALUE in its stated\ndomain. It does NOT prove that a word satisfying all the other current\nnecessary conditions is rejected by(28). In particular(34) alone is\nnot a surviving full-cone region and must not be advertised as one.\n\n## 8.
