The parent uses an explicit rank-one cross coupling and changes\nonly two values in its left copy:\n\n    f_2 -> f_2-d_atom,\n    f_6 -> f_6-alpha^2 d_atom,\n    d_atom=(1-rho)(K-1)eta>0.                         (1)\n\nEvery position and gradient of both children is retained. All other\nvalues are retained with their appropriate spatial-square scaling.\nOne explicit static, globally defined, convex 1-smooth function realizes\nall sixteen samples. All 272 distinct original ordered interpolation\npairs, including the optimum, hold. The exact minimizer set is a convex\nhull of three specified residuals; the true initial distance is R.\n\nThe physical word is\n\n    h_3=(a,b,a),\n    h_7=(h_3,1+r_0,h_3),\n    h_15=(h_7,1+r_1,h_7).                            (2)\n\nIts true-distance normalized payoff V_15 and capacity state satisfy\n\n    W_15(h_15) >= V_15 = beta^2 alpha^2 N/R^2,\n    kappa_15=(lambda^2 A)^2 V_15 > 1+1/1000.         (3)\n\nThis is a finite capacity surplus, not a comparison replacing the\noriginal denominator B_15 by capacity squared. The exact original\nbenchmark ratio of this witness is B_15 V_15. It supplies neither an\narbitrary-h result nor an all-depth construction.\n\nFor clarity, the FULL released affine parent model has arbitrary\nsimilarities E,F of the fixed child space with E^T E=ell I and\nF^T F=nu I, ell,nu>=0, arbitrary cross Gram, and physical relative\ntranslation t_join=E d-F P. Its left samples are (E X_i,E G_i), its\nright queried samples are (t_join+F X_j,F G_j), and its final point is\nt_join+F X_7 with a free terminal gradient. Every value and one common\nminimizer are free; the full Gram, including optimum and terminal\nvectors, is PSD. All original rows are imposed. The normalization uses\ndistance to an actual common minimizer, and a claimed witness must price\nits nearest-minimizer distance.\n\nWe construct a point of this full model by choosing ell=1, nu=beta^2,\nt_join=0 and an explicit F. This is a SUFFICIENT CONSTRUCTION, not a\nrestriction asserted lossless for the full model. The model's free\nterminal gradient is legally selected as F G_7. Its incoming rows are\nchecked too. Independent scales, translations, values, and PSD range\nconditions are not discarded in an impossibility claim.\n\n## 2. Exact atom and its original rows\n\nUse Euclidean inner products throughout. Define\n\n    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,\n    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300, rho=599/600,\n    z=b-1, K=a+rho, m=a+b, eta=z/K,\n    A=1+2a+b=(143+105s)/50,\n    r_0=theta A=(67+38s)/50, r_1=A,\n    Dlen=m+K=A-1/600.\n\nTake orthogonal U,V in R^2, with ||U||=1 and ||V||^2=eta.\nPut w=rho V and p=m U+K V. The atom is\n\n    g=(U,U,V,w),\n    x=(p,bU+KV,KV,w),\n    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2, 0<=i<=3.\n\nIt obeys the three updates (a,b,a). Its residuals y_i=x_i-g_i\nand energies E_i=<g_i,y_i> are\n\n    y_0=(m-1)U+KV, E_0=m-1,\n    y_1=zU+KV,     E_1=z,\n    y_2=(K-1)V,    E_2=e=(K-1)eta,\n    y_3=0,         E_3=0.\n\nFor any jet define aheight_i=f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2 and\nDelta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. The original convention is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =aheight_i-Delta_j+<g_i,y_j>.                (4)\n\nFor the atom aheight_i=0 and Delta_i=E_i. Its complete table of\n<v-g_j,y_j>, for receiver j and possible source gradient v, is\n\n| j | v=0 | v=U | v=V | v=w |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| 0 | -(m-1) | 0 | -a | -a-(1-rho)z |\n| 1 | -z | 0 | 0 | -(1-rho)z |\n| 2 | -e | -e | 0 | -(1-rho)e |\n| 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n\nAll entries are nonpositive. The duplicate U occurs at both actual\nindices 0 and 1. This checks every atom pair and every optimum pair.\nWrite\n\n    R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Kz,\n    N=||w||^2=rho^2 eta,\n    D=<p,w>=rho z,\n    d_atom=(1-rho)e=e/600.                          (5)\n\nThe symbol R here is a coordinate radius, distinct from the project's\nobjective convention W=2R.\n\n## 3. The internally translated seven-step geometry\n\nFix sigma in [9999/10000,1]. Put\n\n    delta=1-sigma, chi=delta/sigma,\n    alpha=sigma r_0 sqrt(N)/R, k=alpha^2.\n\nLet e_axis=w/sqrt(N), p_axis=p/R. In the Euclidean direct sum\nthe real line direct-sum e_axis^perp direct-sum p_axis^perp define isometries\n\n    L u=(<u,e_axis>,u-<u,e_axis>e_axis,0),\n    T v=(-<v,p_axis>,0,v-<v,p_axis>p_axis).\n\nThus\n\n    <L u,alpha T v>\n        =-sigma r_0 <u,w><p,v>/R^2.                 (6)\n\nDefine the INTERNAL translation t=-delta r_0 Lw. In particular\nt=chi alpha T p. The seven-step jets are\n\n    X_i=Lx_i, G_i=Lg_i, F_i=f_i,                  0<=i<=3,\n    X_(4+i)=t+alpha T x_i,\n    G_(4+i)=alpha T g_i, F_(4+i)=k f_i,           0<=i<=3.\n\nThe bridge lands at -r_0 Lw=t+alpha T p. Hence this is an actual\ntrajectory on h_7. Write P=X_0=Lp and w_1=G_7=alpha T w.\nIts endpoint is X_7=w_1+t, not w_1.\n\nLet Y_i=X_i-G_i and use aheight_i,Delta_i for these seven-step jets.\nFor 0<=j<=3 one has\n\n    Y_j=Ly_j, aheight_j=0, Delta_j=E_j,\n    Y_(4+j)=alpha T(chi p+y_j),\n    aheight_(4+j)=-chi k <p,g_j>,\n    Delta_(4+j)=k E_j.                             (7)\n\nAll original child pairs hold. Within each atom this follows from the\ntable and spatial scaling; translation cancels within the right atom.\nFor a left source i and right receiver 4+j, (4) gives\n\n    Q_(i,4+j)=-kE_j+<Lg_i,t+alpha T y_j> <=0.\n\nFor a right source 4+i and left receiver j it gives\n\n    Q_(4+i,j)=-<alpha T g_i,t>-E_j\n                                  +<alpha T g_i,Ly_j> <=0.\n\nEvery atom gradient and residual has nonnegative U,V coordinates.\nThus (6) makes the mixed terms nonpositive, while\n<Lg_i,t><=0 and <alpha T g_i,t>=chi k<p,g_i>>=0.\nFinally aheight<=0 and Delta>=0 check all optimum rows.\n\n## 4. The two allowed value changes inside the left child\n\nSet c_2=d_atom, c_6=k d_atom, and c_i=0 at every other child index.\nThe modified left child has values F_i-c_i, unchanged X_i,G_i,\n\n    aheight_i^L=aheight_i-c_i,\n    Delta_i^L=Delta_i-c_i.                          (8)\n\nEvery child interpolation row remains nonpositive. The only rows that\ncan increase are those into receiver 2 or 6. For receiver 2, its own\natom's other sources have slacks e,e,d_atom. Every source from the\nother atom has slack at least e, by the second displayed cross formula\nin section 3. Thus adding c_2=d_atom is legal; decreasing the source's\nvalue can only help. For receiver 6, its own atom's other sources have\nslacks k e,k e,k d_atom, and every source from the other atom has slack\nat least k e, by the first displayed cross formula. Adding c_6 is legal.\nThese statements also cover the row between the two changed samples.\n\nThe optimum rows remain legal: aheight_i^L<=0, and the changed positive\nenergies are Delta_2^L=rho e and Delta_6^L=k rho e. No endpoint value\nchanges. In particular both endpoint energies Delta_3,Delta_7 stay zero.\n\nThese are changes to one final list of jets. We do not change a function\nonline while an algorithm runs; section 9 builds a single function from\nthe entire list before the actual run.\n\n## 5. Top bridge and explicit full Gram realization\n\nThe actual outgoing vector at the next bridge is\n\n    d=t-r_1 w_1=alpha T(chi p-r_1 w).\n\nUse the auxiliary scalars\n\n    omega=r_1 rho-chi K,\n    Mout=r_1 D-chi R^2,\n    Hout=||chi p-r_1 w||^2\n        =r_1^2 N-2chi r_1 D+chi^2 R^2,\n    beta=||d||/R, nscale=beta^2=k Hout/R^2.         (9)\n\nAll are positive except chi, which may be zero. In particular d!=0.\nIn the real line direct-sum d^perp direct-sum P^perp define\n\n    E_top u=(<u,d/||d||>,u-<u,d/||d||>d/||d||,0),\n    T_top v=(<v,P/R>,0,v-<v,P/R>P/R),\n    B_top=beta T_top.\n\nThese maps give a realization in dimension five and satisfy\n\n    B_top P=E_top d,\n    <E_top u,B_top v>=<u,d><P,v>/R^2.               (10)\n\nThe actual parent samples are\n\n    Z_i=E_top X_i, J_i=E_top G_i, fhat_i=F_i-c_i,    0<=i<=7,\n    Z_(8+j)=B_top X_j, J_(8+j)=B_top G_j,\n    fhat_(8+j)=nscale F_j,                           0<=j<=7.\n\nThe bridge equality in (10) verifies the step 1+r_1. All other steps\nfollow from the child recursions. The complete Gram of these vectors\nand optimum zero is PSD by this explicit Euclidean factorization; all\nrange conditions are present. The final gradient J_15 is an allowed\nchoice for the full model's free unqueried gradient.\n\n## 6. Rational scalar estimates used in the full pair check\n\nThe following deliberately loose rational bounds follow from\n14142/10000<sqrt(2)<14143/10000, proved by squaring, and the defining\nformulas:\n\n    341/100<m<171/50, 241/100<K<121/50,\n    1<z<201/200, 29/5<Dlen<59/10,\n    29/5<r_1<6, 12/5<r_0<49/20,\n    14<R^2<71/5, 2/5<N<21/50, 0<D<11/10,\n    e>1/2, rho>99/100, 0<=chi<=1/9999.             (11)\n\nFor example R^2> (341/100)^2+241/100>14 and\nR^2<(171/50)^2+(121/50)(201/200)<71/5.\nSince rho^2>99/100, N>99/242>2/5; also\nN<eta<(201/200)/(241/100)<21/50.\nThe bound e>1/2 follows from (K-1)eta>(141/100)(50/121)>1/2.\n\nConsequently omega>0, Mout>0, and\n\n    Hout>67/5, 1/8<nscale<1/5.                     (12)\n\nIndeed Hout>(29/5)^2(2/5)-2(1/9999)6(11/10)>67/5.\nUsing sigma^2>999/1000, the lower estimate for nscale is greater than\n\n    (999/1000)(12/5)^2(2/5)(67/5)/(71/5)^2 >1/8.\n\nFor the upper estimate discard the negative term in Hout and use\nchi^2 R^2<1/1000000. The upper bound becomes\n\n    (49/20)^2(21/50)[36(21/50)+1/1000000]/14^2 <1/5.\n\nDefine the auxiliary quantities\n\n    Caff=K Dlen/(r_0 r_1 rho^2),\n    Tfirst=Mout Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout),\n    Lcoef=omega eta sigma r_0 D/R^2,\n    Tsecond=Lcoef Dlen/(R^2 nscale).\n\nThe crucial strict endpoint bound is\n\n    rho Caff<1.                                    (13)\n\nIt has the exact algebraic certificate\n\n    r_0 r_1 rho-K Dlen\n       =(-381891+284836 sqrt(2))/9000000>0,\n\nwhere already sqrt(2)>7/5 proves positivity. In particular\nCaff<600/599 and Caff-1<1/599.\n\nWe also have\n\n    Tfirst<=Caff,\n    Tsecond-Tfirst < (11/5)chi   if chi>0,\n    Lcoef/z<1/2,\n    Tsecond+chi(Lcoef-z)/(nscale z)<=Caff.           (14)\n\nAt chi=0 the second difference is zero and the other non-strict\nrelations continue to hold. Here are derivations. The inequality\nTfirst<=Caff is equivalent to\n\n    (1+chi)(r_1 D-chi R^2)r_1 N <= D Hout.\n\nAfter cancelling the constant terms, the difference of the left side\nand right side is\n\n    chi r_1 [r_1 DN-R^2 N+2D^2]\n                 -chi^2 R^2(r_1 N+D) <=0.\n\nThe bracket is negative because R^2 N>28/5, whereas\nr_1 DN+2D^2<6(11/10)(21/50)+2(11/10)^2<28/5.\nNext z omega=Mout+chi m^2, so\n\n    Tsecond-Tfirst=chi m^2 Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout).\n\nThe coefficient is less than 11/5 by (11)-(12): its numerator is\nless than (171/50)^2(59/10), and its denominator exceeds\n(999/1000)(12/5)(99/100)(67/5); comparison of these two rational\nproducts gives the asserted bound. Also\n\n    Lcoef/z <= r_0 r_1 N/R^2\n             < (49/20)6(21/50)/14 <1/2.\n\nThus chi(1-Lcoef/z)/nscale >=(5/2)chi, with strict inequality when\nchi>0. Subtracting this from the second bound gives the last part of\n(14). These estimates keep the internal residual cost; it is precisely\nthe term chi(Lcoef-z) that must not be dropped at the critical row.\n\nFor later use, Tfirst,Tsecond<6/5. This follows from Caff<600/599,\nthe second difference bound, and chi<=1/9999. Moreover\n\n    nscale z(Caff-1)<d_atom/2.                      (15)\n\nIf Caff<=1 it is immediate. Otherwise the left side is less than\n(1/5)(201/200)/599=201/599000, while d_atom/2>1/2400;\nthe comparison is 482400<599000.\n\nPut b_j=<p,y_j> and l_j=<w,y_j> for the atom. Their table is\n\n| j | E_j | b_j | l_j |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| 0 | m-1 | R^2-m | D |\n| 1 | z | z Dlen | D |\n| 2 | e | (K-1)z | rho e |\n| 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n\nFor j=0,1,2 one has b_j<R^2 and l_j/E_j<=rho. Also\nb_1/E_1=Dlen, while b_j/E_j<(4/5)Dlen for j=0,2.\nFor j=0, the numerator is less than 54/5 and\n(4/5)Dlen(m-1)>(4/5)(29/5)(12/5)>54/5.\nFor j=2, b_2/E_2=K<(4/5)Dlen follows from (11).\n\nFinally the two bounds needed in the reverse orientation are\n\n    sigma r_0 r_1 D m rho/R^4 <1/3,\n    (sigma r_0 D/R^4) Mout <1/10<nscale.             (16)\n\nFor the first, bound its numerator by (49/20)6(11/10)(171/50)\nand its denominator below by 196. For the second, use\nMout<=r_1D and bound the numerator by (49/20)6(11/10)^2.\nBoth resulting rational inequalities are strict.\n\n## 7. Every forward cross row\n\nAbbreviate the following four exact child correlations:\n\n    u_i=<G_i,d>, v_i=<P,G_i>,\n    p_i=<P,Y_i>, q_i=<d,Y_i>.\n\nFrom (6)-(9) their entries are\n\n    u_0=u_1=0,\n    u_2=sigma r_0 rho eta Mout/R^2, u_3=rho u_2,\n    u_4=u_5=k chi m, u_6=-k omega eta, u_7=rho u_6;\n\n    v_0=v_1=m, v_2=z, v_3=D,\n    v_(4+j)=-(sigma r_0 D/R^2)<p,g_j>;\n\n    p_j=b_j,\n    p_(4+j)=-sigma r_0 D(chi+b_j/R^2);\n\n    q_j=sigma r_0 Mout l_j/R^2,\n    q_(4+j)=k[-chi Mout+chi b_j-r_1 l_j],           0<=j<=3.\n                                                               (17)\n\nIn particular p_0,p_1,p_2>0, p_3=0, p_4,p_5,p_6<0,\nand p_7<=0. All q_0,q_1,q_2>0, q_3=0, q_4,q_5,q_6<0,\nand q_7=-k chi Mout<=0. To see the strict late signs, use\nchi b_j<=chi R^2<1/600, while r_1 l_j>1 for j=0,1,2.\n\nEquation (4) and the cross identity (10) give every forward cross row:\n\n    Q_(i,8+j)=aheight_i-c_i-nscale Delta_j+u_i p_j/R^2.\n                                                               (18)\n\nAll aheight_i-c_i are nonpositive. Therefore only positive products\nu_i p_j need further consideration; zero products cause no problem.\n\nFirst let the receiver be j=0,1,2. Positive products can have source\ni=2,3,4,5. For i=4,5, aheight_i=-chi k m and u_i=chi k m,\nso their sum with the mixed term is chi k m(b_j/R^2-1)<=0.\nFor i=2 the ratio of u_2 b_j/R^2 to nscale E_j is\n\n    Tfirst * b_j/(Dlen E_j).\n\nAt j=0,2 it is less than (6/5)(4/5)<1. At j=1, (14)-(15)\ngive Q_(2,9)<=nscale z(Caff-1)-d_atom<-d_atom/2.\nFor i=3 the same ratio is multiplied by rho, and (13)-(14)\nmake it strictly smaller than one, including at j=1.\n\nNow let the receiver be 4+j, for atomic j=0,1,2,3. A positive product\ncan only have source i=6 or 7. Formula (18) becomes exactly\n\n    Q_(6,12+j)/k\n       =chi(Lcoef-z)+Lcoef b_j/R^2-nscale E_j-d_atom,\n    Q_(7,12+j)/k\n       =rho[chi(Lcoef-z)+Lcoef b_j/R^2]-nscale E_j.\n                                                               (19)\n\nFor j=3, E_j=b_j=0 and Lcoef<z, so both are nonpositive.\nFor j=0,2 drop the nonpositive chi term and use\nTsecond b_j/(Dlen E_j)<(6/5)(4/5)<1.\nFor j=1 use the last inequality of (14). It gives\n\n    Q_(6,13)/k <=nscale z(Caff-1)-d_atom<-d_atom/2,\n    Q_(7,13)/k <=nscale z(rho Caff-1)<0.\n\nReceivers with p_j=0 and products of opposite signs were already\ncovered by (18). This exhausts all 64 forward cross rows, including\nevery row into the actual final sample 15.\n\n## 8. Every reverse cross row and the total original system\n\nThe other orientation is\n\n    Q_(8+i,j)=nscale aheight_i-Delta_j+c_j+v_i q_j/R^2.\n                                                               (20)\n\nFor early receivers j=0,1,2, a positive product requires i=0,1,2,3.\nTheir v_i are at most m. The positive term divided by E_j is at most\n\n    m sigma r_0 Mout rho/R^4\n       <=m sigma r_0 r_1 D rho/R^4<1/3.\n\nBut Delta_j-c_j>=rho E_j, so (20) is nonpositive. Other source signs\nonly help. Receiver j=3 has q_j=Delta_j=c_j=0, leaving\nnscale aheight_i<=0.\n\nFor a late receiver 4+j, a positive product requires a late source\n4+i. Set Patom_i=<p,g_i>, which is one of m,m,z,D and is positive.\nUsing (7) and (17), combine the source height and mixed term exactly:\n\n    nscale aheight_(4+i)+v_(4+i)q_(4+j)/R^2\n      = k Patom_i {chi[-nscale+(sigma r_0 D/R^4)Mout]\n               +(sigma r_0 D/R^4)[-chi b_j+r_1 l_j]}.\n                                                               (21)\n\nFor j=3 the last bracket is zero; (16) makes (21) nonpositive.\nFor j=0,1,2 discard its nonpositive chi terms. The remaining term,\ndivided by k E_j, is at most\n\n    m sigma r_0 r_1 D rho/R^4<1/3.\n\nThe receiver has Delta_(4+j)-c_(4+j)>=k rho E_j. Thus (20) is\nnonpositive. Early sources have v_i>0 and q_(4+j)<=0, so they are\nalready safe. This checks all 64 reverse cross rows, including rows\nwhose source is the final gradient.\n\nSections 3-4 check all 56 ordered within-left rows; section 3 and\nscaling check all 56 within-right rows. Sections 7-8 check all 128\ncross rows. The 32 optimum incidences hold because every aheight is\nnonpositive and every Delta is nonnegative, including after (1).\nThus all 272 distinct ordered rows on {star,0,...,15} are proved.\nNo sampled gradient, remote pair, or original position has been removed.\n\n## 9. One global function and the TRUE radius\n\nLet yhat_i=Z_i-J_i and Deltahat_i=fhat_i-||J_i||^2/2. On the same R^5\ndefine\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,max_(0<=i<=15)(<yhat_i,v>-Deltahat_i)),\n    F_parent(Z)=sup_v(<Z,v>-H(v)).                  (22)\n\nAll rows just proved, together with accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`,\nshow that this is one finite, everywhere differentiable, convex,\n1-smooth function satisfying F_parent(Z_i)=fhat_i, grad F_parent(Z_i)=J_i,\nand F_parent(0)=0=min F_parent. Its trajectory from Z_0 is exactly (2).\n\nOnly samples 3,7,11,15 have Deltahat_i=0; their residuals are\n0,E_top t,0,B_top t. All other Deltahat_i are strictly positive.\nIt follows directly that\n\n    argmin F_parent=conv{0,E_top t,B_top t}.         (23)\n\nFor completeness, at sufficiently small epsilon>0 in any fixed\ndirection v,\n\n    H(epsilon v)=epsilon^2||v||^2/2\n       +epsilon max(0,<E_top t,v>,<B_top t,v>).\n\nEvery point of the displayed convex hull has conjugate value zero.\nIf Z is outside it, let z be its closest point and v=Z-z. The elementary\nprojection inequality gives <Z,v> greater than the maximum of the\nthree support values. The displayed local expression then gives\nF_parent(Z)>0. This proves both inclusions in (23).\n\nThe exact nearest-minimizer distance is R. Indeed\n\n    <P,t>=-delta r_0 D<=0,\n    <P,d>=sigma r_0 D Mout/R^2>0,\n    <E_top P,B_top t>=<P,d><P,t>/R^2<=0.\n\nHence <Z_0,z><=0 for every minimizer z in (23), and\n||Z_0-z||^2=R^2+||z||^2-2<Z_0,z>>=R^2.\nZero attains equality. At sigma<1 the internal residuals are nonzero;\nthey have not been identified with zero or hidden in a surrogate radius.\n\nThe endpoint value is fhat_15=nscale k N/2. Thus (3)'s exact payoff is\n2F_parent(Z_15)/dist(Z_0,argmin F_parent)^2=nscale k N/R^2.\nRescaling F_parent(RZ)/R^2 makes the true initial distance one without\nchanging this normalized payoff or any step. Multiplying the function\nby a desired L_smooth>0 gives the same normalized payoff with steps\nh_i/L_smooth. This is the original doubled-gap W=2R convention.\n\n## 10. Paid state, retained value costs, and the next question\n\nThe atom's state is kappa_3=A^2 N/R^2. Its exact defining expressions are\n\n    m=(574+319s)/300, K=(567+622s)/600,\n    z=(145+4s)/150,\n    R^2=(620189+458670s)/90000,\n    N=358801(145+4s)/[90000(567+622s)].\n\nMultiplication using s^2=2 gives\n\n    kappa_3-1=(-8324118905+8781249146s)\n                     /(2305581607500+1614558620000s)>1/2000.\n\nThe denominator is positive. Two thousand times its numerator minus\nits denominator equals 15947939672000s-18953819417500; using s>7/5\ngives the positive lower bound 3373296123300.\n\nThe actual capacities are A_1=lambda A and A_2=lambda^2 A. The seven-\nstep endpoint and the new fifteen-step endpoint satisfy\n\n    N_1=kN, kappa_7=A_1^2 N_1/R^2=sigma^2 kappa_3^2,\n    N_2=nscale N_1, kappa_15=A_2^2 N_2/R^2\n                   =[Hout/(r_1^2 N)] kappa_7^2.     (24)\n\nSince sigma kappa_3>(9999/10000)(2001/2000)>1+1/3000,\nkappa_7>1+1/1500. Put tau=<w_1,t>/N_1=chi D/N and\nnu_res=||t||^2/N_1=chi^2 R^2/N. Bounds (11) give\ntau<3/9999<1/3000, so\n\n    Hout/(r_1^2 N)=1-2tau/r_1+nu_res/r_1^2>1-1/7500.\n\nTherefore kappa_15>(1-1/7500)(1+1/1500)^2>1+1/1000.\nFor the last comparison it suffices to use\n1/750-1/7500-1/(750*7500)>1/1000.\n\nThe actual next boundary includes MORE than these three scalars. One global function, its minimizers, and exact normalization\n\nDefine ynew_i=Xnew_i-Gnew_i and\nDeltanew_i=Fnew_i-||Gnew_i||^2/2. (47)\n\nTo check this directly, for any fixed v and sufficiently small\nepsilon>0, the maximum of affine terms in Hnew(epsilon v) retains\nonly the zero-energy slopes. Capacity squared\nhas not replaced B_31. Why copied values fail and what the new boundary actually costs\n\nThe extra values are essential for this concrete join when chi>0.\nIf L_i in (28) were zero, the actual queried row with source 15 and\nreceiver 23 would be\n\n    Q_(15,23)=chi n k rho(Lcoef_2-z)>0. No seed, global no-seed result, or new fact.", "evidence": "# Exact value-flow prices and two complete redundancy results\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof draft. This is max2's first analytical round\nafter completed baseline22, under TASK.md and guidance f7608aa358751108.\nNo numerical evaluation, solver, parameter scan, replay, machine algebra,\nformal verification, installation, or subordinate agent was used.\n\nAccepted inputs: 04219554e41d9750, Section10 (the ORIGINAL primitive\ncertificate), and 1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraphs1--2 (all-pair smooth\nconvex interpolation and its actual conjugate construction). Everything\nelse needed below is proved in this document. Earlier C82/C83 and coupling\ndrafts are comparison targets, never accepted premises.\n\nThe required concrete outcome is exact redundancy of value pricing on\nthe ENTIRE unit-curvature repaired-cut family, including all its legal\nrepair amplitudes, arbitrary nonnegative words, zero steps, and all\noriginal coordinates. The lifted-cut geometry in2948d4cd3660413f also has\nidentically zero price. A separate C82 calculation gives an exact price\nand a fully minimized formula in a specified parameter domain. It proves\nredundancy there when a^2+a<=1; its complementary regime is not asserted\nto resolve a surviving full-cone region.\n\nThe original asymptotic target, existence/nonexistence of a threshold\nseed, and general four-step cone reversal remain OPEN. No new fact is\nasserted and no theorem below is promoted past the verifier gate.\n\n## 1. Original orientation, objective, and formal coordinates\n\nLet n>=0, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a finite nonnegative word, s=sum h_i,\nC=1+s, and I={0,...,n}. Keep every original gradient coordinate, including\nthe terminal coordinate and every coordinate at a zero step. Fixed\nphysical vectors x_i,g_i in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space obey\n\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.\n\nFor sampled values f_i put\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V(f)=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n          +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_n||^2.       (1)\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 proves Q_ij<=0 for every ordered pair of actual\nsmooth convex samples, and constructs a single interpolant from these\ninequalities. Assume initially that all these finite inequalities hold.\nAn optimum sample will be retained explicitly when it is part of the\nfixed geometry; Section3 also handles the case where its location is free.\n\nFor the original nonnegative multipliers Lambda_ij, put Lambda_ii=0 and\n\n    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,\n    L^T e=omega.                                         (2)\n\nHere e is the (n+1)-entry all-ones column. Thus omega_i is outgoing minus\nincoming flow. The TERMINAL is the source, of mass2s, and index i<n is\na sink of mass2h_i. Reversing these arrows gives the wrong value problem.\n\nWith T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i, let\n\n    D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<n, -s(s+2)),\n    M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 gives, on every physical realization,\n\n    V(f)+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.   (3)\n\nThe full certificate requires M PSD on ALL of R^(n+1). Nothing below\ndeletes a coordinate, restricts Lambda support in that feasibility\nquestion, or identifies formal gradients at zero steps. Sparse flows\nconstructed below solve an auxiliary transport minimum; they are NOT\nclaimed to have PSD matrices.\n\n## 2. An exact finite price, with elementary proof and attained flow\n\nFix legal base values f_i and put w_ij=-Q_ij>=0 for distinct i,j. Regard\nw_ij as the cost of the directed arc i->j. Let d_i be the least cost of\na path from n to i, with d_n=0. The complete finite graph makes every\ndistance finite. A minimizing path exists: a repeated-vertex segment\nhas nonnegative cost and can be deleted, so it is enough to consider\nfinitely many simple paths.\n\nFor replacement values f_i+z_i, the complete constraints are exactly\n\n    z_i-z_j<=w_ij.                                      (4)\n\nSumming along an n->i path gives z_n-z_i<=its cost. Therefore\n\n    z_i-z_n>=-d_i,\n    V(f+z)>=V(f)-Pflow,\n    Pflow=2sum_(i<n)h_i d_i.                             (5)\n\nAll h_i are nonnegative, including possibly zero. The simultaneous\nchoice z_i=-d_i attains every lower bound in(5): the distance inequality\nd_j<=d_i+w_ij is precisely its feasibility in(4). Thus this is an\nattained minimum of the value problem, not only a lower bound from a\ndual program.\n\nThe same number is the exact minimum slack cost of the ORIGINAL flow:\n\n    min_{Lambda>=0, L^T e=omega} sum Lambda_ij w_ij\n       =Pflow.                                         (6)\n\nTo prove the lower bound, multiply w_ij>=d_j-d_i by Lambda_ij and sum.\nThe result is\n\n    sum Lambda_ij w_ij >= -sum_i omega_i d_i\n                         =2sum_(i<n)h_i d_i.\n\nFor attainment, choose a shortest n->i path for every i<n with h_i>0\nand send mass2h_i along that path. Sum these path flows. Intermediate\nvertices have equal incoming and outgoing mass; the endpoints give\nexactly(2). Each path contributes2h_i d_i. Zero-weight sinks need no\npath, but their vertices and all their available arcs remain present.\n\nCombining(3) with(6) gives a necessary condition for any full certificate:\n\n    V(f)>=Pflow.                                        (7)\n\nThe selected optimal values give the same condition as V(f-d)>=0. Indeed\ntheir new slacks are w'_ij=w_ij+d_i-d_j, and every prescribed-flow array\nsatisfies\n\n    sum Lambda_ij w'_ij=sum Lambda_ij w_ij-Pflow.\n\nThe Gram matrix and the formal certificate matrix do not change with\nthe values. The full PSD restriction can impose more than the minimum\nover flows in(6); it has not been dropped from the original problem.\n\n### Zero costs, coincident gradients, and fixed optimum constraints\n\nNo strict positivity of costs is needed. A zero-cost directed cycle\nhas every arc of cost zero, and(4) then forces equal shifts around that\ncycle. Distances and the path-flow construction remain valid without\nchoosing a unique shortest path.\n\nIf two base gradients coincide, Q_ij+Q_ji=0, so legality forces BOTH\nresiduals to vanish. Hence replacement shifts at these two samples are\nequal, even when their positions differ. If two base positions coincide,\nthe sum of the two residuals is ||g_i-g_j||^2; legality first forces\ncoincident gradients and then coincident values. These are properties of\nactual jets, not extra formal equations in the PSD certificate.\n\nOne may also include a fixed optimum jet x_star=g_star=f_star=0 as an\nextra graph vertex. Let d_i^* be distances in this larger graph. Then\nz_i=d_star^*-d_i^* is feasible, has z_star=0, and minimizes the same\nvalue objective subject to ALL finite and optimum pairs. Its improvement\nis2sum h_i d_i^*. In general d_i^*<=d_i, so this price need not equal\nthe original finite-flow price. They WILL agree in every concrete family\nbelow. The distinction is important: optimum edges are not original\nprimitive multipliers and cannot silently be used as such.\n\n## 3. The optimized finite values give one static function and real minima\n\nThis section also explains why(5), without a fixed optimum vertex, is\nnot merely a formal jet result. For any legal replacement values f'_i\non the finite samples, set\n\n    y_i=x_i-g_i, beta_i=||g_i||^2/2-f'_i,\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in I){<y_i,v>+beta_i},\n    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.                              (8)\n\nThis is the construction in accepted1252b8823bb5a290, written out here\nto identify its minimizers. H is finite, continuous, 1-strongly convex,\nand has quadratic growth. The supremum has one maximizer v(x). Strong\nsubgradient inequalities give\n\n    <x-x',v(x)-v(x')>>=||v(x)-v(x')||^2,\n\nand the maximizing inequalities give\n\n    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<v(x),x'-x>\n      <=<v(x')-v(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.\n\nThus F is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with\ngradient v(x). The all-pair inequalities say that at v=g_i the i-th\nquadratic in H is active and has gradient x_i. Consequently v(x_i)=g_i\nand F(x_i)=f'_i. This is ONE fixed F used for all steps.\n\nLet beta_max=max_i beta_i, and A={i:beta_i=beta_max}. The exact minimizer\nset of F is\n\n    argmin F=conv{y_i:i in A},   min F=-beta_max.          (9)\n\nIndeed gradient F(x)=0 is equivalent to x being a subgradient of H at0.\nThe subgradients there are exactly the convex hull of the active affine\nslopes y_i. To check the reverse inclusion without an unproved formula,\nif x is outside that convex hull, a separating vector u has\n<x,u> greater than every <y_i,u> for i in A. For sufficiently small\npositive t, inactive affine pieces remain inactive at tu, and\nH(tu)-H(0)=t max_(i in A)<y_i,u>+t^2||u||^2/2,\ncontradicting the subgradient inequality for x. The forward inclusion\nfollows by convexly combining the active supporting inequalities.\n\nIn particular a real minimizer always exists, even if none was fixed in\nthe graph. The true initial distance is exactly\n\n    dist(x_0,conv{y_i:i in A}).                           (10)\n\nNo arbitrary radius or changed function is inserted into the GD run.\nThe specific families below either preserve the optimum0 or identify\nthe closest minimizer explicitly.\n\n## 4. Entire unit-curvature repaired-cut family: price exactly zero\n\nThis is the main complete redundancy result. It works for any n>=1,\nany nonnegative word, and any cut j with0<=j<n and h_j>1. Put\n\n    P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t,\n    eta=(h_j-1)/(1+T_j)>0,\n    p_0=1, p_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) for0<=i<=j.\n\nChoose an amplitude epsilon>=0. Define two scalar coordinate arrays:\n\n    xB_i=1+sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t, gB_i=1        (i<=j),\n    xA_i=gA_i=p_i                            (i<=j),\n\n    R_k=sum_(t=k)^(n-1)h_t                    (k>j),\n    xB_k=-eta*(1+R_k), gB_k=-eta              (k>j),\n    xA_k=p_j*xB_k, gA_k=-eta*p_j              (k>j).\n\nEmpty sums have value zero. At the cut,\nxB_(j+1)=1-h_j=-eta*(1+T_j), and the same equality multiplied by p_j\nholds for coordinate A. Before the cut the A coordinate follows unit\nquadratic GD. After it both coordinates have constant gradients. Hence\nthe vectors\n\n    X_i=(xB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*xA_i),\n    G_i=(gB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*gA_i),\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2                            (11)\n\nobey every original GD equation. Include X_star=G_star=F_star=0.\n\nHere are ALL interpolation pairs. For saturated values(11),\n\n    Q_ik=<G_i-G_k,X_k-G_k>.\n\nFor a prefix target k<=j, its normal is\n\n    X_k-G_k=(sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t,0).\n\nEvery prefix source has residual zero. Every tail source has residual\n-(1+eta)sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t<=0. The optimum source has residual\n-sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t<=0.\n\nFor a tail target k>j, its normal is\n\n    X_k-G_k=-eta R_k*(1,sqrt(epsilon)*p_j).\n\nEvery tail source has residual zero. A prefix source i<=j has residual\n\n    -eta R_k*[1+eta+epsilon*p_j*(p_i+eta*p_j)].           (12)\n\nThe optimum source has residual\n-eta^2 R_k*(1+epsilon*p_j^2)<=0. Every pair into the optimum is zero.\nThese cases exhaust all directed finite and optimum pairs.\n\nIf T_j>0, legality is EXACTLY the finite set of inequalities\n\n    1+eta+epsilon*p_j*(p_i+eta*p_j)>=0   for i<=j.        (13)\n\nThe necessity follows by using target j+1, where R_(j+1)=T_j>0.\nIf T_j=0, every tail normal vanishes and no condition(13) is necessary.\nZero p_j, zero epsilon, zero intermediate steps and repeated gradients\ncause no exception in these formulas. This specifies the full legal\nrepair-amplitude family, rather than only its most-used endpoint.\n\nFor EVERY legal epsilon, the finite zero-cost arcs include\n\n    n->j,\n    j->i for every i<j,\n    n->k for every j<k<n,\n    i->n for every i<n.                                 (14)\n\nThe first and last groups hold because X_j=G_j and X_n=G_n. The second\ngroup holds because both sources and targets are prefix samples. The\nthird holds because all tail gradients coincide. Thus every original\nsample is in the zero-cost component of n, so\n\n    d_i=d_i^*=0 for EVERY i,\n    Pflow=0.                                           (15)\n\nEquivalently, all feasible value replacements have z_i=z_n. With the\nfixed zero optimum, Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=0 also forces z_n=0. Thus ALL\nsampled values in(11) are forced, not merely optimal for this objective.\n\nAn explicit attaining ORIGINAL flow sends2sum_(i<=j)h_i from n to j,\nsends2h_i from j to i for i<j, and sends2h_k directly from n to k for\nj<k<n. Its other entries vanish. The balance is(2), and every used arc\nis in(14). It has zero cost. This does not assert its matrix is PSD.\n\nThere is one actual static smooth convex witness for every legal\namplitude: use(8) with the zero optimum included. For its prefix samples,\nbeta_i=-sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t; for its tail samples,\nbeta_k=-eta^2 R_k*(1+epsilon*p_j^2). These constants are nonpositive,\nand equality in either formula implies that the corresponding normal\nis zero. The optimum sample also has constant and normal zero. Thus(9)\ngives the UNIQUE minimizer0 for this single conjugate interpolant. Its\nTRUE squared initial distance is(1+P_j)^2+epsilon, in every case above.\n\nThe projection construction used in the earlier drafts is also valid:\nlet K=conv({0} union {G_i}) and define\n\n    F(X)=max_(G in K){<X,G>-||G||^2/2}.                  (16)\n\nThe complete normal list proves that the optimizer at X_i is G_i.\nProjection onto K is1-Lipschitz: add the two projection normal\ninequalities to obtain ||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>. The same maximizing\ninequalities used in Section3 prove differentiability. Since0 is in K,\nF>=0=F(0). This is a real minimizer, and(11) are its actual values.\n\nThe saturated-value telescoping identity is\n\n    V=||X_0||^2-||X_n+sG_n||^2.\n\nTo check it, use2h_i F_i+h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2\n=||X_i||^2-||X_(i+1)||^2, sum, and substitute F_n.\nHere X_n=G_n, so the family gives exactly\n\n    V=(1+P_j)^2+epsilon\n         -C^2 eta^2*(1+epsilon*p_j^2).                  (17)\n\nReoptimizing values adds NO inequality to nonnegativity of(17), for\nany legal epsilon, word, cut or zero-step pattern. Consequently it adds\nnone to an existing collection of necessary conditions that already\nincludes this ENTIRE family. This is a family-level exact redundancy\nclaim, not global redundancy for arbitrary queried geometries.\n\n### The actual four-step C83 member and true radius\n\nTake h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), a,b,c,d>0, j=2, and\n\n    C=5+a+b+c+d, Q=3+a+b, eta=c/(2+d), p_2=ab.\n\nThe only possibly negative coefficient in(13) is i=1, where p_1=-a.\nIf b eta<1, the full amplitude interval is\n\n    0<=epsilon<=(1+eta)/(a^2 b(1-b eta)).                (18)\n\nThe other two coefficients have positive p_i. The endpoint in(18)\nis exactly the repaired-cut value used in d3abee1b17da52da; there is\nno new function-value freedom at ANY amplitude in this interval.\n\nFor0<ab<1 and epsilon>0, the two prefix gradients\n(1,sqrt(epsilon)) and(1,-a sqrt(epsilon)) have G_2 in the relative\ninterior of their segment. Since G_3=-eta G_2, zero is in the interior\nof their nondegenerate triangle with G_3. Thus the minimizer of(16) is\nuniquely0: a nonzero normal at0 would have positive pairing with a\npoint of an open ball in K. The TRUE squared distance is Q^2+epsilon.\nAt epsilon=0 the instance is scalar and0 is again the closest minimizer\nto the displayed initial point. No benchmark ratio is inferred from V.\n\n## 5. The lifted-cut coupling geometry also has zero value price\n\nThis section reconstructs the actual geometry used in draft\n2948d4cd3660413f, independently of that draft's quantitative matrix gap.\nLet a,b,c,d>0, put u=1+a, C=5+a+b+c+d, H=3+c+d, eta=b/H,\nand assume a eta<1. Define\n\n    nu=(1+eta)(1-a eta)/a>0,\n    U=(1,0), Z=-eta U+sqrt(nu)*(0,1),\n    X=(U+uZ, U, -bU, -(2+d)eta U, -eta U),\n    G=(Z, U, -eta U, -eta U, -eta U),\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (19)\n\nThe original four updates follow from b=H eta. Include the zero optimum.\nThe normals at targets1 and4 vanish. At target0 the normal is U+aZ;\nthe source1 residual is zero, and each source2,3,4 residual is -a nu.\nThe optimum-source residual there is -(1-a eta)<0. At target2 the\nnormal is -(b-eta)U: source0 has residual zero, source1 has residual\n-(1+eta)(b-eta), and tail sources have residual zero. At target3 the\nnormal is -(1+d)eta U: source0 has residual zero, source1 has residual\n-(1+eta)(1+d)eta, and tail sources have residual zero. Optimum-source\nresiduals at2,3 are respectively -eta(b-eta) and-(1+d)eta^2.\nEvery residual into the optimum is zero. This checks every pair.\n\nIn particular the zero-cost finite paths are\n\n    4->1->0, 4->2, 4->3, and i->4 for every i<4.        (20)\n\nAll value differences are therefore forced, and the optimum pairs fix\ntheir common shift. Both finite-flow and fixed-optimum prices vanish.\nThe function(16) with K=conv{0,Z,U,-eta U} realizes these samples.\nIts minimizer set is{(0,t):t<=0}; the initial second coordinate is\nu sqrt(nu)>0, so0 is the closest minimizer. Its true squared initial\ndistance is ||U+uZ||^2, not an independent calibration variable.\n\nThe energy is exactly\n\n    Erow=||U+uZ||^2-C^2 eta^2\n        =a+3+1/a-[(1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a]eta-C^2 eta^2.     (21)\n\nValue-flow pricing returns only Erow>=0. It cannot recover a strictly\npositive lower bound on Erow on this SAME geometry: the auxiliary flow\nminimum is attained at zero and the values have no remaining freedom.\nThe further full-matrix restrictions in2948d4cd3660413f use PSD coupling\nwith a different quadratic direction and the first row. This result\nneither assumes that unverified coupling theorem nor refutes it; it\nidentifies exactly what the present price can and cannot add to it.\n\n## 6. C82 triangle: exact nonzero price and a real interpolant\n\nHere keep canonical h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) with a,b,c,d>0, and let\n\n    C=5+a+b+c+d, D=2+d, eta=bc/D,\n    ell=max{0,c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2},\n    t=a v-1, Htri(v)=(v+1)(a v-1).\n\nUse parameters v>=b and z>=0 with ell<=z^2<=Htri(v). For nondegenerate\ntriangles take z>0; the boundary z=0 is legal by the same normal checks.\nThese inequalities imply t>=0. Define\n\n    X_0=((1+a)v-1,z), G_0=(v,0),\n    X_1=G_1=(-1,z),\n    X_2=(b,-bz),     G_2=(b,0),\n    X_3=(-bc,-bz),   G_3=(-eta,0),\n    X_4=(-eta,-bz),  G_4=(-eta,0),\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (22)\n\nEvery GD equation has the original four step lengths. For completeness,\nK=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3} contains0. At target0 the residual normal is(t,z).\nIts comparisons with G_1,G_2,G_3 and0 are, respectively,\n\n    z^2-(v+1)t, (b-v)t, -(eta+v)t, -v t,\n\nall nonpositive. At target1 the normal vanishes. At target2 the normal\nis(0,-bz), so all horizontal-gradient sources give zero and source1\ngives -bz^2. At target3 the normal is(-(1+d)eta,-bz). Its source1\nresidual is b*[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0; its horizontal-gradient\nsources and optimum source are nonpositive. At target4 the normal is\n(0,-bz), again giving zero for horizontal-gradient sources and -bz^2\nfor source1. Every residual into the optimum is zero. Thus all finite\nand optimum pairs hold, and(16) gives one actual base function.\n\nThe finite zero-cost arcs4->1,4->2,4->3 show d_1=d_2=d_3=d_4=0.\nThe least cost into0 is\n\n    Dprice=min{(v-b)t, (v+1)t-z^2}.                      (23)\n\nThe other incoming costs, (v+eta)t and v t from the optimum, are no\nsmaller than(v-b)t. Every path into0 has a final incoming arc, while\neach minimizing source1 or2 is reached at cost zero. Therefore\n\n    d_0=d_0^*=Dprice, all other d_i=d_i^*=0,\n    Pflow=2(1+a)Dprice.                                 (24)\n\nThe complete fixed-geometry value optimum is attained by\n\n    F'_0=F_0-Dprice, F'_i=F_i for1<=i<=4, F'_star=0.      (25)\n\nAll incoming residuals at0 increase by Dprice and stay nonpositive\nby(23); all outgoing residuals from0 decrease. All other pairs are\nunchanged. This is a direct check of all thirty ordered pairs including\nthe optimum, independently confirming the path calculation.\n\nFor the actual function use(8) with these values and the optimum.\nIts affine constants beta_i are\n\n    beta_0=-v t+Dprice<=-b t,\n    beta_1=beta_2=beta_4=beta_star=0,\n    beta_3=-(1+d)eta^2<0.\n\nWhen t>0, the active normals in(9) are0 and(0,-bz). Therefore its\nminimizer set is the segment joining these two points, and0 is the\nclosest minimizer to X_0, whose second coordinate z is nonnegative.\nIf t=0, z=0 and the initial normal also vanishes, with the same\nclosest minimizer0. Thus the true squared initial distance is\n\n    ((1+a)v-1)^2+z^2.\n\nThe base telescoping energy and the exact optimized energy are\n\n    Ebase(v,z)=((1+a)v-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2 eta^2,\n    Eprice(v,z)=Ebase(v,z)-2(1+a)Dprice.                 (26)\n\nIn particular a generic claim that saturated projection values are\nalways rigid is FALSE. Formula(23) gives a strictly positive price\nwhenever v>b, t>0, and z^2<(v+1)t. This is a statement about a fixed\nactual geometry, not yet about improvement over an optimized family.\n\n## 7. Full C82 minimum for0<a<1,0<b<=sqrt(3),ab<=1\n\nAssume0<a<1,0<b<=sqrt(3),ab<=1, and keep c,d>0 arbitrary and ell as\nabove. This domain includes the compact-domain inequalities used in the\nearlier threshold drafts, when those inequalities are taken as explicit\nhypotheses; no unverified global reduction is being assumed here. Put\n\n    R=[1-a+sqrt((1+a)^2+4a ell)]/(2a),\n    v_cross=[1+ell/(b+1)]/a,\n    v_stationary=a/(1-a),\n    v_best=min{v_cross,max{R,v_stationary}},\n    K=C^2 eta^2.                                       (27)\n\nThen R>=1/a>=b and R<=v_cross. The first assertion follows because\nHtri has its nonnegative zero at1/a and is increasing thereafter. For\nthe second, Htri(R)=ell implies(b+1)(aR-1)<=ell.\n\nThe exact infimum of(26) over EVERY legal triangle parameter is\n\n    Enew=(1-a^2)v_best^2-2(1+a)a v_best+1+2(1+a)\n                +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.                 (28)\n\nIf ell>0 the minimizing triangle has z=sqrt(ell)>0 and v=v_best.\nIf ell=0 the same formula is attained by the displayed degenerate\nlegal data; alternatively it is the limit of nondegenerate triangles.\n\nHere is the complete minimization. For fixed v>=R, the price switches\nat z^2=(b+1)t, which is no larger than Htri(v) because v>=b. Below\nthis value its coefficient of z^2 is1-b^2; above it the coefficient\nis1-b^2+2(1+a)>=2a>0. If b<=1, z^2=ell is a minimizer. If b>1, the\nminimizer is z^2=max{ell,(b+1)t}. Thus for R<=v<=v_cross BOTH cases\nuse z^2=ell, the second term in(23) is the minimum, and(26) becomes\n\n    J_left(v)=(1-a^2)v^2-2(1+a)a v+1+2(1+a)\n                         +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.         (29)\n\nFor v>=v_cross and b<=1 it becomes\n\n    J_right(v)=(1-a^2)v^2+2(1+a)ab v+1-2(1+a)b\n                              +(1-b^2)ell-K.           (30)\n\nJ_right has strictly positive derivative on that half-line. If b>1,\nthe corresponding expression for v>=v_cross is instead\n\n    J_kink(v)=(1-a^2)v^2\n        +a[2(1+a)b+(1-b^2)(b+1)]v\n        +1-2(1+a)b-(1-b^2)(b+1)-K.                    (30a)\n\nIts derivative is positive for v>=1/a. To see this, divide that\nderivative by a and use v>=1/a and ab<=1:\n\n    J_kink'(v)/a\n      >=2/a^2-2+2(1+a)b-(b^2-1)(b+1)\n      >=2(1+a)b-(b^2-1)(b-1)\n      >=2(1+a)b-2(b-1)=2(ab+1)>0.\n\nThe last inequality uses b^2<=3 and b>1. Each right expression agrees\nwith J_left at v_cross. Hence in either b regime no minimum occurs\nstrictly after v_cross. J_left is a strictly convex quadratic with\nstationary point a/(1-a). Clipping that point to[R,v_cross] proves\n(27)--(28), including both endpoint cases.\n\nThe original unpriced C82 minimum in this domain is\n\n    Eold=((1+a)R-1)^2+(1-b^2)ell-K,                    (31)\n\nFor b<=1 its energy increases in v and is minimized at z^2=ell. For\nb>1 its minimizing z^2 at fixed v is Htri(v). The resulting quadratic\nhas leading coefficient(1+a)^2+(1-b^2)a>=1+a^2>0 and derivative at\nv=1/a equal to(1+a)[2/a+1-b^2]>0, since a<1 and b^2<=3. Its derivative\ntherefore stays positive for v>=R>=1/a, and its minimum is at R too.\nAt v=R, Htri(R)=ell makes Dprice=0, and J_left(R)=Eold.\n\nConsequently value pricing adds EXACTLY NO information to this fully\noptimized C82 condition whenever\n\n    R>=a/(1-a).                                        (32)\n\nIn particular(32) holds throughout a^2+a<=1, because R>=1/a.\nThe positive root of a^2+a=1 is(sqrt(5)-1)/2. This includes the\nfirst-step projection range39/100<=a<=41/100 from d3abee1b17da52da,\nwith its b range79/100<=b<=81/100. The C83 price was already zero there\nby Section4, independently of this comparison.\n\nWhen ell>0 and R<a/(1-a), one has v_cross>R and v_best>R, so the\noptimized energy is strictly smaller. The precise positive reduction is\n\n    Eold-Enew=(1-a^2)*[(R-v_stationary)^2\n                                 -(v_best-v_stationary)^2]>0. (33)\n\nEquivalently this strict-improvement regime is\n\n    a^2+a>1,\n    0<ell<(a^2+a-1)/(1-a)^2.                            (34)\n\nThis proves a strict improvement of the C82 FAMILY VALUE in its stated\ndomain. It does NOT prove that a word satisfying all the other current\nnecessary conditions is rejected by(28). In particular(34) alone is\nnot a surviving full-cone region and must not be advertised as one.\n\n## 8. Comparison, resources, and the precise remaining interface\n\n* C83: Section4 completely resolves the proposed value-price mechanism\n  for all legal amplitudes of all unit-curvature repaired cuts. The\n  optimum/source flow has an explicit zero-cost realization; the full\n  value system is rigid. This includes the actual unit-curvature j=2\n  separator in d3abee1b17da52da. Curvatures strictly between0 and1 are\n  NOT covered by this rigidity theorem.\n\n* C82: Sections6--7 solve the fixed geometry, construct its optimized\n  actual function, and eliminate both geometric parameters in the\n  explicitly stated domain. A positive price at a nonoptimal triangle\n  cannot be called a new word obstruction when(32) holds.\n\n* 554ec17f7f590bf0 and d3abee1b17da52da: their endpoint core is not\n  being declared empty. These findings contain further triangle,\n  clipping and endpoint conditions; no claim of their complete\n  redundancy is made. On the specified projected prism, the tested\n  C82/C83 value re-selections add no new condition beyond those\n  already present.\n\n* 2948d4cd3660413f: Section5 gives an exact zero-price result for its\n  actual lifted-cut geometry, with every pair reconstructed. A\n  transport minimum cannot replace that draft's simultaneous full\n  PSD/first-row analysis. Its quantitative gap remains a separate\n  unverified joint result.\n\n* 04219554e41d9750: its ORIGINAL twenty-sign, exact-flow, full R5 PSD\n  interface remains the relevant sufficient seed condition. Every\n  implication here uses precisely that identity. No sparse flow\n  constructed for pricing is promoted to a feasible certificate.\n\nTwo concrete analytical directions remain for the next authorized\nround: decide whether the explicit C82 improvement(28) excludes a\nregion surviving the OTHER full necessary conditions; or apply the\nexact price to a genuinely non-unit-curvature geometry, where zero\npaths need not pin the values. Either needs an actual static function\nand a comparison on the same word. Neither has been decided here.\n\nRetrieval: Matlas returned unrelated results to the complete-statement\nvalue/transport query. {"id": "25f0424231f49cc6", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T10:53:25.937926+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C93 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT: on the ORIGINAL H_m for every m>=128, the entire static centered-Huber-ridge-plus-PSD-quadratic class whose actual samples stay in one fixed region has EXACT true-distance supremum K_n(H_m)=1/(1+2 sum H_m), with original B-ratio tending to Z1/(2e)<4/5. Any number of nonorthogonal subunit ridges and noncommuting quadratic coupling are retained. New resolvent bound P_Hm(t)^2<=(1+2S_m t)^-1 controls every curvature. Full-curvature ridges additionally decouple, with sharp subunit coupling prices. A genuine static two-sign-change/noncommuting-gate escape refutes deleting the region premise, but is explicitly dominated by an accepted scalar multi-cut witness on the SAME word. No fact or target resolution.", "evidence": "# C93: curvature costs and an exact single-region obstruction for static ridge sums\n\nStatus: **UNVERIFIED JOINT PROOF DRAFT**. Current runtime round is 1 of\nthe new allocation, completed baseline 23 and completed ceiling 25. This\nround would bring the completed count to 24 after the runtime returns.\nNo numerical/model evaluation, sweep, solver, replay, installation,\nsubordinate agent, or verifier call was used. The original problem is open.\n\nAccepted mathematical predecessors used here are `1252b8823bb5a290`\n(necessity of every original smooth-convex interpolation inequality),\n`b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact scalar Huber/single-cut lower bound and the\noriginal benchmark), `1fca3e5dd555cb4a` (the explicit alternating-ray\nconstruction), and `04219554e41d9750` (the original dyadic benchmark limit).\nAll new assertions are proved together below. No shared unverified result\nis a mathematical predecessor.\n\nThe shared ruler audit `bf591e52671f6d33` suggested the activated-product\nquantity used in Section 5. Its needed estimates are rederived here, and\nthe new conclusion is a resolvent inequality, rather than its quadratic\nobjective bound. The accepted finite C59 fact `e9a55f57461f253c` and max's\nshared actual31 construction `7ffb4d0516ace32f` were read for scope. Neither\nis inserted as a child, used as an asymptotic premise, or replayed.\n\n## 1. Family, definitions, and resolved scope\n\nAll vector spaces are finite-dimensional real Euclidean spaces. Matrices\nare symmetric unless otherwise specified, and matrix inequalities refer\nto positive semidefinite order. The identity matrix is denoted I.\n\nFor positive radii r_minus,r_plus, define the scalar asymmetric Huber\nfunction phi by phi(0)=0 and\n\n    phi'(s)=max(-r_minus,min(s,r_plus)).                 (1)\n\nThus phi(s)=s^2/2 inside [-r_minus,r_plus],\nphi(s)=r_plus*s-r_plus^2/2 for s>=r_plus, and\nphi(s)=-r_minus*s-r_minus^2/2 for s<=-r_minus.\nBoth joins include their boundaries, where the values and derivatives agree.\n\nConsider ONE STATIC function\n\n    F(x)=x^T A0 x/2 + sum_(j=1)^r alpha_j phi_j(u_j^T x), (2)\n\nwhere r is any nonnegative integer, A0>=0, every u_j is a unit vector,\nalpha_j>0, and every phi_j has its own positive radii. Assume\n\n    H0=A0+sum_j alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.                 (3)\n\nThe number of ridges, dimension, matrices, directions, and both radii may\ndepend arbitrarily on the horizon. The ridges need not be orthogonal.\nThe quadratic part need not commute with any ridge matrix.\n\nFor a fixed finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), set\n\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, K=1/(1+2S),\n    P_h(t)=product_(i<n)(1-h_i*t),\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F(x_i), 0<=i<n.                (4)\n\nThe normalized payoff is\n\n    V=2F(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2,                     (5)\n\nand initial points in the minimizer set are omitted.\n\nA **fixed-region run** means that, for EACH ridge separately, all samples\nx_0,...,x_n, INCLUDING the actual unqueried terminal sample, belong to one\nof that ridge's three closed intervals: its core, its positive outer ray,\nor its negative outer ray. Different ridges may choose different regions.\nAt a shared boundary either compatible region may be chosen. No condition\nis imposed on signs or correlations of the full gradients.\n\nThe main quantitative conclusion of this joint draft is:\n\n* If P_h(t)^2 <= 1/(1+2S*t) for every t in [0,1], every fixed-region run\n  of (2) has V<=K. This includes all true normalization costs.\n* On the ORIGINAL diagnostic H_m defined in Section 5, for every m>=128,\n  that polynomial inequality holds, and the supremum of V over the entire\n  fixed-region class is EXACTLY K_n(H_m)=1/(1+2 sum H_m).\n* Its exact original-benchmark ratio tends to Z_1/(2e)<4/5. Here\n  Z_1=lim_(m->infinity)(1+sqrt(2))^(-m) B_(2^m-1), as in accepted\n  `04219554e41d9750`, and e is the exponential of 1.\n\nThis closes the full fixed-region subclass with arbitrary quadratic\ncoupling and any number of subunit-curvature ridges. It does NOT close\ntrajectories that change a ridge region, including a change only at the\nterminal sample. Section 8 gives a genuinely coupled static example\nwith two physical sign changes and actual noncommuting region visits.\nIt refutes dropping the fixed-region premise even on a word satisfying\nthe same polynomial inequality. Its payoff is explicitly compared with\nthe stronger already accepted scalar construction and is NOT presented\nas new lower-bound strength.\n\n## 2. Global legality, minimizers, and every original pair\n\nEach phi_j is differentiable, convex, nonnegative, and zero only at zero.\nFor any x,z, monotonicity and the Lipschitz bound in (1) provide numbers\nd_j in [0,alpha_j] such that\n\n    grad F(x)-grad F(z)\n      =[A0+sum_j d_j u_j u_j^T](x-z).\n\nIf u_j^T(x-z)=0 its contribution is zero and d_j may be taken as zero.\nThe displayed matrix lies between zero and H0, and hence between zero\nand I. Its operator norm is at most 1. This proves global 1-smoothness;\nconvexity also follows directly from the sum in (2).\n\nConversely, all radii are positive, so a ball about zero lies in every\ncore. In that ball F(x)=x^T H0 x/2. Thus global 1-smoothness forces (3).\nCondition (3) is exact for this centered model, not merely a sufficient\ntrace bound.\n\nThe minimizer set is precisely the subspace\n\n    N=ker(A0) intersect intersection_j u_j^perp.        (6)\n\nIndeed every summand of (2) is nonnegative, and their simultaneous\nvanishing is exactly (6). Let z_0 be the orthogonal projection of x_0\nonto N and replace every x_i by x_i-z_0. The objective, gradient, ridge\ncoordinates, GD recursion, and fixed-region condition are unchanged,\nbecause all gradients lie in N^perp. The initial squared norm after\nthis replacement equals the TRUE squared distance in (5). We therefore\nwork below with x_0 in N^perp and denominator ||x_0||^2.\n\nThe pair convention is the original one:\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    f_i=F(x_i), g_i=grad F(x_i), x_star=g_star=f_star=0.\n\nEvery Q_ij is nonpositive, including both terminal directions and both\noptimum directions. To spell out the accepted necessity argument from\n`1252b8823bb5a290`, fix i and define\nJ(z)=F(z)-<g_i,z>. Convexity and grad J(x_i)=0 make x_i a global\nminimizer of J. Smoothness gives\nJ(x_j-grad J(x_j))<=J(x_j)-||grad J(x_j)||^2/2.\nThe minimum J(x_i) is at most this value. Rearranging gives Q_ij<=0.\nThis one argument checks every ordered pair of the actual trajectory;\nno subset of remote, zero-step, optimum, or terminal rows is discarded.\n\n## 3. A sharp curvature price, including shifted ridge cores\n\nThis section treats a broader geometry than (2): allow an additional\nlinear term and shifts in every ridge argument, and let each scalar\nconvex ridge have a nonempty open interval where its second derivative\nis the constant alpha_j>0. Assume the whole sum is globally 1-smooth\nand its quadratic Hessian A0 is positive semidefinite.\n\nAt a point in one ridge core, second differences in an arbitrary\ndirection z, using nonnegativity of the second differences of all\nother convex terms, give\n\n    A0+alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.                         (7)\n\nFor two NONPARALLEL directions u,v, their shifted open core slabs\nintersect. In fact the map x -> (u^T x,v^T x) is surjective onto R^2:\non span(u,v) its matrix has determinant 1-<u,v>^2>0. Thus any desired\npair of interior core arguments can be achieved simultaneously.\nAt such a point the same second-difference argument gives\n\n    A0+alpha u u^T+beta v v^T <= I.                    (8)\n\nThese arguments require no differentiability of other summands at that\npoint. For sufficiently small real t the two selected core terms have\nsecond differences exactly t^2 alpha<u,z>^2 and t^2 beta<v,z>^2,\nwhile 1-smoothness bounds the total symmetric second difference by\nt^2||z||^2.\n\nWriting rho=<u,v>, the largest eigenvalue of\nalpha uu^T+beta vv^T on their span is\n\n    [alpha+beta+sqrt((alpha-beta)^2+4alpha beta rho^2)]/2.\n\nConsequently (8) implies alpha,beta<=1 and the sharp necessary bound\n\n    alpha beta rho^2 <= (1-alpha)(1-beta).             (9)\n\nTo obtain (9), square the nonnegative inequality\nsqrt((alpha-beta)^2+4alpha beta rho^2)<=2-alpha-beta\nand cancel. With A0=0 it is also sufficient for this pair's matrix to\nbe bounded by I, so this numerical curvature price is sharp.\n\nThere is a sharp bound on coupling to A0 as well. For one ridge let\ns=1-alpha and write A0 relative to u and u^perp as\n\n    A0 = [ a  b^T ; b  C ].\n\nIf b is nonzero, put v=b/||b|| and c=v^T C v. Positivity of A0 and\nof I-A0-alpha uu^T, restricted to span(u,v), gives\n\n    ||b||^2<=a c,  ||b||^2<=(s-a)(1-c),\n    0<=a<=s, 0<=c<=1.\n\nIf a<=s(1-c), the first bound is at most s c(1-c). If\na>=s(1-c), the second bound is at most that same quantity. Hence\n\n    ||(I-uu^T)A0 u|| <= sqrt(1-alpha)/2.              (10)\n\nThe case b=0 already satisfies this. Equality is attained in dimension\ntwo by u=e_1 and\n\n    A_s=(1/2)[s,sqrt(s);sqrt(s),1], alpha=1-s,\n    0<s<1.                                          (11)\n\nBoth A_s and I-A_s-alpha e_1e_1^T are rank-one positive semidefinite\nmatrices. This is an ACTUAL available coupling, not a forbidden one.\n\nIn particular a ridge of full individual curvature alpha=1 satisfies\nA0 u=0: (7) gives u^T A0 u=0, and positive semidefiniteness implies\nA0 u=0. Any two nonparallel full-curvature ridge directions are\northogonal by (9). Parallel shifted ridge terms must be grouped into\none scalar function; they need not have overlapping cores.\n\nIt follows that a sum consisting entirely of full-curvature convex\nridges plus a PSD quadratic, with any shifts and any linear term,\nseparates orthogonally into scalar ridge groups and an independent\nquadratic on their orthogonal complement. If its minimum is attained,\nits minimizer set is the Cartesian product of the scalar minimizer\nsets and the quadratic minimizer affine subspace. Squared distance to\nthat product is the sum of squared component distances. GD separates\nin the same coordinates. For every h the true-distance payoff is\ntherefore a weighted average of the actual scalar-group payoffs and\nquadratic eigenmode payoffs\n\n    t * product_(i<n)(1-h_i*t)^2, 0<=t<=1.             (12)\n\nA component starting in its minimizer set stays there and contributes\nzero to both numerator and denominator. This proves that rotating or\nadding such full-curvature ridges cannot yield a genuinely coupled\nmultidimensional improvement over the best component on the SAME word.\nIt does not bound arbitrary scalar multi-return profiles by K_n(h).\n\n## 4. Exact terminal algebra and the fixed-region bound\n\nReturn to the centered model (2). For a fixed-region run absorb all\nalways-core ridges into the quadratic matrix and write\n\n    A=A0+sum_(j in Core) alpha_j u_j u_j^T,\n    b=sum_(j in Outer) epsilon_j q_j u_j,\n    D0=sum_(j in Outer) q_j^2/alpha_j,                 (13)\n\nwhere epsilon_j is +1 or -1 for its chosen outer ray, and\nq_j=alpha_j times its radius on that ray. Every actual sample satisfies\n\n    f_i=x_i^T A x_i/2+b^T x_i-D0/2,  g_i=A x_i+b.    (14)\n\nCondition (3) becomes\n\n    A+sum_(j in Outer) alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.         (15)\n\nLet L=I-A. For every vector z, scalar Cauchy--Schwarz and (15) give\n\n    (b^T z)^2 <= D0 * z^T L z.                       (16)\n\nConsequently b is orthogonal to ker L. Define L^dagger by reciprocating\nits positive eigenvalues and assigning zero to its zero eigenvalues.\nPutting z=L^dagger b in (16), or treating b=0 separately, proves\n\n    D0 >= b^T L^dagger b.                            (17)\n\nThus the intercept cost required by global smoothness is retained even\nwhen many nonorthogonal ridge forces add in the same direction.\n\nDefine polynomials (with their continuous values at t=0)\n\n    R_h(t)=(1-P_h(t))/t, R_h(0)=S,\n    T_h(t)=(1-P_h(t)^2)/t, T_h(0)=2S.\n\nUnrolling g_i=A x_i+b yields\n\n    x_n=P_h(A)x_0-R_h(A)b.                           (18)\n\nThis identity is also proved inductively using\nR_(prefix+h)(t)=(1-h*t)R_prefix(t)+h. Substituting (18) into (14),\nand using I-A R_h(A)=P_h(A), gives the EXACT identity\n\n    2f_n=x_0^T A P_h(A)^2 x_0\n           +2b^T P_h(A)^2 x_0-D0-b^T T_h(A)b.        (19)\n\nNo radius, value, forcing term, or terminal quantity has been removed.\n\nAssume now the polynomial condition\n\n    P_h(t)^2 <= 1/(1+2S*t), 0<=t<=1.                 (20)\n\nDiagonalize A orthogonally. For one eigenvalue t in (0,1), abbreviate\np=P_h(t)^2 and\n\n    d=1/(1-t)+(1-p)/t.\n\nBy (17), its contribution to the right side of (19) is bounded by\n\n    t p X^2+2p X B-d B^2,                            (21)\n\nwhere X,B are the coordinates of x_0,b on this eigenvector. We claim\n(21)<=K X^2. First d>0 by (20), and\nt p<=t/(1+2S*t)<=K. The determinant needed to complete the square is\n\n    (K-t p)d-p^2\n       =[K-p(K+t(1-K))]/[t(1-t)] >=0,               (22)\n\nbecause K/[K+t(1-K)]=1/(1+2S*t). Thus the two-variable quadratic\nK X^2-(21) is positive semidefinite.\n\nAt t=0, p=1, and its contribution is at most\n2XB-(1+2S)B^2<=K X^2 by completing a square. At t=1, (16) gives\nB=0, and the contribution is P_h(1)^2 X^2<=K X^2 by (20).\nSumming all eigenvector inequalities proves 2f_n<=K||x_0||^2.\nSection 2 identified that norm with the true closest-minimizer distance.\nThis proves the first quantitative claim in Section 1, including zero\neigenvalues, unit eigenvalues, empty outer sets, and zero physical steps.\n\nEquality is attained by the actual scalar global Huber witness for ANY\nnonnegative word: take A0=0, one unit ridge of curvature 1, both radii\nq=1/(1+2S), and initial point x_0=1. All iterates are\nx_i=1-q sum_(t<i)h_t >=(1+S)/(1+2S)>=q. The fixed positive outer\nregion condition includes the terminal point. Direct substitution gives\n2F(x_n)=1/(1+2S), the initial true distance is 1, and the minimizer is\nunique. Hence whenever (20) holds the restricted supremum is exactly K.\n\n## 5. The resolvent inequality on the ORIGINAL H_m, with all curvatures\n\nFix an integer m>=128, and keep the following enclosing-horizon\nparameters fixed in EVERY child word:\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), c=sigma(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    d_j=1+a*c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nThe actual physical word is h=H_m, of length n=2^m-1. Write\nS_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j, r_c=c-2, and A_mass=a/r_c. Counting the\noccurrences of each bridge gives\n\n    M_j=A_mass*c^j+(1-A_mass)*2^j,\n    P_j(t)=product_(l=0)^(j-1)(1-d_l*t)^(2^(j-l-1)).   (23)\n\nHere P_j is the polynomial of the complete physical word H_j; P_0=1.\n\nThe following bounds follow from squaring the rational comparisons\n7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7 and using m>=128:\n\n    12/5<c<49/20, 3/8<a<sigma-2<c-2<9/20,\n    127/128<=ac<1, 0<A_mass<1, c*r_c<5/4.            (24)\n\nFor the upper c bound, c<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<49/20.\nFor the lower a bound, a>(127/128)/(49/20)>3/8. The upper bound on\na follows from a=(m-1)/[(m+1)sigma]<1/sigma=sigma-2.\nIn particular (23) gives M_j<=c^j and\n\n    t*S_j >= a*c^j*t/r_c-t.                          (25)\n\nWe prove (20) for this exact h. At t=0 it is equality. Fix t in (0,1].\nIf every d_l*t<1 for 0<=l<m, all factors are nonnegative and\n1-z<=exp(-z) gives\n\n    P_m(t)^2 <= exp(-2S_m*t) <= 1/(1+2S_m*t).        (26)\n\nIf a factor is zero the required conclusion is immediate. Otherwise\nlet j be the first level with d_j*t>=1. Set\n\n    v_l=a*c^l*t, Y_l=c*v_l*|P_l(t)|.\n\nIf j=0 then Y_0=ac*t<=1. If j>=1, all factors of P_j are on their\nnonnegative side, and t<1/d_0<8/11. From (25),\n\n    Y_j<=c*v_j*exp(-v_j/r_c+t)\n         <=c*r_c*exp(t-1)<1.                         (27)\n\nThe middle bound maximizes v exp(-v/r_c) over v>=0, with maximum\nr_c/e. The last inequality uses c*r_c<5/4 and\nexp(3/11)>1+3/11=14/11>5/4.\n\nFor every l>=j, the exact physical recursion in (23) gives\n\n    Y_(l+1)=[1-(1-t)/v_l]*Y_l^2 <=Y_l^2<=1.          (28)\n\nThe bracket is between zero and one, since d_l*t>=1 and t<=1.\nConsequently, with z=a*c^m*t,\n\n    P_m(t)^2 <= 1/(c^2*z^2).                         (29)\n\nActivation implies t>=1/d_(m-1). Since m>=3, (24) implies\n\n    z>=c*[a*c^(m-1)]/[1+a*c^(m-1)]\n       >(12/5)*(54/25)/(1+54/25)=648/395>3/2.        (30)\n\nAlso S_m<c^m by (23). Therefore\n\n    (1+2S_m*t)P_m(t)^2\n       <=[1+(16/3)z]/[(144/25)z^2]\n       <=25/36<1.                                  (31)\n\nFor the final inequality the function z^(-2)+(16/3)z^(-1) decreases\nfor z>0, and its value at z=3/2 gives 25/36 after multiplication by\n25/144. Equations (26) and (31) cover EVERY t in [0,1]. In particular\nthere was no curvature grid, schedule replay, or fixed-curvature premise.\n\n## 6. Same-word scalar comparison and the ORIGINAL benchmark\n\nWe also rederive that the best accepted Huber/single-cut value on this\nword is the global Huber value, so the comparison is on the SAME word.\nFor a cut at level j its squared numerator is (a*c^j)^2. Holding the\ntotal S_m and bridge d_j fixed, the denominator is\n\n    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_m-d_j-P)],\n\nwhere P is the actual preceding mass. Its logarithm has strictly\nnegative second derivative in the interior of the allowed mass range.\nThus its minimum over the interval between the first and last occurrence\nis at an endpoint. The palindrome H_m makes the following mass at its\nfirst occurrence at least S_(m-1), and its preceding mass is S_j. At\nthe last occurrence the preceding mass is at least S_(m-1), and the\nfollowing mass is S_j. Put M=M_(m-1).\n\nFrom (23), a*c^j<=r_c*M_j, M_j<=M, and M_m<=c*M. The first-occurrence\ncut is at most r_c^2/(2M-1). The last-occurrence cut is at most\n\n    r_c^2*M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)] <= r_c^2/(2M-1),\n\nbecause x^2/(2x-1) is nondecreasing for x>=1. Finally c<5/2,\nr_c<1/2, and M>=1 give\n\n    r_c^2(2M_m-1) <=(5M-1)/4 <=2M-1.\n\nEvery cut in accepted `b98bb807d514e80d` is therefore no larger than\n1/(2M_m-1). Its global Huber term attains that value. Hence\n\n    sup_fixed-region V = K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).         (32)\n\nFor the limit, divide (23), at j=m, by sigma^m. Since a/(c-2)->1,\n(1+1/m)^m->e, and (2/sigma)^m->0, one gets\n\n    M_m/sigma^m -> e.\n\nThe accepted ray theorem `04219554e41d9750` supplies\nB_(2^m-1)/sigma^m -> Z_1. Thus the exact restricted ratio has limit\n\n    B_(2^m-1)*sup_fixed-region V -> Z_1/(2e).         (33)\n\nFor an explicit strict gap, its accepted upper enclosure at b=1,j=0\ngives Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2. The accepted original recurrence gives\nB_1=4. Therefore Z_1<=41209/10000. The positive exponential series\ngives e>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3, whence\n\n    Z_1/(2e)<123627/160000<4/5.                      (34)\n\nNo fit, substituted recurrence, internal capacity, or scalar radius\nof a higher-dimensional witness is used in this comparison.\n\n## 7. The coupled fixed-region class is nonempty on every word\n\nHere is a fully specified genuinely coupled instance satisfying its\nregion hypothesis for ANY finite nonnegative h. It is included to rule\nout a merely vacuous obstruction. Work in R^2 and put\n\n    u=e_1, w=(e_1+e_2)/sqrt(2),\n    eta=1/[8(1+S)], A0=eta*w*w^T,\n    alpha=1/2, q=eta, r_minus=r_plus=q/alpha,\n    F(x)=x^T A0 x/2+alpha*phi(u^T x), x_0=e_1.       (35)\n\nThe matrices A0 and uu^T do not commute. Also\nA0+alpha uu^T <=(eta+alpha)I<=5I/8<I. The minimizer is unique zero,\nsince the two distinct directions w and u span R^2.\n\nTo check the actual gates without numerical evaluation, first run the\nexplicit affine recursion z_(i+1)=(I-h_i A0)z_i-h_i q u, z_0=e_1.\nEvery factor I-h_i A0 has norm at most one, because\n0<=h_i eta<=S/[8(1+S)]<1. Consequently\n||z_i||<=1+q sum_(t<i)h_t<=9/8. Its displacement from e_1 satisfies\n\n    ||z_i-e_1||<=sum_(t<i)h_t(eta*9/8+q)<17/64.\n\nThus u^T z_i>47/64, whereas r_plus=1/[4(1+S)]<=1/4.\nEvery sample including z_n is strictly in the positive outer region.\nIt follows inductively that z_i IS the actual GD run of the single\nfunction (35). Section 2 supplies all original pairs and true radius 1.\nThe payoff need not equal (32); Section 4 shows that on H_m it cannot\nexceed (32). This is not a scalar copy hidden behind a coordinate change:\nits actual outer Hessian A0 and central Hessian A0+alpha uu^T do not\ncommute, although this particular run only visits the outer region.\n\n## 8. A static multiple-sign escape, and its honest payoff comparison\n\nThe next example attacks the fragile extension that the polynomial\ncondition alone might bound ALL trajectories of (2) by the global Huber\nvalue. It preserves that condition and releases the fixed-region premise.\nIt also shows that the curvature rigidity at alpha=1 is not valid for\narbitrarily small positive slack. It is a scope example, not a new\ncompetitive lower bound on H_m or on arbitrary words.\n\nTake the fixed physical word\n\n    h=(1,4,1,4,1), S=11, K=1/23.                    (36)\n\nIts polynomial is P_h(t)=(1-t)^3(1-4t)^2. For 0<=t<=1/4, all factors\nare nonnegative, so P_h(t)^2<=exp(-22t)<=1/(1+22t). For t>=1/4 put\nz=1-t in [0,3/4]. Then P_h(t)=z^3(3-4z)^2. Its derivative as a\nfunction of z is z^2(3-4z)(9-20z), and the maximum is\n\n    (9/20)^3*(6/5)^2=6561/50000<1/7.\n\nIt follows that (1+22t)P_h(t)^2<=23/49<1 on this second interval.\nThus (20) holds on ALL of [0,1] for the same word (36).\n\nLet phi have radii r_minus=3/4 and r_plus=1/2. Fix ANY\n0<epsilon<=1/1000000 before the run, put s=epsilon^2, and define\n\n    F_epsilon(x,y)=(epsilon*x+y)^2/4+(1-s)*phi(x),\n    (x_0,y_0)=(5/4,0).                              (37)\n\nIts quadratic is A_s from (11), its ridge direction is e_1, and its\nridge curvature is 1-s. Thus it is globally convex and 1-smooth. Its\nunique minimizer is (0,0), giving true initial squared distance 25/16.\nIts outer and central Hessians are\n\n    A_s=(1/2)[s,epsilon;epsilon,1],\n    C_s=A_s+(1-s)e_1e_1^T.\n\nThey do not commute for epsilon>0. Both are PSD and bounded by I, since\nI-C_s=(1/2)(epsilon,-1)(epsilon,-1)^T.\n\nAt epsilon=0, the exact first-coordinate trajectory on (36) is\n\n    5/4, 3/4, -5/4, -1/2, 3/2, 1,                  (38)\n\nand the second coordinate stays zero. The corresponding first gradient\ncoordinates are\n\n    1/2, 1/2, -3/4, -1/2, 1/2, 1/2.\n\nIts ridge regions are positive outer, positive outer, negative outer,\ncore, positive outer, positive outer. Every listed point has distance\nat least 1/4 from the boundary of its specified open region.\n\nHere is a uniform TEXTUAL perturbation bound establishing those SAME\nactual region visits for every epsilon in the specified interval.\nThe matrix change A_s-A_0 has norm at most epsilon, and |phi'|<=3/4.\nAt every unperturbed sample, whose norm is at most 3/2, this gives\n\n    ||grad F_epsilon(z_i^0)-grad F_0(z_i^0)||<=3epsilon.\n\nLet E_i=||z_i^epsilon-z_i^0|| for the actual GD runs, and put\nD_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t). Global 1-smoothness gives\nE_(i+1)<=(1+h_i)E_i+3epsilon*h_i, and hence\n\n    E_i<=3epsilon(D_i-1)<=597epsilon<1/1000.          (39)\n\nThe product at n=5 is exactly 2*5*2*5*2=200. Equation (39) is a\nfive-step analytic perturbation estimate, not a numerical replay.\nIt preserves every strict gate in (38). In particular the actual run\nvisits A_s,C_s,A_s at queried indices 2,3,4. It has TWO actual sign\nchanges in both the first position coordinate and first gradient\ncoordinate, with gradient signs +,+,-,-,+,+.\n\nThe correlations are not constant, and the gradients are not collinear.\nFor the first two actual gradients, which use the positive outer region,\ndirect substitution gives\n\n    g_0=(1/2+s/8,5epsilon/8),\n    g_1=((1-s)(1/2+s/16),epsilon(1-s)/16),\n    det(g_0,g_1)=-epsilon(1-s)(9+s)/32 !=0.           (40)\n\nTheir inner product is positive. The error of any actual gradient from\nits unperturbed counterpart is at most E_i+3epsilon<=600epsilon.\nThus <g_1,g_2><0: its unperturbed value is -3/8, while the absolute\nchange is at most (5/4)(600epsilon)+(600epsilon)^2<1/100.\nThis proves distinct normalized correlations as well as noncollinearity.\n\nAll original ordered Q rows, including the actual terminal rows, follow\nfrom Section 2 applied to (37). No Hessian is chosen online.\n\nThe payoff comparison must keep the true radius. At z_5^0=(1,0),\n\n    F_epsilon(1,0)=3/8-s/8,\n    grad F_epsilon(1,0)=(1/2,epsilon/2).\n\nThis gradient has norm less than one. Convexity, smoothness and (39)\ntherefore give |F_epsilon(z_5^epsilon)-3/8|<1/100. Since the true\nsquared radius is 25/16,\n\n    9/20 < V_epsilon=(32/25)F_epsilon(z_5^epsilon)<1/2. (41)\n\nFor example the stronger bounds from 3/8 plus or minus 1/100 are\n2336/5000<V_epsilon<2464/5000, which imply (41).\nThis strictly exceeds the global Huber value 1/23 despite (20), so\ndropping the fixed-region premise of Section 4 is refuted.\n\nOn this SAME word the exact accepted single-cut maximum is\n\n    K_5(h)=max(1/23,9/52,3/49)=9/52<9/20.            (42)\n\nNevertheless the stronger accepted scalar construction\n`1fca3e5dd555cb4a` already gives 73/48>1/2. Its two checkpoints are\n4 and 4, with all three ordinary masses equal to 1. The fact's exact\nparameters are m_0=1/2,m_1=3/4,O_1=1/2,z_term=7/4,e_term=1/4,\nand q_opt=7/12. Feasibility is z_term>=2m_0=1, and its payoff is\n\n    2e_term+z_term^2/3=1/2+49/48=73/48.              (43)\n\nThus (37) is an actual nonlinear, noncommuting, multiple-sign scope\nexample with a checked quantitative gap over the single-cut calibration.\nIt is DOMINATED by an already accepted scalar witness and is not new\nlower-bound strength. The word (36) is not H_m and supplies neither the\narbitrary-h nor the non-dyadic quantifiers of the project goal.\n\n## 9. Interface left for the next authorized round\n\nThe fixed-region mechanism on the original H_m is exactly Huber-tight,\neven with an arbitrary number of centered ridges, arbitrary dimension,\nsubunit ridge curvatures, and noncommuting quadratic coupling. The\nfull-curvature relay is additionally forced to split into scalar groups.\nThese are separate scoped exclusions; neither excludes all static ridge\nsums.\n\nThe explicit escape (37) shows the remaining mechanism is real: a\nsubunit ridge can visit its core between opposite outer visits, while\nthe background quadratic generates changing correlations. What remains\nmissing is a fixed static gated construction on ORIGINAL H_m with a\nterminal value exceeding its accepted scalar baseline, with all region\nvisits and true distance controlled uniformly. The matrices and affine\nforcing are fixed by (37), not freely selectable at each bridge. There\nis no authorized numerical search or verifier call for that step.\n\nFor max's actual31 state, this result supplies a price only if a proposed\nrealization is specifically a centered Huber-ridge sum of (2). No claim\nis made that its conjugate interpolant has that representation. Its\npositive endpoint state, changed values, residuals, and minimizer hull\ncannot be inserted into (19) without proving the representation and\nthe fixed-region hypothesis. C59 likewise is not claimed to belong to\nthis family, and its fixed H6 parameters were never replaced by those\nof another enclosing horizon.\n\nLiterature context only: Aravkin, Burke, and Pillonetto, arXiv:1301.4566v2,\nRemark 4 (Huber scalar example) and Remark 5 (sum representation), were\nread from the exact PDF. {"id": "f2c5786ba58ac55c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T10:53:51.544050+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4 round29 COMPLETE JOINT DRAFT: exact full-PSD/all20-sign completion along an original four-edge terminal circulation.
